\paragraph{The factorised $2\to4$ head's data check on catalog\_v3 (2026-10-25).} Daniel's catalog asked it of $40$
probes with an added gluon or photon (par.\ Design (ii) on the data); catalog\_v3 asks it of every $2\to4$ class built
as a $2\to3$ class plus one gluon, photon, $Z$ or $H$: $880$ classes ($312$, $298$, $164$, $106$), $303$ of them held
out, none skipped (\code{tools/factorised\_2to4.py --v3}, CPU; \code{analysis/catalog\_v3/factorised\_2to4.py}). The
method is the one of Daniel's catalog with three changes: for a $Z$ or $H$ the map's beam fraction is $x=K^2/\hat s$;
on $b$ beams, where a rescaled beam would leave its mass shell, the other finals are kept and the beams absorb the
recoil, each on its mass shell, back to back in $K$'s rest frame (\code{beam\_recoil\_map}; momentum conserved to
$10^{-13}$ and masses exact on constructed events; $5$ of the $532{,}000$ $b$-beam events below its threshold are
dropped and counted), so the $266$ $b$-beam classes are summarised apart; and the learned correction is fitted in $10$
folds grouped by parent (a class's siblings on the same parent never in its own fit), $400$ events per class, in place
of leave-one-probe-out. Every parent's backend reproduces its own pool exactly where it has one; $78$ parents never
generated were compiled for the check. Medians over the classes of the variance of $\log|\mathcal M|^2$, the class's
mean removed:
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabular}{lrccc}
\hline
added particle, beams & classes & its own & after the parent & and learned \\
\hline
gluon, light & $205$ & $10.9$ & $3.57$ & $0.49$ \\
gluon, $b$ & $107$ & $10.7$ & $3.72$ & $0.52$ \\
photon, light & $210$ & $10.7$ & $4.29$ & $1.15$ \\
photon, $b$ & $88$ & $10.6$ & $4.31$ & $1.10$ \\
$Z$, light & $116$ & $5.05$ & $1.18$ & $0.87$ \\
$Z$, $b$ & $48$ & $4.31$ & $0.87$ & $0.50$ \\
$H$, light & $83$ & $8.88$ & $1.48$ & $1.06$ \\
$H$, $b$ & $23$ & $6.32$ & $0.98$ & $0.65$ \\
\hline
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The parent carries a massive emission as well as a massless one: after it a $Z$ or $H$ leaves about $1$, and the learned
correction, which removes most of what a gluon or photon leaves, adds little there. The $b$-beam classes behave as
the light ones. Against the network: on catalog\_v3, unlike Daniel's catalog, the short dimensionless run already
reads a held-out $2\to4$ class's shape zero-shot about as well as the exact parent with the learned correction. On the
$124$ held-out $2\to4$ families among these classes (H2), its shape is $0.47$ / $1.15$ / $0.81$ / $0.73$ (gluon /
photon / $Z$ / $H$, median) against the factorised $0.55$ / $1.23$ / $0.90$ / $1.34$, the factorised form better on
$22$ of $42$, $14$ of $44$, $9$ of $23$ and $6$ of $15$. On the $144$ top classes (H3) the factorised form is ahead,
$0.39$ / $0.89$ / $0.29$ / $0.39$ against the network's $0.57$ / $1.08$ / $0.61$ / $1.08$, better on $34$ of $45$,
$32$ of $43$, $31$ of $35$ and $19$ of $21$. The information budgets differ as on Daniel's catalog: the factorised
numbers use the parent's exact $|\mathcal M|^2$ and a correction fitted on the true $2\to4$ labels of other parents'
classes, the network's are zero-shot. So where catalog\_v3 trains on the multiplicity, the head would add little; where
it might is at a multiplicity not trained on, the $40$ $2\to5$ probes (each a $2\to4$ class plus one particle, $38$
with a gluon, $2$ with a photon, all on $e^+e^-$ beams). Onto their $2\to4$ parents, the correction fitted on all $880$
$2\to4$ classes and applied unchanged: own spread $16.3$, after the exact parent $3.45$, and learned $0.46$, under the
dimensionless run's zero-shot shape ($1.02$; $0.93$ with the context vector, $2.35$ for the baseline) on $34$ of the
$40$. With the network's own $2\to4$ prediction at the mapped events in place of the exact parent
(\code{tools/factorised\_2to4\_net.py --v3-nfinal 5}, each of the four short runs; the parents are training
classes) the gain is gone for the three dimensionless runs: $1.01$ against $1.02$, $0.93$ against $0.93$ with the
context vector, $1.12$ against $0.98$ for the twin, under on $16$, $18$ and $15$ of the $40$. For the baseline it is
not: its own parent gives $1.00$ against its zero-shot $2.35$, under on $39$ of the $40$. The baseline's $2\to5$ error
is its level (Tab.~\ref{tab:v3_short}), and reading a $2\to5$ process through its $2\to4$ parent carries the level
over, which the dimensionless target already does on its own; both land at about $1$. The network's error on its own training classes at the mapped events adds about $0.5$, as much as the
factorisation removes, for the dimensionless target. That is no failure at the mapped events: every mapped event lies inside its parent's training
$\sqrt{\hat s}$ window (median share $1.00$), and the error there, $0.51$ (median; $10$--$90\%$ $0.20$--$1.34$), equals the
run's own validation error on the same $40$ parents ($0.50$, $0.18$--$1.22$; \code{result.json}'s per-process loss at
the best checkpoint times the shared standardization's $\sigma^2$), worse on $19$ of $40$. It is simply how well a
$4264$-step run knows a $2\to4$ class. So on these short runs a factorised head buys nothing one multiplicity out beyond what the dimensionless target
already gives (it does repair a target whose level does not extrapolate);
it pays only once the parent's error falls well under the $0.46$ the exact parent leaves, which a longer pretraining may
or may not give faster than it improves the $2\to5$ zero-shot ($1.02$, only twice the trained $2\to4$ error).

\begin{figure}[H]
\centering
\widerow{\pnl{../analysis/catalog_v3/figs/factorised_2to4_a.pdf}\hfill\pnl{../analysis/catalog_v3/figs/factorised_2to4_b.pdf}} \\[1ex]
\widerow{\pnl{../analysis/catalog_v3/figs/factorised_2to4_c.pdf}\hfill\pnl{../analysis/catalog_v3/figs/factorised_2to4_d.pdf}}
\caption{catalog\_v3's $2\to4$ classes built as a $2\to3$ class plus one particle: per class (ordered by its own
spread) the variance of $\log|\mathcal M|^2$, after dividing by the parent's exact $|\mathcal M|^2$ at the mapped event,
and after also removing a correction learned on other parents' classes. (a) A gluon added, (b) a photon, (c) a $Z$,
(d) an $H$; light and $b$ beams together.}
\label{fig:v3_factorised}
\end{figure}

\paragraph{The context vector's $K$-shot fits on catalog\_v3 (2026-10-25).} The fit of par.\ The context vector, on
catalog\_v3's short run with the context vector, for every held-out process of every axis it reads ($617$; the user's go,
$\sim27$ GPU-h; \code{scripts/job\_kshot\_context\_v3.sh}, \code{tools/kshot\_context.py} unchanged: $c$ alone fitted on
$K=1$, $4$, $16$ of the process's validation events, $5$ draws, $300$ Adam steps at $0.05$, scored on the others),
against the plain and the shrunk offset on the same draws (\code{analysis/catalog\_v3/kshot\_context.py},
\code{data/kshot\_context.json}, Fig.~\ref{fig:v3_kshot}). Medians over each axis's processes, at $K=16$:
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabular}{lrcccccc}
\hline
held-out axis & processes & zero-shot & floor & plain offset & shrunk offset & context fit & fit better \\
\hline
new combinations & $54$ & $0.75$ & $0.63$ & $0.66$ & $0.67$ & $0.93$ & $9\%$ \\
$2\to4$ families & $168$ & $1.08$ & $0.84$ & $0.89$ & $0.88$ & $1.25$ & $13\%$ \\
the physical top & $237$ & $1.46$ & $0.80$ & $0.85$ & $0.84$ & $0.80$ & $53\%$ \\
one loop & $30$ & $1.34$ & $0.78$ & $0.81$ & $0.81$ & $0.50$ & $63\%$ \\
$\alpha_s=0.13$ & $8$ & $0.16$ & $0.09$ & $0.09$ & $0.10$ & $0.19$ & $13\%$ \\
$p_T\geq30$\,GeV & $40$ & $0.42$ & $0.40$ & $0.44$ & $0.42$ & $0.73$ & $8\%$ \\
$2\to5$ & $40$ & $1.03$ & $0.93$ & $0.97$ & $0.96$ & $1.32$ & $8\%$ \\
$\sqrt{\hat s}\in[1,10]$\,TeV & $40$ & $2.29$ & $1.59$ & $1.69$ & $1.69$ & $2.11$ & $33\%$ \\
\hline
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The floor is the variance of the zero-shot error, what any one number per process reaches; ``fit better'' is the share
of processes where the context fit beats the shrunk offset. Unlike Daniel's catalog, where the fit went under the floor
on the $43$ new ($0.21$ against $0.46$), on catalog\_v3 it is worse than an offset on six of the eight axes, and worse
than not fitting at all on five ($p_T\geq30$: $0.73$ against a zero-shot $0.42$). It goes under the floor only on the
one-loop pools ($0.50$ against $0.78$) and reaches it on the top classes ($0.80$). Where it fails, the context
run's zero-shot error is already mostly shape (its level part $0.01$ to $0.18$, Tab.~\ref{tab:v3_short}), so there is
little a per-process vector can carry, and sixteen free numbers fitted on $K\leq16$ events chase the noise of the draw
(at $K=1$ the fit is above the zero-shot on every axis but one loop and the top). Where it helps, the level part is the largest ($0.31$ one loop, $0.35$ top) and the
processes differ from training in something a per-process vector can encode: the one-loop finite part's normalisation,
the top's mass and width. The fit's settings were kept from Daniel's catalog for comparability; a fit with a
penalty on $|c|$, or with the step count chosen on held-out draws, is the obvious next try, not run.

\begin{figure}[H]
\centering
\includegraphics{../analysis/catalog_v3/figs/kshot_context.pdf}
\caption{catalog\_v3: the MSE of $\log|\mathcal M|^2$ on a held-out process's other validation events after $K$
labelled events, by the plain per-process offset, the shrunk offset and the fit of the context vector $c_p$, against
the floor (the variance of the zero-shot error); medians over processes. (a) The $537$ processes of the six axes
inside the catalog's range; (b) the $80$ beyond it ($2\to5$, $\sqrt{\hat s}\in[1,10]$\,TeV).}
\label{fig:v3_kshot}
\end{figure}

\paragraph{The level's two targets on catalog\_v3 (2026-10-25).} The hand-off's level item set two targets on Daniel's catalog, when its $2\to4$ zero-shot error was $52$:
zero-shot $2\to4$ within $2\times$ its one-event error, and the tops' shape floor under $0.5$
with the heavy-quark layer. Both are read off the short pretrainings (Tab.~\ref{tab:v3_short}) and the context arm's K-shot fits
(par.\ The context vector's $K$-shot fits on catalog\_v3, \code{analysis/catalog\_v3/data/kshot\_context.json}), medians over processes:
\begin{itemize}
  \item \emph{$2\to4$: met, and the target no longer separates anything.} The context arm's zero-shot error on the $168$
    held-out $2\to4$ classes is $1.08$; one labelled event gives $1.43$ with the plain offset and $1.10$ with the shrunk one,
    so zero-shot sits at $0.76\times$ and $0.99\times$ the one-event error. On the $40$ $2\to5$ probes, $1.03$ against $1.58$
    and $1.08$. One event carries more noise than level, because the level is already small ($0.09$ to $0.20$ over the four
    arms on $2\to4$) and what is left is shape ($0.79$ to $0.94$), which no per-process number reaches.
  \item \emph{The tops' shape floor: not met.} With the heavy-quark layer in training ($105$ pools at
    $m_Q\in\{50,300,600\}$\,GeV), the median shape on the $237$ held-out top classes is $0.88$ (baseline), $0.80$
    (dimensionless), $0.80$ (with the context vector) and $0.85$ (the twin), against the target $0.5$. Their floor in the
    K-shot read, the variance of the zero-shot error, is $0.80$.
\end{itemize}
Both targets now point at the same thing: the zero-shot error left on catalog\_v3 is shape, not level.

\paragraph{The shape proxy on catalog\_v3 (2026-10-25).}\label{par:v3_shape_proxy} Since the zero-shot error left is shape, which target leaves
the least of it? Per class, one small regressor (sklearn MLP $(64,64)$, the same for every target) fitted on $7500$
validation events and scored on $2500$ more, with the same inputs for every target (the pair invariants and masses over
$\hat s$, and $\log\hat s$); its MSE on the $2500$ scoring events after the mean is the shape a target costs within a process
(\code{analysis/catalog\_v3/prep\_shape.py}, run FA-ccin2p3-17fcc322; \code{prep\_shape\_read.py},
\code{data/prep\_shape.json}; the port of the proxy on Daniel's catalog). Four targets: $\log|\mathcal M|^2$ (the baseline
arm's), $+(N-4)\log\hat s$ (the $\hat s$ factor alone), $+L_q$ (the emission scales, the dimensionless arms' target) and
$L_q$ with a soft minimum ($\tau=0.3$). $200$ training classes drawn with seed $0$ and every class of the seven held-out
tree axes, none skipped. Medians over classes (Fig.~\ref{fig:v3_prep_shape}a):
\begin{itemize}
  \item \emph{The $\hat s$ factor alone leaves the least shape on every set but $\alpha_s=0.13$, the emission scales the most.} On the $168$
    held-out $2\to4$ classes: $0.062$ for the $\hat s$ factor, $0.077$ for $\log|\mathcal M|^2$, $0.093$ with the soft
    minimum, $0.123$ for the emission scales; per class the $\hat s$ factor is below $\log|\mathcal M|^2$ on $145$ of $168$
    (median ratio $0.84$) and the emission scales above it on $163$ (ratio $1.61$). On the $40$ $2\to5$ probes the gap
    widens: $0.091$, $0.124$, $0.181$, $0.236$ (emission scales above on all $40$). The same order on the training classes
    ($0.052$, $0.059$, $0.071$, $0.086$), the top ($0.023$, $0.027$, $0.030$, $0.040$) and every other axis but
    $\alpha_s=0.13$ ($8$ classes), where all four are below $0.007$ and the order differs: the soft minimum, $0.0034$, is just
    below the $\hat s$ factor, $0.0036$, then the emission scales, $0.0058$, and $\log|\mathcal M|^2$, $0.0067$.
  \item \emph{The order holds at a third of the events} (run FA-ccin2p3-31d221cd, $2500$ to fit,
    \code{data/prep\_shape\_n2500.json}): every MSE about doubles ($2\to4$: $0.141$, $0.163$, $0.192$, $0.243$) and the
    ranking is the one above on every set but $\alpha_s=0.13$, where it is $0.0067$ for the $\hat s$ factor, $0.0087$ with
    the soft minimum, $0.011$ for $\log|\mathcal M|^2$ and $0.014$ for the emission scales.
  \item \emph{The proxy tracks where the network leaves shape.} Across held-out classes, the rank correlation between the
    proxy's MSE and the short pretrainings' zero-shot shape (Tab.~\ref{tab:v3_short}) is $0.68$ on $2\to4$ and $0.80$ on
    the top for the dimensionless arm on its own target, $0.68$ and $0.71$ for the baseline (Fig.~\ref{fig:v3_prep_shape}b).
    In size the network's zero-shot shape is $7$ to $20\times$ the proxy's (ratio of the medians: $0.85$ against $0.12$ on
    $2\to4$, $0.80$ against $0.04$ on the top): a per-class fit on $7500$ events is not what zero-shot gets, so the proxy ranks targets and
    classes, it does not bound the network.
\end{itemize}
The emission scales bought the $2\to4$ level on Daniel's catalog ($52$ to $2.2$, par.\ The emission scales in training) and
cost shape there (par.~\ref{par:qperp_cost}); on catalog\_v3, where the level is already small, that cost is what remains, and the proxy puts it at
$1.6\times$ within a process. The candidate it suggests is the $\hat s$ factor alone with the same dimensionless inputs:
the least shape of the four on every axis but $\alpha_s=0.13$, though it gives up the emission scales' level on
$2\to4$, which only a network can weigh.
\begin{figure}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics{../analysis/catalog_v3/figs/prep_shape.pdf}
\caption{The shape proxy on catalog\_v3. (a) Per set (the $200$ training classes, tr, and the held-out axes H1 new
combinations, H2 $2\to4$, H2.5 $2\to5$, H3 the top, H6 $\alpha_s=0.13$, H7c $p_T\geq30$\,GeV, H7E $1$ to $10$\,TeV), the
median over classes of a fixed small regressor's MSE on the $2500$ scoring events for each target. (b) Per held-out class, that MSE for
the emission-scale target against the dimensionless arm's zero-shot shape on the same class.}
\label{fig:v3_prep_shape}
\end{figure}

\paragraph{The shape proxy's candidate in a network (2026-10-25).} The dimensionless arm with $\hat s$ alone as the
target's reference scale in place of the emission scales (the same dimensionless inputs and coupling orders; arm
\code{dimensionless\_shat} of \code{scripts/job\_catalog\_v3\_short.sh}, one seed, the short setup and HPs of the four
arms, run FA-ccin2p3-fbd73961, under D99), read zero-shot on every axis as in Tab.~\ref{tab:v3_short} (all $617$ probes;
\code{analysis/catalog\_v3/prep\_shat.py}, \code{data/prep\_shat.json}, Fig.~\ref{fig:v3_prep_shat}). Medians over each
axis's processes, MSE (level, shape), against the dimensionless arm's in Tab.~\ref{tab:v3_short}:
\begin{itemize}
  \item \emph{Inside the catalog's range it is the best of the five arms on three of the six axes and second on two.} New combinations
    $0.74$ ($0.14$, $0.53$), $2\to4$ $0.85$ ($0.12$, $0.67$), the top $1.51$ ($0.42$, $0.78$), one loop $0.87$,
    $p_T\geq30$\,GeV $0.34$; against the dimensionless arm's $0.84$, $1.04$, $1.67$, $1.12$, $0.44$. The context vector's
    arm keeps the top ($1.46$), the twin one loop ($0.85$); on $\alpha_s=0.13$ ($8$ probes) it is $0.17$ against $0.14$. Per probe its shape is below
    the dimensionless arm's on $124$ of the $168$ $2\to4$ classes (median ratio $0.80$), $147$ of $237$ tops ($0.90$), $45$
    of $54$ new combinations ($0.81$), and $22$ of $30$ to $33$ of $40$ on the others: the proxy's ranking holds in the
    network, the shape about $10$ to $30\%$ lower.
  \item \emph{It gives up the level at a new multiplicity.} On the $2\to5$ probes the MSE is $14.8$, of it $13.6$ level,
    against $1.21$ ($0.10$) for the emission scales, worse on all $40$ although its shape is lower on $32$; at $1$ to
    $10$\,TeV it is $1.73$ ($0.28$, $1.11$) against $2.88$, so the energy extrapolation the $\hat s$ factor carries holds.
    It is still below the baseline's $46$ on every $2\to5$ probe: $\hat s$ alone sets part of a new multiplicity's level,
    the emission scales the rest.
  \item \emph{Signs.} On the $17$ one-loop pools that change sign, the median wrong-sign fraction is $15\%$, against $18\%$
    for the emission scales and $5\%$ for the baseline.
\end{itemize}
So the two references trade one against the other: the emission scales set the level of a multiplicity never seen and
cost shape everywhere, $\hat s$ alone keeps the shape and loses that level. One seed each.
\begin{figure}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics{../analysis/catalog_v3/figs/prep_shat.pdf}
\caption{catalog\_v3's short pretraining with $\hat s$ alone as the target's reference scale against the dimensionless
arm (the emission scales), per held-out probe: (a) the shape of the zero-shot error, (b) its MSE, on
$\log|\mathcal M|^2$. Triangles: the $2\to5$ and $1$ to $10$\,TeV probes. One seed each.}
\label{fig:v3_prep_shat}
\end{figure}

% allow: ^2 N-4 4a159066
% allow: 10000 because it is the number of training events a held-out probe's statistics read, a setting
% allow: 2000 because it is the number of validation events read per probe, a setting
\paragraph{The hybrid reference scale, a design (2026-10-25).} The two references trade shape against level (the
paragraph above), and one number per process can take both: the target $\log|\mathcal M|^2+(N-4)\log\hat s+c_p$ with
$c_p$ the mean over the process's events of $L_q-(N-4)\log\hat s$, the emission scales' factor less the $\hat s$ one.
Within a process $c_p$ is a constant, so the per-event shape is that of $\hat s$ alone; its class mean is the emission
scales' exactly. The rule for a process never trained on: $c_p$ needs only momenta, no amplitude, so it is computed
from the process's own training events, as for a training class (a held-out probe's first $10000$, the events
\code{tools/zero\_shot\_parent.py} reads for the probe's own statistics; its validation or test events only when it has
no training split), not from the events being predicted. A fine-tune onto a process its parent trained on keeps the
parent's $c_p$, which the frozen data statistics carry (\code{6b387fb}). The two choices of events give nearly the same
constant: on $9$ catalog\_v3 pools of five to seven particles
% src: python3 analysis/catalog_v3/hybrid_check_read.py @11519b9
% claim: n_pools=9
% claim: dcp_min=0.0024
% claim: dcp_max=0.091
% claim: ratio_max=2.1
% claim: serr_min=0.026
% claim: serr_max=0.086
(\code{analysis/catalog\_v3/hybrid\_check.py} on CC's login node; \code{hybrid\_check\_read.py},
\code{data/hybrid\_check.json}), $c_p$ on the first $10000$ training events and on the first $2000$ validation events
differ by $0.0024$ to $0.091$, at most $2.1$ times the validation estimate's sampling error ($0.026$ to $0.086$). The
CPU half of the design, on every catalog\_v3 tree class
(\code{analysis/catalog\_v3/prep\_level.py}, run FA-ccin2p3-4a159066; \code{prep\_level\_read.py},
\code{data/prep\_level.json}):
\begin{itemize}
  \item \emph{$c_p$ is stable under resampling.} On the $2\to5$ probes, where it is largest (median $-15.4$), it moves by

% claim: cp_H2p5_c_p_median=-15.4
    a median $0.045$ between the validation and the test events and by $0.075$ between a tenth of them and all.
  \item \emph{Its level is the emission scales', the best of the three at a new multiplicity.} The notes' composition

    model (a ridge on the counts of each kind of particle in and out, the coupling orders removed; par.\ The candidate
    targets on the pools themselves), fitted on the training classes (the $12$ BSM ones included) and scored on the held-out

    against the emission scales' $0.123$ and $0.236$.
\end{itemize}
% allow: 2 because it is the GPU-hour estimate of the job, a plan
% allow: 9b88e434
What the CPU cannot say is whether the network's level skill with the emission scales comes from the class mean, which
the hybrid keeps, or from the per-event $L_q$, which it drops; only a network answers that. The GPU test: one more arm
of the short pretraining, the same inputs, orders, setup and HPs as the other five, with the hybrid target, read
zero-shot on every axis; one seed, about $2$\,GPU-h. Approved (the user, 2026-10-25: ``Yes, run that hybrid test
then.'') and run on CC with the target option \code{data.target\_physics\_hybrid} (\code{66559de};
FA-ccin2p3-9b88e434, which died on the full disk, rerun as FA-ccin2p3-2f7d86e7); its read is the next paragraph.

% claim: wrong_sign_base=5
% claim: signed_pools=17
    $18\%$ for the emission scales, $15\%$ for $\hat s$ alone and $5\%$ for the baseline.
\end{itemize}
So one number per process takes most of both references' gains: $\hat s$'s shape (lower still inside the range) and
most of the emission scales' level at $2\to5$, at the price of the top's level and the level under harder cuts. One seed
each arm, so differences of a few tens of percent on an axis are within what a second seed could move (not measured).

% claim: n_probes=617
\paragraph{Blending the hybrid with the emission scales (2026-10-26).} The hybrid target plus $\lambda$ times the
per-event emission-scale term less its process mean, $\lambda\,[L_q-(N-4)\log\hat s-c_p]$
(\code{physics\_scales.hybrid\_blend}, \code{data.target\_physics\_hybrid\_blend}): $\lambda=0$ is the hybrid,
$\lambda=1$ the emission scales' target. The added term has zero mean over the process's own training events, so the
class mean stays the emission scales' at every $\lambda$, and $\lambda$ sets only how much of the per-event $L_q$ the
network is asked to carry. Two more arms of the short pretraining, $\lambda=0.25$ and $0.5$, with the same inputs,
orders, setup and HPs as the other arms, one seed each (approved, the user 2026-10-25: ``Yes, run those two
pretrainings blending emission scales''; the two runs and the commit they ran at: \code{FA-ccin2p3-6d35589c},
\code{FA-ccin2p3-0bddbf48}, \code{cdcfaf5}), read
zero-shot on every axis, all $617$ probes (\code{analysis/catalog\_v3/prep\_blend.py}, \code{data/prep\_blend.json},
Fig.~\ref{fig:v3_prep_blend}). Medians over each axis's processes, listed at $\lambda=0$, $0.25$, $0.5$, $1$; the
per-probe ratios are the median over probes of a blend's value over the hybrid's:
\begin{itemize}
  \item \emph{The per-event term costs shape on every axis.} At $\lambda=0.25$ the shape is $1.05$ to $1.26$ of the

    the $40$). None of the blends overtakes the best of the other arms there: the context vector's on $2\to5$ ($1.03$)

    $21\%$, $18\%$.
\end{itemize}
% allow: 0.25 0.5 0 because they are the blend weights lambda, settings
% allow: 0.13 because it is the held-out coupling value, a setting
So the per-event emission-scale term trades shape for level at every weight tried, and the trade nets a gain only
outside the catalog's range, at $\lambda=0.25$ and by tens of percent at most; $\lambda=0.5$ is no better than
$0.25$ on any axis but $2\to5$. On new combinations, $2\to4$, $\alpha_s=0.13$ and one loop the hybrid ($\lambda=0$) is
still the best of the four weights, and on the first three the best of the eight arms. One seed
each arm, so differences of a few tens of percent are within what a second seed could move (not measured).

\paragraph{The signed one-loop pools' data (2026-10-26).}\label{par:v3_prep_signed} The shape proxy left the
sign-changing one-loop pools apart (par.~\ref{par:v3_shape_proxy}); this is their check: how often the stored value is
negative, how much of its sign the event's kinematics decide, and what each target costs on its magnitude. On every
one-loop pool of catalog\_v3's training recipe and of the one-loop held-out axis, on its validation and test events
together, the first $7500$ to fit and the next $2500$ to score (\code{analysis/catalog\_v3/prep\_signed\_pools.py}, run
FA-ccin2p3-7145824e on CC's CPUs; \code{prep\_signed.py}, \code{data/prep\_signed.json}, Fig.~\ref{fig:v3_prep_signed}):
the share of negative events, the wrong-sign share of one sign per pool and of a classifier on the shape proxy's inputs
(Fig.~\ref{fig:v3_prep_shape}; sklearn MLP $(64,64)$; none when the fit events hold fewer than $20$ of one sign), the same classifier on $\log\hat s$
alone, and the shape proxy's regressor on the magnitude $\log\max(\abs x,s)$, $s$ the pool's $1\%$ quantile of $\abs x$
(the sign head's floor). Medians over the sign-changing pools, training then held out:
\begin{itemize}

% claim: base_clip_pct=4.9
% allow: 16 60 5 because they are the validation before the jump, the sign-changing training pools' count and the clipping bound
    Where it blew up (Fig.~\ref{fig:v3_pair_head_blowup}): between the $16$th and the $17$th validation (steps $1360$
    to $1445$) its validation loss went from $0.0208$ to $0.391$, on every pool at once: the median pool's loss rose
    $9.3$ times, the one-loop pools' $79$ times, $90\%$ of the pools more than threefold; later it peaked at about
    $900$ times its best and ended $56$ times above it, with $12$ steps skipped in all. In the same interval the
    median sign accuracy on the $60$ sign-changing training pools fell from $0.9775$ to $0.32$, below one sign per
    pool: the sign logit flipped and stayed flipped. The first non-finite gradient comes after that validation, so the
    skipped steps follow the jump, they do not start it. Over the run the gradient norm before clipping has the median
    $9.09\times10^{6}$ against the baseline's $0.144$, and clipping at $5$ bounds $73.3\%$ of the steps against
    $4.9\%$; the run keeps no per-step norm, so whether the norms grew before step $1360$ is not read. It was already
    less stable before the jump: $1.82$ times the baseline's validation loss at the $16$th validation. The pair term
    feeds the products $h_ih_j$ of two unnormalised output channels into an unbounded logit; that this is the route is
    not checked. A retry bounds the pair inputs and logs the gradient norm per step, and the head's sizes and rate go
    into the search that follows.
\begin{figure}[t]\centering
\includegraphics{../analysis/catalog_v3/figs/pair_head_blowup.pdf}
\caption{The sign arms' short pretrainings on catalog\_v3, one seed each, at the baseline's hyperparameters.
(a) Validation loss without regularisation against step; crosses: the pair sign heads' steps skipped for a non-finite
gradient norm, at the validation interval they fall in. (b) Median sign accuracy on the sign-changing training pools at
each validation.}\label{fig:v3_pair_head_blowup}
\end{figure}
  \item \emph{More sign-changing training pools help the sign, not the rest.} The third arm changes the data, not the

    model: the baseline at its HPs, one seed (run FA-ccin2p3-ecc413f9), trained on the one-loop QCD virtual of every
    coloured $2\to3$ training tree class that catalog\_v3's one-loop layer lacked, $95$ pools of $10^4$ training events
    (\code{recipes/catalog\_v3\_train\_signext.yaml}, $1492$ processes against the baseline's $1397$, the training
    one-loop pools from $137$ to $232$). The new pools are training classes only, so no held-out pool or its class is
    among them. Their sign-changing share was the point, but the arm also has more processes, more one-loop data
    and, at the baseline's $4264$ steps, $4.7$ epochs per pool instead of $5$: a gain cannot be told from more one-loop
    data of any sign, and a cost elsewhere may be the fewer epochs. On the $17$ held-out pools the median wrong-sign
    share is $4.8\%$ against the baseline's $5.4\%$; it beats the baseline on $12$ of the $17$ and loses on $3$, and the
    pools it gets more than half wrong drop from $4$ to $1$. Within a factor $10$ of zero it is $14.2\%$ against
    $39.4\%$, and over the whole one-loop axis ($30$ pools) $7.7\%$ against $13.1\%$; about $3$ points of that are
    $u\bar u\to e^+e^-Z$, all positive in validation, which the baseline calls $90\%$ negative and this arm $0.3\%$.
    $e^+e^-\to c\bar cg$ stays at $71\%$ wrong. The size moves the other way: the zero-shot MSE on
    $\log|\mathcal M|^2$ per held-out probe over the baseline's has the median $1.07$ (above $1$ on $355$ of the $617$
    probes), $0.86$ on the one-loop axis (median MSE $0.84$ against $1.25$) but $1.17$ on $2\to5$ and $1.36$ on the
    $1$ to $10$\,TeV axis; the magnitude's MSE on the $17$ sign-changing pools is $1.42$ against $1.25$. One seed;
    the second seeds follow.

    probes (Fig.~\ref{fig:v3_prep_signed_seeds}). The pair sign head and the near-zero weighting were not run again:
    they stay at one seed, so their comparisons above carry no seed spread.

    The arm's second run blew up: between its $16$th and $17$th validation (steps $1360$ to $1445$, the same
    interval as the pair sign head's, so a blow-up at these hyperparameters is not the pair term's alone; whether the
    coincidence of the step is more than chance is not checked) its validation loss went from $9.18\times10^{-3}$ to $0.381$; $28$ steps were
    skipped for a non-finite gradient norm and it ended at $1.09$. Its reads are at its best checkpoint, step $1360$,
    whose validation loss is $1.6$ times the baseline second seed's $5.88\times10^{-3}$ (the first seeds:
    $4.40\times10^{-3}$ baseline, $4.67\times10^{-3}$ arm; the runs' logs). Why it blew up is not checked.
    The sign: the seed alone moves pools both ways, the baseline's second seed less wrong on $6$ of the $17$ pools and
    more on $9$, the arm's on $4$ and $10$; the arm moves them one way, less wrong than the baseline on $12$ of $17$ at
    seed $42$ and $11$ (more on $4$) at seed $43$, and on $14$ (more on $1$) by the two seeds' mean per pool. Within a
    factor $10$ of zero ($16$ pools have such events) the baseline is at $39.4\%$ and $41.3\%$, the arm at $14.2\%$ and
    $14.6\%$; over the whole one-loop axis $13.1\%$ and $14.8\%$ against $7.7\%$ and $8.0\%$: gaps several times the
    seed spread, and the near-zero one over ten times it. The median wrong-sign share is no stable number: the
    baseline's goes from $5.4\%$ to $19.2\%$ with the seed (the arm's $4.8\%$ and $3.3\%$), so the near-zero and axis
    shares carry the gain, not the median. The size: the seed alone moves the zero-shot MSE per probe by a median
    factor $1.14$ for the baseline (above $1$ on $382$ of the $617$ probes; $1.17$ on $2\to5$, $1.30$ on the $1$ to
    $10$\,TeV axis) and $1.36$ for the arm, whose second run is the blown-up one. The arm over the baseline is $1.07$
    at seed $42$, $1.32$ at seed $43$ and $1.16$ on the two seeds' geometric mean per probe; on $2\to5$ it is $1.17$ and
    $1.86$ (mean $1.67$), on the $1$ to $10$\,TeV axis $1.36$ and $0.63$ (mean $0.99$). The seed-$42$ cost is inside
    the baseline's own seed spread, the energy axis' cost reverses at the other seed, and the larger costs at seed $43$
    come from a run read before its blow-up at a higher loss, so they cannot be told from the blow-up. What stands:
    the added sign-changing pools buy a sign gain well beyond the seed spread; a zero-shot cost is not shown at seed
    $42$ and is confounded with the blow-up at seed $43$.
  \item \emph{The seed-$43$ blow-up does not repeat, and with it goes most of the zero-shot cost.} The more-signed-pools
    arm's seed-$43$ run was run again with its configuration unchanged (the dumped configs differ only in the run's
    name and paths), now writing the gradient norm before clipping, the training loss and the learning rate at every
    step (run FA-ccin2p3-5a8c8fde). The two runs are not the same run: the per-step record is the only code change,
    yet the first validation already differs, $0.247$ against $0.127$ (the runs' logs), so a run at a fixed seed is
    not reproducible on these GPUs, and a blow-up is a property of the run, not of the seed.

% allow: 16 because it is the head's design, its pair channels
% allow: 3 1000 100 5 10 because they are the read's thresholds (the loss's rise, the norm's jump, the loss's blow-up, the clipping bound) and the blow-up rule's factor
% allow: 600 because it is a window edge of the read

    It blew up between its $8$th validation and its $9$th (steps $680$ to $765$, validation loss $0.178$ to $3.58$),
    against the $16$th and $17$th of the unbounded head; its best checkpoint is at step $595$ ($0.0312$), it ended at
    $2.52$, and $33$ steps had a non-finite gradient norm (a blow-up is a validation over $10$ times the running
    minimum, as above). That factor, the lead-up's thresholds below, its five-step median, its reference window
    and the step-$600$ split of the clipping shares are open settings of this read, chosen with no decision behind
    them. The per-step record against the reference window, steps $510$ to $595$ (median loss $0.0627$,
    median gradient norm $0.837$; Fig.~\ref{fig:v3_pair_head_steps}): the loss rises first. From step $646$, at $0.957$
    of the peak learning rate (warmup ends at step $675$), its five-step median stays above $3$ times the reference,
    while the gradient norm's is $3.81$, under the clipping bound of $5$; the norm's stays above that bound from step
    $654$. At step $671$ one gradient norm exceeds $1000$ times the reference while the loss is $22.9$ times its own,
    an increase of four orders of magnitude in the norm within two steps with no matching increase in the loss; the first non-finite norm is at step
    $688$ and the loss exceeds $100$ times the reference from step $691$. After step $600$ clipping bounds $97.8\%$ of
    the steps and does not stop it. Which loss term jumps: the per-step loss sums the sign and the magnitude terms, so
    the record cannot split them; the validations can. At the $8$th (step $680$) the magnitude loss rose by a median
    factor $3.5$ on the $62$ pools with a negative value in some role, $3.53$ on the other one-loop pools and $4.59$ on
    the trees, while the median sign accuracy on the sign-changing training pools fell from $0.965$ to $0.785$: both
    at once, the trees as much as the signed pools, so the failure is the whole network's, not the sign logit's alone.

    Its reads are at step $595$, before the lead-up, a seventh of the way through: on the $17$ held-out pools the median
    wrong-sign share is $24.4\%$ against the baseline's $5.4\%$ and the unbounded head's $11.3\%$, less wrong than the
    baseline on $3$ and more on $11$; within a factor $10$ of zero $37.6\%$ against $39.4\%$ and $32.8\%$, over the
    one-loop axis $16.7\%$ against $13.1\%$ and $13.0\%$; $e^+e^-\to c\bar cg$ $72\%$. The zero-shot MSE on
    $\log|\mathcal M|^2$ per probe over the baseline's has the median $2.28$ (above $1$ on $502$ of the $617$ probes)
    against the unbounded head's $1.77$, and the one-loop axis' median MSE is $2.32$ against $1.25$. These read a model
    stopped early, not the head at its length. So the blow-ups are not the unbounded pair products' alone: a run with
    no pair head blew up in the same interval as the first pair head, its rerun did not, and the bounded head blew up
    near the peak of the learning rate. Whether a lower peak rate or a longer warmup prevents them is not checked; the
    search over the heads' sizes has to search the rate with them, the coupled space (CLAUDE.md, the HPO rules).
\begin{figure}[t]\centering
\includegraphics{../analysis/catalog_v3/figs/pair_head_steps.pdf}
\caption{The per-step record of two short pretrainings on catalog\_v3 at the baseline's hyperparameters, one run each.
(a) Gradient norm before clipping per step: the bounded pair sign head and the more-signed-pools arm's seed-$43$ rerun;
dashed, the clipping bound; dotted, the end of warmup. (b) The bounded head's lead-up: its gradient norm and training
loss per step, each over its median in the reference window (steps $510$ to $595$); vertical lines, the steps the text
names.}\label{fig:v3_pair_head_steps}
\end{figure}
  \item \emph{Searched together with the learning rate under Muon, neither sign knob shows in the training objective nor
    in the training pools' validation signs; the best trial beats the Muon baseline held out, but at a higher rate that
    cannot be told from its pair head.} The question, from the bounded head's read above: does the pair sign head or the near-zero
    weighting help once its own sizes and the learning rate are searched together? The design
    (\code{sweep/sweep\_config\_ccin2p3\_sign\_head\_search.yaml}; open: no decision of the user's is behind the
    design, its ranges, its single seed, its objective or the zero-shot read of its best trial alone): a single-fidelity
    DyHPO search of $20$ trials ($6$ random, then the Gaussian process) at the short pretraining's $4264$ steps, every
    trial the more-signed-pools arm (the baseline target, one shared standardization, $1492$ training pools, seed $42$)
    with Muon at the Muon search's decoupled weight decay $0.0217$ (par.~\ref{par:v3_muon}). Searched: the learning
    rate in $[1.667\times10^{-3}, 1.5\times10^{-2}]$, half a decade either side of the Muon search's best $5\times10^{-3}$;
    the warmup fraction in $[0.05, 0.2]$; the pair head's width in $\{0, 8, 16, 32\}$ ($0$ is no pair head), its hidden
    width in $\{16, 32, 64\}$ and the $\tanh$ bound on or off; the near-zero weight in $\{1, 3, 10, 30\}$ ($1$ is none)
    and its band in $\{3, 10, 30\}$ times from zero. The pair and near-zero values are open settings, chosen with no
    decision of the user's behind them: an octave or half a decade around the first, untuned arms' values. The
    objective is the loss the model trains on, on the training processes' validation events only: per process the
    magnitude MSE plus the sign head's cross-entropy, geometric mean over processes, read at the checkpoint with the
    best magnitude loss. The comparison point is the Muon baseline (run FA-ccin2p3-0a9d3d4c): the same arm with both
    knobs off at learning rate $5\times10^{-3}$ and warmup $0.158$, a rate and weight decay taken from the Muon search
    on the dimensionless target, not searched here, so the fair comparison is inside the search. One seed each
    (\code{analysis/catalog\_v3/sign\_head\_search.py}, \code{data/sign\_head\_search.json},
    Fig.~\ref{fig:v3_sign_head_search}).
% src: python3 analysis/catalog_v3/sign_head_search.py @151979fe

% claim: near_obj=1.397e-03
% claim: near_lr=8.25e-03
% claim: n_both=5
% claim: both_obj=1.517e-03
% claim: both_lr=5.916e-03

% allow: 1 10 because they are the energy axis's range in TeV, the axis's name
% allow: 6 4264 1492 42 0.0217 because they are the search's design (random start-up trials, steps, training pools, seed, Muon's weight decay)
% allow: 1.667 1.5 5 0.05 0.2 because they are the searched ranges and the Muon search's best rate they centre on
% allow: 0 8 32 64 1 3 10 30 because they are the searched pair and near-zero values
% allow: 0.158 because it is the Muon baseline's warmup, its design
% allow: -3 -2 because they are the powers of ten of the rates
    All $20$ trials ended, none failed. Four blew up (a validation over $10$ times its running minimum, as above),
    all at learning rate at least $8.959\times10^{-3}$ and all with an unbounded pair head: of the $7$ trials at or
    above that rate, $5$ had an unbounded pair head and $4$ of those blew up; they stay in every number below, at
    their best checkpoint. Best objective by which knob is on: both off $2.039\times10^{-3}$ (best of $2$ trials, at
    rate $2.337\times10^{-3}$); the pair head alone $1.187\times10^{-3}$ (of $6$; rate $1.246\times10^{-2}$, width
    $16$, hidden $16$, unbounded, warmup $0.081$, magnitude loss $9.673\times10^{-4}$); the near-zero weight alone
    $1.397\times10^{-3}$ (of $7$; rate $8.25\times10^{-3}$); both $1.517\times10^{-3}$ (of $5$; rate
    $5.916\times10^{-3}$). The Muon baseline's is $1.374\times10^{-3}$ (magnitude $1.11\times10^{-3}$). The best trial,
    the pair head's, is at $0.86$ of the baseline's objective and $0.87$ of its magnitude loss, and only $2$ of the
    $20$ trials beat the baseline. Inside the search the best with a knob on is at $0.58$ of the best with both off,
    but the search put both of its knobs-off trials at low rates, and over the $16$ trials that did not blow up the
    objective's rank correlation with the rate is $-0.89$: that ratio measures the rate, not the knobs. The objective
    cannot see the sign either: it is $1.18$ to $1.25$ times the magnitude loss in every trial, whatever the knobs
    ($1.24$ for the baseline), so the sign cross-entropy on the training processes' events, whose signs the network
    learns within a pool, adds a near-constant share and the search ranks the trials by their magnitude. So the
    search shows no gain from either knob on what it measures, one seed each, and the pair head's best only at a rate
    where its unbounded variant mostly blows up.
    Since the objective cannot see the sign, the trials are ranked again by the wrong-sign share on the validation
    events of the $155$ sign-changing training pools ($200$ events each), training processes only, read at no new
    compute from the sign accuracy every validation logs, at the best checkpoint
    (\code{data/sign\_head\_search\_signlog.txt}). The median over pools separates nothing: every trial that did not
    blow up gets $1$ to $3$ events of $200$ wrong in its median pool ($3$ trials at one event, $10$ at two, $3$ at
    three), the Muon baseline $1$, the AdamW arm with more sign-changing pools $3$. The worst pool, nearly always the
    one-loop $u\bar d\to\gamma c\bar s$, is $5.0\%$ to $20.5\%$ wrong over the calm trials; the best of each kind is
    $5.0\%$ with the pair head, $6.0\%$ with both off, $6.0\%$ with both on, $7.5\%$ with the near-zero weight, against
    $5.5\%$ for the Muon baseline and $10.5\%$ for the AdamW arm, and the best trial by the objective has $7.5\%$. One
    pool's share has a binomial spread of $1.6$ points at these values, so the best of every kind and the Muon
    baseline are within one spread of each other; the worst-pool share follows the overall fit (rank correlation
    $0.71$ with the objective over the calm trials, $-0.4$ with the rate), not a knob. On the training processes'
    signs neither knob wins.
    The best trial read zero-shot on every held-out axis (run FA-ccin2p3-ef655fd7, the short pretraining's read in
    eval mode), against the Muon baseline's and the AdamW arm's reads, scored as \code{prep\_signed.py} scores them:
    on the $17$ held-out one-loop pools that all three read as sign-changing, its median wrong-sign share is $2.8\%$,
    the Muon baseline's $3.8\%$ and the AdamW arm's $4.8\%$. It gets $12$ pools less wrong than the Muon baseline and
    $3$ more (the AdamW arm $6$ and $10$), and the gain is in the two pools the baseline gets most wrong: the one-loop
    $e^+e^-\to c\bar c g$, $40.8\%$ to $26.7\%$ (AdamW $70.5\%$), and $d\bar d\to\gamma g$, $37.9\%$ to $29.6\%$
    (AdamW $48.8\%$). Its zero-shot MSE over the $617$ held-out probes all three read is a median $0.67$ of the Muon
    baseline's per probe, above it on $137$; per axis $0.51$ to $0.78$, except the $1$ to $10$ TeV energy axis at
    $1.3$. The AdamW arm is at $2.07$, above on $482$. So the best trial is better than the Muon baseline held out, on
    the sign and on the magnitude, but it differs from it twice at once: the pair head, and a rate $2.5$ times the
    baseline's with a shorter warmup, at which its training magnitude loss is already $0.87$ of the baseline's; and on
    the training pools' validation signs, where a sign knob would show first, it is not separated from the knobs-off
    trials. With one seed and no knobs-off trial at its rate, the held-out gain cannot be given to the pair head. The
    test that can: the Muon baseline, both knobs off, at the best trial's rate and warmup, read zero-shot the same way.
\begin{figure}[t]\centering
\includegraphics{../analysis/catalog_v3/figs/sign_head_search.pdf}
\caption{The sign head search on catalog\_v3: $20$ short pretrainings with Muon, one seed each, by which sign knob is
on; hollow, the trials that blew up (read at their best checkpoint); star, the Muon baseline (both knobs off, not a
trial of the search). (a) The objective (magnitude MSE plus the sign cross-entropy on the training processes'
validation events) against the learning rate. (b) The objective over the magnitude loss against the learning
rate. (c) The wrong-sign share on the worst sign-changing training pool's validation events at the best checkpoint,
against the learning rate. (d) Per held-out sign-changing one-loop pool, the zero-shot wrong-sign share of the best
trial (the pair head's) and of the AdamW arm against the Muon baseline's; the line is equality.}\label{fig:v3_sign_head_search}
\end{figure}
\end{itemize}

So the sign is no data problem: within a pool a small classifier on the invariants gets all but $1.0\%$ and $0.8\%$ of
it (the pools with too few of one sign to fit it are nearly all one sign), and the
zero-shot errors of $5$ to $21\%$ are a transfer failure from the training pools' signs, smallest for the baseline. The
magnitude's target matters less on these pools than on the trees. One seed each arm.

\paragraph{The sampling sensitivity on catalog\_v3 (2026-10-26).}\label{par:v3_prep_sampling} How much of what a target
and a trained network show depends on how the pool drew its events? Two reweightings of each pool's events: flat in
$\log\hat s$ (equal weight per bin of $\log\hat s$, catalog\_v4's measure inside catalog\_v3's window), and toward the
singular regions (the fifth of the events with the smallest pair invariant over $\hat s$ carrying half the weight).
On the validation pools of the shape proxy's training classes and of every held-out axis, and on the eight short arms'
zero-shot predictions of every held-out probe (\code{analysis/catalog\_v3/prep\_sampling\_pools.py}, run
FA-ccin2p3-bc20e0d6 on CC's CPUs at \code{26c4ecf0}; \code{prep\_sampling.py}, \code{data/prep\_sampling.json},
Fig.~\ref{fig:v3_prep_sampling}); the saved events are matched to the pool in order of $\log|\mathcal M|^2$, the events
at the sign head's floor left out, and every arm aligns on every probe.
% src: python3 analysis/catalog_v3/prep_sampling.py @26c4ecf0
% allow: ^2
% claim: n_classes=817
% claim: tr_n=200
% claim: ho_n=602
% claim: all_n_probes=617
% claim: n_missing=0
$817$ classes: $200$ training and $602$ held-out positive pools in the data part, all $617$ held-out probes in the
network part, $0$ arm and probe pairs without numbers. Medians over classes, training then held out:
\begin{itemize}
  \item \emph{The $\hat s$ factor makes the level less sensitive to the energy measure, not more.} Reweighted flat in

    $\log\hat s$, the level of $\log|\mathcal M|^2$ moves by $0.30$ and $0.25$ of the pool's std, of the $\hat s$ factor's
    target by $0.10$ and $0.074$, of the emission scales' by $0.11$ and $0.087$ (the soft minimum's the same), and the
    std hardly changes (ratio $0.97$ to $0.98$). On Daniel's catalog the factor raised it ($0.30$ to $0.39$, par.\
    More data-free normalizations); the next item says why.
  \item \emph{Why the factor helps on catalog\_v3 and hurt on Daniel's catalog: the cuts, not the factor.} The factor
    adds $N-4$ to the within-process slope of $\log|\mathcal M|^2$ on $\log\hat s$, so it removes the level's
    dependence on the energy draw only where that slope follows the mass dimension ($0$, $-1$, $-2$ at $2\to2$, $2\to3$,
    $2\to4$). Per training class, the slope of $\log|\mathcal M|^2$ and of the least pair invariant over $\hat s$ on
    $\log\hat s$ (\code{analysis/catalog\_v3/prep\_shat\_slope.py}: catalog\_v3's $200$ training classes on CC's login
    node at \code{47d38a08}, Daniel's $1352$ from their stored moments):

% claim: catalog_v3.n=200
% claim: daniel.n=1352
% claim: catalog_v3.byN.5.b=-1.14
% claim: catalog_v3.byN.6.b=-2.15

% claim: catalog_v3.byN.5.b_lmin=-0.03
% claim: catalog_v3.byN.6.b_lmin=-0.014
% claim: catalog_v3.byN.6.share=0.73
% claim: catalog_v3.byN.5.abs_raw=0.28
% claim: catalog_v3.byN.5.abs_dless=0.105
% claim: daniel.byN.5.b=0.62
% claim: daniel.byN.5.b_plus_factor=1.62
% claim: daniel.byN.4.b=1.14
% claim: daniel.byN.5.b_lmin=-0.65
% claim: daniel.byN.4.b_lmin=-0.47
% claim: daniel.byN.5.share=0.825
% claim: daniel.byN.5.abs_raw=0.27
% claim: daniel.byN.5.abs_dless=0.36
% allow: ^2
% allow: 0 because it is the mass dimension's slope at 2\to2
% allow: -1 because it is the mass dimension's slope at 2\to3
% allow: -2 because it is the mass dimension's slope at 2\to4
% allow: 4 because it is the 4 of the factor's exponent N-4
    on catalog\_v3 $\log|\mathcal M|^2$ falls with $\log\hat s$ as its mass dimension says, slope $-1.14$ at $2\to3$ and
    $-2.15$ at $2\to4$ ($-0.14$ and $-0.15$ with the factor), and the least invariant over $\hat s$ does not move with
    $\hat s$ (slope $-0.03$ and $-0.014$): its pools reach the singular regions at the same fraction of $\hat s$ at every
    energy. On Daniel's catalog the least invariant over $\hat s$ falls with $\hat s$ (slope $-0.65$ at $2\to3$,
    $-0.47$ at $2\to2$), the cut fixed in GeV letting a high-$\hat s$ event reach closer to its soft and collinear
    limits, and that lifts $\log|\mathcal M|^2$ above its mass dimension (slope $+0.62$ at $2\to3$, $+1.14$ at
    $2\to2$); the factor then adds to a slope that was already positive ($+1.62$). At $2\to3$, the multiplicity both
    catalogs share, the shift goes from $0.28$ to $0.105$ on catalog\_v3 and from $0.27$ to $0.36$ on Daniel's. The mix
    of multiplicities only sets the size: $2\to4$ is $0.73$ of catalog\_v3's training classes, $2\to3$ is $0.825$ of
    Daniel's. So the $\hat s$ factor makes a target independent of the energy draw only on pools whose singular reach
    scales with $\hat s$, as catalog\_v3's do; on pools cut in GeV only a per-event scale that sees the singular region (the
    per-event scales of par.\ More data-free normalizations) lowered it.
  \item \emph{Toward the singular regions every target moves by about a fifth of its std.} The level moves by $0.135$

% claim: ho_raw_sd_low=1.06
% claim: ho_dless_sd_low=1.11
    and $0.19$ for $\log|\mathcal M|^2$, $0.22$ and $0.25$ with the $\hat s$ factor, $0.205$ and $0.205$ with the emission
    scales, and the std grows ($1.06$ to $1.11$ held out): the per-event terms do not take the singular regions out
    of the level.
  \item \emph{The network's error moves the same way for every arm.} Over all held-out probes the median zero-shot MSE

    on $\log|\mathcal M|^2$ changes by a factor $0.97$ to $1.015$ flat in $\log\hat s$ and $1.12$ to $1.19$ toward the

% allow: ^2

    uniform one on new combinations and $2\to4$ (the hybrid), on the top (the context arm), on one loop and on $1$ to
    $10$\,TeV (the $\hat s$ arm). It changes on near ties: at $2\to5$ flat (the blend at $\lambda=0.5$ over the context
    arm), at $\alpha_s=0.13$ flat (the blend at $0.25$ over the hybrid), and at $p_T\geq30$\,GeV toward the singular
    regions (the hybrid, $0.40$, over the $\hat s$ arm, $0.45$). The whole order of the eight arms is kept in $2$ of
    the $16$ axis and reweighting pairs, by swaps of neighbours. The largest moves are at $1$ to $10$\,TeV: toward the
    singular regions the dimensionless arms' error grows by $1.22$ to $1.50$, while the baseline's, mostly level,
    falls ($0.83$; $0.56$ flat in $\log\hat s$).
\end{itemize}
So the sampling measure does not decide between the targets: weighting the singular regions up costs every arm
about the same fraction, and the ranking changes only between arms already within a few percent of each other, which
one seed each cannot separate (a second seed not run).

open. (b) Per short arm, the median zero-shot MSE over all held-out probes reweighted against uniform. One seed each.}
\label{fig:v3_prep_sampling}
\end{figure}

\paragraph{Muon against AdamW on the short pretraining, its zero-shot reads and small-$D$ fine-tunes (2026-10-26).}\label{par:v3_muon} The
user's question (D144): does Muon, the orthogonalised-update optimizer, now with decoupled weight decay, beat AdamW in
this setup, and does it transfer better, as is claimed for it? The design: the dimensionless short arm (the short
setup of the paragraphs above, the same HPs, its learning rate the per-process pretraining sweep's best for AdamW) run
with \code{MuonAdamW} (\code{optim/muon.py}): Muon on $\mu$P's hidden matrices with decoupled weight decay and its
update scaled to AdamW's RMS, so the same learning rate number applies, and AdamW as before on every other parameter.
Each optimizer is run with two seeds (model initialisation and batch order; the pools unchanged, D146), every run
read zero-shot on every held-out axis as the other short arms. Neither optimizer is retuned for this catalog, so this
compares Muon at AdamW's learning rate. That baseline is itself untuned here: AdamW's HPs (learning rate
$8.94\times10^{-3}$, the L2 weight, warmup and EMA) are the best trial of a $32000$-step search on Daniel's catalog,
transferred to this catalog and a horizon about eight times shorter, and both of AdamW's validation curves spike sharply
mid-run (Fig.~\ref{fig:v3_muon}a), the mark of a learning rate too high for the setup. Stage 1 therefore measures Muon
against an untuned AdamW, not against AdamW at its best, which the A/B protocol requires; stage 2 (D146) searches both,
each an 8-trial single-fidelity search at this horizon from this run's exact configuration (learning rate in
$[3\times10^{-3}, 2.7\times10^{-2}]$ for both, Muon's decoupled weight decay in $[10^{-3}, 0.3]$, AdamW's L2 weight in
$[10^{-10}, 10^{-6}]$; \code{sweep/sweep\_config\_ccin2p3\_muon\_test\_search\_\{muon,adamw\}.yaml}), read best
against best in the item below.
% allow: 8.94 32000 because they are the transferred learning rate and the source search's horizon, settings
% allow: 3 2.7 0.3 10 8 because they are the stage-2 search windows and trial count, settings
% allow: 1 2 because they number the stages of the test
Small-$D$ fine-tunes from both pretrainings are the third item
(\code{analysis/catalog\_v3/muon\_compare.py}, \code{data/muon\_pretrain.json}, \code{data/muon\_compare.json},
Fig.~\ref{fig:v3_muon}; runs FA-ccin2p3-ee733aa9, FA-ccin2p3-2bc30261 (Muon), FA-ccin2p3-87426222 (AdamW's second
seed)).
\begin{itemize}
  \item \emph{Pretraining.} The best-checkpoint validation loss (standardized target, no regularization) is
% src: python3 analysis/catalog_v3/muon_compare.py @880da85c
% claim: pre.dimensionless=0.0155
% claim: pre.dimensionless_seed2=0.0173
% claim: pre.dimensionless_muon=0.00197
% claim: pre.dimensionless_muon_seed2=0.00245
% claim: pre_ratio42=7.88
% claim: pre_ratio43=7.07
% claim: pools.n=1397
% claim: pools.ratio_median=8.03
% claim: pools.muon_wins_both_pct=99.4
% claim: pools.seed_spread_adamw=0.065
% claim: pools.seed_spread_muon=0.107
% allow: 42 43 because they are the seeds, settings
    $0.0155$ and $0.0173$ with AdamW, $0.00197$ and $0.00245$ with Muon: $7.88\times$ lower at seed 42, $7.07\times$
    at seed 43, while the second seed moves either optimizer's loss far less. Per training pool
    (all $1397$, each at the best checkpoint), AdamW over Muon is a median $8.03$, and Muon is lower at both seeds on
    $99.4\%$ of them; the seeds move a pool by a median $0.065$ decades with AdamW, $0.107$ with Muon. Every run's best
    is its last validation: both are still improving at this horizon.
  \item \emph{Zero-shot.}
% src: python3 analysis/catalog_v3/muon_compare.py @880da85c
% claim: zs.all.n=617

% allow: 0.13 because it is the held-out coupling value, a setting
% allow: 30 1 10 because they are the pT threshold and the energy window in TeV, settings
    Over all $617$ held-out probes the ratio of AdamW's error to Muon's (each the two seeds'
    geometric mean) is a median $4.47$; Muon is lower at both seeds on $88\%$ of the probes, AdamW on $4\%$, and the
    seeds move a probe by a median $0.124$ decades (AdamW) and $0.148$ (Muon), well under the gap. Per axis: new
    combinations $6.56$, $2\to4$ families $5.53$, the top $3.74$, one loop $2.81$, $\alpha_s=0.13$ $12.4$, $p_T\geq30$\,GeV
    $7.53$. The two extrapolation axes gain least: $2\to5$ $1.77$ (Muon lower at both seeds on $68\%$, AdamW on $15\%$),
    and $1$ to $10$\,TeV $1.18$, a tie (Muon lower on $50\%$, AdamW on $30\%$; the median error is $2.88$ for AdamW's
    first seed and $3.42$ for Muon's second).
  \item \emph{Stage 2: each optimizer at its own HPs.} The question: does Muon's lead survive once AdamW, whose
    stage-1 curves spike, is tuned for this catalog and horizon? The design: two single-fidelity DyHPO searches at the
    short pretraining's $4264$ steps on CC, $8$ trials each ($3$ random start-up, then the GP surrogate, run in three
    waves through \code{sweep\_manager}), every trial the AdamW short arm's exact configuration with only the searched
    HPs changed (seed 42, the same pools and validation events as stage 1): the learning rate in $[3\times10^{-3},
    2.7\times10^{-2}]$ for both, Muon's decoupled weight decay in $[10^{-3}, 0.3]$, AdamW's L2 weight in
    $[10^{-10}, 10^{-6}]$; the warmup fraction, the minimum learning rate and the EMA at stage 1's values. Both searches
    draw from one candidate pool (seed 42), so the start-up trials share their learning rates. The objective DyHPO
    observed is each trial's \code{val\_loss\_no\_reg} at its best checkpoint (the MSE loss, no regularization), the
    number stage 1 reports. All $16$ trials ended, none crashed (\code{data/muon\_search.json},
    Fig.~\ref{fig:v3_muon_search}).

% allow: 4264 8 3 because they are the horizon, the trial count and the start-up trials, settings
% allow: 3 2.7 0.3 10 because they are the search windows' edges, settings
% allow: 16 because it is the two searches' trial count
% allow: 42 because it is the seed, a setting
% allow: 8.94 0.1 because they are stage 1's learning rate and weight decay, settings
% allow: 1 2 because they number the stages of the test
    \emph{Best against best}: AdamW $0.0141$ (learning rate $0.00782$, L2 weight $9.99\times10^{-7}$, at the window's
    top), Muon $0.00286$ (learning rate $0.005$, weight decay $0.0217$): AdamW over Muon $4.91$. Tuning AdamW gained
    $1.1\times$ over stage 1's $0.0155$ (seed 42). Muon's search did not reach stage 1's untuned point: its best is
    $1.45$ times stage 1's $0.00197$ (learning rate $8.94\times10^{-3}$, weight decay $0.1$, not among the trials), and
    stage 1's second seed ($0.00245$) is also below every Muon trial. The configurations differ only in the searched
    HPs (checked on the run configs), and the code between the stage-1 runs and the searches changed nothing a fresh
    pretraining runs (the EMA restart rebuilds the same shadow), so that point is either a narrow optimum the eight
    trials missed or a favourable draw; not resolved. Against the best Muon run known, the stage-1 point, the best
    AdamW is $7.14$ times higher. \emph{The landscape}: both optimizers are flat inside the stable range and fail at
    the same learning rate. AdamW runs from $0.0176$ at learning rate $0.00349$ to $0.0141$ at $0.00782$, Muon stays
    between $0.00286$ and $0.00322$ from $0.00391$ to $0.0105$ (the worst at weight decay $0.3$): neither second HP,
    over two decades, moves its optimizer by as much as the gap between them. From $0.0148$ up every trial of both
    blew up, whatever its second HP (no trial lies between $0.0105$ and $0.0148$): $3$ per optimizer, AdamW's at
    steps $680$ to $850$ (best checkpoints $0.088$ to $0.0933$), Muon's at $595$ to $1020$ ($0.0246$ to $0.0476$),
    each read at its best checkpoint before the blow-up (a validation over ten times its running minimum, a technical
    choice under the user's ``do whatever is correct and simpler''; five times picks the same validations on every run
    so far, the pair sign head's included). One more
    AdamW trial (learning rate $0.005$, L2 weight $1.44\times10^{-8}$) spiked at step $1445$ and recovered to
    $0.0196$, best at its last validation like every stable trial. So after both searches Muon's pretraining lead is
    about five times (search against search) to seven (against stage 1's Muon point), not an artefact of an untuned
    AdamW.
    \emph{Zero-shot, best against best.} The question: does Muon's zero-shot lead of stage 1 hold once both are at
    their own searched HPs? The design: each search's best trial (seed 42, one run each) read zero-shot on all eight
    held-out axes exactly as stage 1's runs were (\code{scripts/job\_catalog\_v3\_short.sh}, arms
    \code{muon\_search\_best\_\{adamw,muon\}}, mode eval; the MSE of $\log|\mathcal M|^2$ on each probe's $2000$
    validation events; runs FA-ccin2p3-41b4f15a, FA-ccin2p3-0caa5e2a), compared per probe on the probes every run has,
    with stage 1's seed spread as the scale a ratio is read against (Fig.~\ref{fig:v3_muon_search_zs}).

% allow: 2000 because it is the validation events read, a setting
% allow: 42 because it is the seed, a setting
% allow: 617 because it is the probe count of the eight axes
% allow: 0.13 30 because they are the held-out coupling and the pT threshold, settings
% allow: 1 2 because they number the stages of the test
% allow: 1.4 because it is 10^0.148 rounded, the seed spread restated as a factor
    The AdamW best's read of one probe of new combinations ($u\bar b\to\gamma\bar b uZ$) failed once on the site
    (its checkpoint reported missing, the same file every other probe loaded), so the comparison is on $616$ of the
    $617$ probes. Over all of them AdamW's best over Muon's best is a median $3.29$ per probe, and Muon's is lower on
    $91.2\%$ (median errors $1.14$ and $0.397$). Inside the catalog's range the lead is large on every axis: new
    combinations $4.63$ (Muon lower on $100\%$), $2\to4$ families $3.87$, the top $2.84$, $\alpha_s=0.13$ $7.16$,
    $p_T\geq30$\,GeV $4.59$ ($100\%$); one loop $1.79$ and $2\to5$ $1.89$ ($85\%$). The energy extrapolation, a tie
    at stage 1, is now Muon's too: $2.94$, lower on $75\%$ (median $2.46$ against $1.26$), because the Muon search's
    best extrapolates better than stage 1's Muon there ($0.781$ times its error) while elsewhere it is equal or worse.
    Against stage 1: tuning moved AdamW by nothing ($1.03$ times stage 1's seed-42 error, inside the seed spread),
    and the Muon best is $1.47$ times stage 1's Muon (lower on only $21.8\%$ of the probes; $1.73$ on new combinations,
    $1.64$ on $2\to4$, flat on $2\to5$ ($1.01$) and one loop ($1.05$)), just past the seed spread of $0.148$ decades,
    a factor of about $1.4$, and the same factor as its pretraining loss ($1.45$). Against stage 1's Muon point, the
    best AdamW is $4.87$ times higher. So the zero-shot follows the pretraining: tuned AdamW does not close the gap,
    which is about three times search against search and five against stage 1's Muon, smallest on the two
    extrapolations in particle count and on one loop.
  \item \emph{Fine-tunes at small $D$} (the user, 2026-10-26: ``I agree with that reframing of the question for the
    finetuning at small D, go ahead with it''), three questions: does the Muon pretraining transfer better, does
    fine-tuning a current pretraining beat scratch at small $D$ (the question par.~\ref{par:ft_harm} hands on), and
    does the fine-tune end below the parent's own zero-shot error. The design (\code{tools/catalog\_ft.py},
    \code{scripts/job\_catalog\_ft.sh}): four held-out probes, the dimensionless arm's median-error probe of new
    combinations ($dg\to dgW^+W^-$), $2\to4$ families ($u\bar s\to\bar c uW^+Z$), the top ($gg\to\gamma t\bar t$) and
    one loop ($u\bar d\to\gamma gW^+$), fixed before any fine-tune ran; the four cells of the small-$D$ round ($D=10$,
    $32$, $100$, $316$ training events at $176$ to $4576$ steps); full retraining from the best checkpoint of each
    optimizer's first-seed pretraining, with each fine-tune optimizer (the $2\times2$ of parent and fine-tune
    optimizer), each in two target standardizations: the parent's kept (its pooled mean and $\sigma$, so step $0$ is
    the parent's zero-shot on the probe) and refitted on the probe's $D$ events (the parent's output then read in the
    probe's own units); and the same cells from scratch with the parent's configuration and AdamW. $128$ fine-tune
    cells and $16$ from scratch, \emph{one seed per cell, the fine-tune HPs untuned}: the parent's learning rate (scale
    $1$), no layer decay (decay $1$), the parent's other HPs, scratch likewise. Every cell is read at its best
    checkpoint as the MSE of $\log|\mathcal M|^2$ on the probe's first $2000$ validation events, the zero-shot read's
    events; the zero-shot reference is each parent-stats cell's step-$0$ validation, the same events in the same target
    (Fig.~\ref{fig:v3_muon_ft}; \code{data/muon\_ft.json}; runs FA-ccin2p3-70adfc03, FA-ccin2p3-8103a0bb,
    FA-ccin2p3-13e57de7, FA-ccin2p3-e5265e31 (parent's standardization), FA-ccin2p3-fe864fda, FA-ccin2p3-f620ed40,
    FA-ccin2p3-60177afb, FA-ccin2p3-29e8ad2f (refit), FA-ccin2p3-00d080ba (scratch)). All of them ran after the fix of
    the weight average that started on the random initialisation (par.~\ref{par:ema_start}); the pre-fix fine-tunes
    are not read. The only seed reference is the zero-shot one above: the pretraining seed moves a probe's error by a
    median $0.124$ to $0.148$ decades, a factor of about $1.4$, so a single-cell ratio inside that is not resolved.

% allow: 1 because it is the fine-tune learning rate scale and layer decay, settings

% allow: 0.1 because it is the tolerance that counts a cell as at the zero-shot, a setting
    \emph{Does the fine-tune end below the zero-shot?} With the parent's standardization kept, almost always: from
    the AdamW parent all $16$ cells end below it with either fine-tune optimizer (fine-tune over zero-shot, median
    $0.387$ with AdamW, $0.416$ with Muon), from the Muon parent all $16$ with the Muon fine-tune ($0.553$) and $13$
    with AdamW ($0.487$). Of the AdamW fine-tune's other three, two ($dg\to dgW^+W^-$ and $gg\to\gamma t\bar t$ at
    $D=10$) end within $0.1\%$ of it, the best checkpoint being the first validation after an update, and one ($dg\to dgW^+W^-$ at
    $D=32$) $1.25$ times above it, since the step-$0$ validation is not a checkpoint the selection can return. The
    gain grows with $D$: from the Muon parent with AdamW the median ratio is $0.847$ at $D=10$ and $0.231$ at
    $D=316$. Refitting the standardization loses this: the refit fine-tunes end below the zero-shot in only $11$ and
    $12$ cells (AdamW parent) and $10$ and $8$ (Muon parent), and from the Muon parent at $D=10$ they end a median
    $8.42$ (AdamW) and $7.35$ (Muon) times above it: the refit start reads the parent's output in the probe's own
    units, which discards the level the zero-shot carries ($dg\to dgW^+W^-$ from the Muon parent: zero-shot $0.0707$,
    the refit start at $D=10$ $3.01$), and at small $D$ the fine-tune does not climb back.
    \emph{Refit against the parent's standardization}, refit over kept: from the AdamW parent a median $1.09$ (kept
    lower in $13$ of $16$, AdamW fine-tune) and $1.02$ ($10$, Muon fine-tune), $1.76$ and $1.96$ at $D=10$, a tie
    ($1.02$, $0.986$) at $D=316$; from the Muon parent $1.83$ ($15$) and $1.84$ ($14$), $9.62$ and $9.8$ at $D=10$,
    still $1.23$ and $1.34$ at $D=316$. Keeping the parent's standardization is the better fine-tune, and matters most
    where the zero-shot is good and $D$ small.
    \emph{Does Muon's pretraining transfer better?} AdamW parent over Muon parent, with the standardization kept: a
    median $3.2$ (AdamW fine-tune, Muon parent lower in all $16$) and $3.23$ (Muon fine-tune, $14$), from $5.43$ and
    $5.99$ at $D=10$ to $3.2$ and $3.23$ at $D=316$; refit, $1.39$ and $1.35$, from $1.11$ at $D=10$ to $2.73$ at
    $D=316$ (AdamW fine-tune), the refit throwing away the Muon parent's head start at small $D$. Either way this does
    not isolate a transfer property of Muon: the Muon parent is the better pretraining to begin with (eight times lower
    on its own pools), and on these probes its lead after fine-tuning is under half its zero-shot lead (AdamW's
    step-$0$ error over Muon's, a median $7.13$ over the $16$ cells).
    \emph{The fine-tune optimizer} adds nothing: AdamW fine-tune over Muon fine-tune is $0.986$ (Muon lower in $6$
    cells) from the AdamW parent and $0.935$ ($6$) from the Muon parent with the standardization kept, $0.993$ ($8$)
    and $0.892$ ($3$) refit.
    \emph{Against scratch}, every one of the $128$ fine-tune cells ends below the same cell from scratch. Scratch over
    fine-tune with the standardization kept: from the AdamW parent $2.18$ (at least $1.33$, AdamW fine-tune) and
    $2.05$ (Muon), from the Muon parent $9.11$ and $9.04$, $11.9$ at $D=10$ and $7.57$ at $D=316$ (AdamW fine-tune);
    refit, $1.74$, $1.71$, $2.55$, $2.58$. Unlike the transfer study's older pretrainings (par.~\ref{par:ft_harm}), a
    current one never loses to scratch here.
    \emph{The one-loop probe's step $0$ moves with $D$} ($1.09$ at $D=10$ to $1.15$ at $D=316$ from the AdamW parent,
    $1.51$ to $1.52$ from the Muon parent, against the zero-shot read's $1.17$), while the three tree probes' is the
    zero-shot read at every $D$. The parent's weights and the validation events are the same in every cell; the
    target is not. $u\bar d\to\gamma gW^+$ at one loop changes sign, so its target is the signed log
    $\mathrm{sgn}(x)\log(1+|x|/s)$, whose scale $s$ is the $1\%$ quantile of $|x|$ over the training split the cell
    sees: $0.542$, $0.372$, $0.365$ and $0.179$ over $D=10$, $32$, $100$ and $316$ events (the zero-shot read's split
    is larger again). The validation target, and with it the step-$0$ error, therefore shifts slightly with $D$ in
    every cell of that probe (refit and scratch too); the cells of one $D$ share it, so the comparisons above, all
    within one $D$, are unaffected.
  \item \emph{The Muon fine-tune hyperparameters} (the user, 2026-10-26: ``We may have to explore a bit the HP space for
    muon, that's fine, the gain is clearly worth it''). The question: the fine-tunes above ran untuned (the parent's
    learning rate, no layer decay); now that Muon is the fine-tune optimizer too, where are its best fine-tune
    learning rate scale and layer decay, and how much do they gain? The design: one single-fidelity DyHPO search
    (\code{sweep/sweep\_config\_ccin2p3\_muon\_hp\_ft.yaml}, $12$ trials, $4$ random start-up, three waves on CC) over
    the fine-tune learning rate scale, log-uniform in $[0.1, 10]$, and the layer decay, uniform in $[0.75, 1]$ (HPO
    rule 4's windows). A trial is the whole grid of the item above at one HP point: the four probes times $D=10$,
    $32$, $100$, $316$, each a full retraining with Muon from the Muon pretraining's best checkpoint (seed 42) with the
    parent's standardization kept and its other HPs, one seed per cell; the objective is the geometric mean of the
    $16$ cells' best-checkpoint \code{val\_loss\_no\_reg} (the parent's standardized target, on each probe's first
    $2000$ validation events), so no single cell sets the best point. The reference is the same $16$ cells untuned,
    from the item above (Muon parent, Muon fine-tune, parent's standardization). Every cell's step-$0$ validation (the
    parent's weights on the same events and target) equals the reference cell's to within a relative
    $5\times10^{-7}$, so the target did not change between the two, and the two launchers differ in nothing a cell
    trains on (same seed, warmup fraction and scheduler; checked on the run configs).
    (\code{analysis/catalog\_v3/muon\_hp\_ft.py}, \code{data/muon\_hp\_ft.json}, Fig.~\ref{fig:v3_muon_hp_ft}.)
% src: python3 analysis/catalog_v3/muon_hp_ft.py @feaaedbb

% claim: muon_hp_ft.step0_maxdev=5e-07

% allow: 12 4 because they are the trial count and the start-up trials, settings
% allow: 0.1 10 0.75 1 because they are the search windows' edges and the untuned point, settings
% allow: 10 32 100 316 because they are the cells' training events, settings
% allow: 16 because it counts the cells of a trial
% allow: 42 2000 because they are the seed and the validation events read, settings
% allow: 0.3 0.36 0.82 0.91 1.2 2.9 0.76 0.767 9.3 because they are trial HPs (data/muon_hp_ft.json trials), rounded
% allow: 6 because it is 0.00498 over 0.00469, the two claimed ends, as a percentage
    All $12$ trials ended, none failed, and no validation of any of their $192$ cells is non-finite. The untuned
    objective is $0.00556$; the best trial reaches $0.00469$ at learning rate scale $0.343$ and layer decay $0.825$,
    $1.19$ times lower. It sits inside both windows, $0.268$ of the way up the logarithmic scale window and $0.299$ up
    the decay window, not at an edge. The landscape is flat over most of the window: $10$ of the $12$ trials beat the
    untuned point, and they lie within $6\%$ of each other ($0.00469$ to $0.00498$) along two arms, a scale near
    $0.3$ to $0.36$ at any decay from $0.82$ to $0.91$ ($0.00469$ to $0.00492$), and a scale from $1.2$ to $2.9$ at a
    decay near $0.76$, the window's lower edge ($0.00495$ to $0.00498$), where the decay lowers the early blocks'
    learning rate by up to a factor of about ten. Both read as one thing, a lower effective learning rate than the
    parent's. Only the two trials at high scale and weak decay are worse than untuned ($0.00622$ at scale $9.3$, decay
    $0.767$; $0.0063$ at scale $5.23$). Per cell, the best trial is lower in $14$ of the $16$ (untuned over best from
    $0.984$ to $1.52$); per probe, the geometric mean over $D$ is $1.11$ ($dg\to dgW^+W^-$), $1.08$
    ($u\bar s\to\bar c uW^+Z$), $1.27$ ($gg\to\gamma t\bar t$) and $1.3$ (one loop); per $D$, over the probes, $1.07$
    at $D=10$, $1.25$ at $32$, $1.23$ at $100$ and $1.2$ at $316$. With one seed per cell and no fine-tune seed
    reference, single-cell ratios near one are not resolved; the $1.19$ on the geometric mean of $16$ cells, and its
    sign agreeing across ten trials, is.
    Every cell, tuned or not, overfits early: the best checkpoint is a median $0.02$ of the way through the run
    over all $192$ cells ($0.0302$ for the best trial) and the last validation ends a median $7.25$ times above it
    ($4.31$ for the best trial). The reported values are the best checkpoints, so this does not enter them, but it
    says the cells' step counts, set by the small-$D$ round, run far past the point where these fine-tunes stop
    improving.
\end{itemize}
So at this horizon and at AdamW's learning rate, Muon fits the training pools about eight times better and carries
most of that into the held-out processes inside the catalog's range; beyond it (one more particle, ten times the
energy) the gain shrinks or vanishes. Fine-tuned at small $D$, the Muon pretraining stays ahead, every fine-tune
beats scratch, keeping the parent's standardization beats refitting it, and Muon as the fine-tune optimizer does not
help. Both optimizers' own searches (stage 2) keep the lead: about five to seven times in pretraining and about
three to five zero-shot, now on every held-out axis including the energy extrapolation. The fine-tunes compared
above are untuned and from stage 1's parents, one seed each; the Muon fine-tune's own search gains $1.19\times$ on
the small-$D$ grid with a lower effective learning rate (a scale near $0.34$ with layer decay $0.83$), and the full
horizon is not tested.
