\paragraph{The grid in one picture (2026-10-05, the paper's summary).} Fig.~\ref{fig:gain_heatmap}
(\code{gain\_heatmap.py}, the $8$k grid) gives each probe--pretraining pair the mean over $D$ of
$\log_{10}(L_{\rm scratch}/L_{\rm fine\text{-}tune})$, each half-decade of $D$ weighted the same (the rule that ranks the
leader in Fig.~\ref{fig:rung_focus}). Of the $840$ cells
$698$ ($83\%$) fine-tune below scratch, and $106$ of the $120$ pairs gain on average. The geometric mean of the gain over
all cells is $1.70$ from rung $0$, $2.1$--$2.4$ from rungs $1$--$6$ and $3.2$--$3.4$ from rungs $7$--$9$; from rungs $7$--$9$ it is
$2.5\times$ at $D\le10^2$ and $3.7$--$4.2\times$ at $D\ge10^{2.5}$. The rung adding a probe's structure beats the rung below on
nine of twelve probes (not $u\bar u\to Zg$, $ee\to b\bar b$ at one loop, $ee\to Z\gamma$), and the best rung is that one
or a later one on nine of twelve (not $ee\to W^+W^-$, best from rung $3$, and the two one-loop probes, best from rungs $5$
and $7$). $ee\to Z\gamma$ is the one probe no pretraining helps ($0.5$--$1.2\times$). The scratch curve of the $W$-pole
arm's probe $u\bar d\to e^+\nu_e$ levels off at $1$--$2\times10^{-4}$ from $D=10^{3.5}$: every top trial there has its best
checkpoint at the last of the $8$k steps and its lr inside the window, so it is the horizon, not the search.
\begin{figure}[H]
\centering
\includegraphics{../analysis/transfer/figs/gain_heatmap.pdf}
\caption{The fine-tune's gain over scratch, $\langle\log_{10}(L_{\rm scratch}/L_{\rm fine\text{-}tune})\rangle_D$, per probe and
pretraining (rung $0$: $ee\to u\bar u$, $64$k steps), the $8$k grid; each cell the best of its search (scratch: eight
trials, fine-tune: five); outlined, the first rung with the probe's structure.}
\label{fig:gain_heatmap}
\end{figure}

\paragraph{Where a probe does not gain (2026-10-24).}\label{par:nogain} The row of $ee\to Z\gamma$ in
Fig.~\ref{fig:gain_heatmap} is negative at most rungs, but not across $D$ (\code{analysis/transfer/nogain.py}, the 8k
grid): the median gain over the ten pretrainings is $-0.13$, $-0.57$, $-1.19$, $-0.16$ decades at $D=10^{1}$ to
$10^{2.5}$ and $+0.21$, $+0.39$, $+0.19$ above. At $D=10^2$ scratch drops $2.3$ decades from the half-decade before
($1.0\times10^{-2}\to5.0\times10^{-5}$; its second-best trial $5.8\times10^{-5}$, so not one lucky point) while the
fine-tunes drop about one ($7.8\times10^{-4}$, median); they reach scratch half a decade later. The same happens on every
probe: at the half-decade where scratch's learning curve in $D$ is steepest, the gain dips below the mean of its two
neighbours in $10$ of $12$ probes (by a median $0.22$ decades; $ee\to d\bar d$ by $0.72$ at its $2.3$-decade step into
$10^3$, $ud\to ud$ by $0.71$; below both neighbours in $7$), and over all $72$ cells the gain falls with scratch's drop into the cell (correlation $-0.30$). A fine-tune's
learning curve in $D$ is smoother than scratch's, so it loses wherever scratch finds the process's structure in one
step; $ee\to Z\gamma$ has the largest such step of the twelve probes and has it at small $D$, where the $D$-average of
Fig.~\ref{fig:gain_heatmap} weighs it as much as any other cell. The fine-tunes stop early more often than scratch
(the best step in the first quarter of the run for $66$--$68\%$ of $ee\to Z\gamma$'s fine-tune trials at $10^{1.5}$,
$10^2$, none of scratch's), but so do those of $ee\to d\bar d$ ($88$--$94\%$), which gain a decade, so early stopping is
not the cause.
Rung $7$ against rung $6$ on $u\bar u\to Zg$ (rung $7$ adds only $u\bar d\to Wgg$ to rung $6$'s mixture): the gain
of rung $7$ over rung $6$ is specific to the probes with a massive vector boson and a massless one, $u\bar u\to Zg$
($+0.51$ decades averaged over $D$, $+1.1$ and $+1.0$ at $D=10^{1.5}$, $10^2$), $ee\to Z\gamma$ ($+0.39$), $u\bar u\to Zggg$
($+0.26$), against a median $+0.1$ over the twelve probes. It is not a better start: the two fine-tunes begin at the
same validation loss ($2.0$ and $1.9\times10^{-2}$ at $D=10^2$, the first validation, median over trials), and rung $6$'s
improves $34\times$ from there, rung $7$'s $308\times$. The parents are each rung's best pretraining trial (the same HP
point, $8.5$ and $4.9\times10^{-8}$ on their own mixtures). It is not the fine-tunes' hyperparameters either: at
$D=10^{1.5}$ and $10^2$ the best trial of each search sits at the same learning rate ($1.1$ and $1.4\times10^{-3}$; $1.2$ and
$1.3\times10^{-3}$). The difference is made in the first tenth of the run: by then rung $7$'s best curve has dropped
$24\times$ and $417\times$ from its first validation, rung $6$'s $3.6\times$ and $17\times$, and rung $6$'s then stalls
($4.7\times$ and $35\times$ at the end; its best checkpoint at $13\%$ of the run, \code{data/nogain\_arms.json}). From
$D=10^{2.5}$ up both keep improving to the end and the gap closes ($1.2$ and $2.5\times10^3$ at $10^{2.5}$). So rung $6$'s
pretrained function sits in a basin that $u\bar u\to Zg$ at small $D$ cannot leave, and adding $u\bar d\to Wgg$ moves it out.
Rung $7$'s frozen features already carry the target (D108; \code{tools/linear\_readout.py}, \code{nogain\_readout.py},
\code{data/nogain\_readout.json}: a ridge read-out of the head's input, mean-pooled per event, fitted on the cell's $D$
training events with the penalty from leave-one-out on them, scored on the validation split). At $D=10^2$ the read-out
on rung $7$'s frozen trunk reaches $1.5\times10^{-4}$, below scratch's $2.5\times10^{-4}$ ($0.6\times$) with no weight
of the trunk moved, and rung $6$'s $4.6\times10^{-3}$, $30\times$ worse; at every $D$ from $10^{1.5}$ rung $7$'s read-out is $2.4$ to
$30\times$ below rung $6$'s, and with $D\ge10^{3}$ it settles at $1.5\times10^{-5}$--$1.6\times10^{-5}$ against $8.8\times10^{-5}$--$1.2\times10^{-4}$. Neither
pretrained head, refitted only by an affine map, is close ($0.12$ to $0.40$). (These read-outs were first reported
multiplied by the target's variance factor a second time, which put rung $7$'s at $D=10^2$ just above scratch.)
% src: python3 analysis/transfer/nogain_readout.py @3ca721f3
% claim: d4.r7_readout=1.5e-4
% claim: d4.scratch=2.5e-4
% claim: d4.r7_over_scratch=0.6
% claim: d4.r6_readout=4.6e-3
% claim: d4.r6_over_r7=30
% claim: r6_over_r7_min=2.4
% claim: r6_over_r7_max=30
% claim: hiD.r7_min=1.5e-5
% claim: hiD.r7_max=1.6e-5
% claim: hiD.r6_min=8.8e-5
% claim: hiD.r6_max=1.2e-4
% claim: head_affine_min=0.12
% claim: head_affine_max=0.40
% allow: 10^2 10^{2} $7$ because they are a cell's D and a rung's number, settings
So adding $u\bar d\to Wgg$ puts the
structure $u\bar u\to Zg$ needs into rung $7$'s representation, linearly readable, and the fine-tune's head start
appears to come from the head finding it rather than the trunk relearning it (the read-out does not show which
weights a fine-tune moves).
On $ee\to Z\gamma$ the dip has a plainer candidate cause: the fine-tunes never searched the learning rate
scratch wins with. At $D=10^{1.5}$ and $10^2$ scratch's best trial sits at $1.6$ and $1.9\times10^{-4}$ and trains to the
end (best checkpoint at $99\%$ and $96\%$ of the run), while all $50$ fine-tune trials per cell (ten rungs, five each) lie
in $[1.0, 9.9]\times10^{-3}$ and the best stops at $14\%$ and $64\%$ (\code{data/nogain\_arms.json}, \code{ee\_Za\_curves}).
At $D=10$ scratch's best ($2.3\times10^{-3}$) is inside the window and the gain is $-0.13$; from $D=10^{2.5}$ up its
optimum comes back to the window's floor ($7.7\times10^{-4}$, $1.1\times10^{-3}$) and the gain returns. The search window is not the cause (D108; \code{nogain\_lowlr.py}, \code{data/nogain\_lowlr.json}): the same
cells searched again with the window lowered to scratch's own ($[2.4\times10^{-5}, 2.4\times10^{-3}]$ at $D=10^{1.5}$,
$[5.3\times10^{-5}, 5.3\times10^{-3}]$ at $10^2$; ten rungs, five DyHPO trials each, as the grid's cells) move the
median gain over the ten rungs from $-0.57$ to $-0.54$ decades at $D=10^{1.5}$ and from $-1.19$ to $-1.16$ at $10^2$
($-0.46$ and $-1.09$ for the better of the two searches per rung); the lowered window wins on $6$ and $7$ of the ten
rungs, by up to $0.3$ decades. Its best trials mostly sit at $2$ to $9\times10^{-4}$, and none reaches scratch at
$D=10^2$ ($1.5\times10^{-4}$ for rung $9$, the best, against $5.0\times10^{-5}$). So $ee\to Z\gamma$'s dip is in what
the pretrained start lets the fine-tune reach, not in where it was searched (Fig.~\ref{fig:nogain}b). The window alone does not make a dip, though: over the $84$ probe and $D$ cells (all twelve probes at every $D$; panel (a) of
Fig.~\ref{fig:nogain} has $72$, those with a half-decade before them),
scratch's best learning rate lies below every fine-tune trial's in $46$ (\code{nogain\_lr.py}, \code{data/nogain\_lr.json}),
and the median gain there is $+0.31$ decades against $+0.34$ in the other $38$ (correlation of the gain with the distance
below the window $-0.15$). $ee\to d\bar d$ and $ee\to b\bar b$ at one loop have scratch about a decade below the window at
$D=10^{1}$, $10^{2}$ and $10^{2.5}$ and gain $0.2$ to $0.9$ decades there. A least-squares fit of the gain on both readings gives
$-0.23$ decades per decade below the window and $-0.23$ per decade of scratch's drop, so the two weigh alike and neither
explains $ee\to Z\gamma$'s $-1.2$ at $D=10^2$ by itself. Nor is a smoother fine-tune curve a general property of the steep cells
(\code{nogain\_start.py}, \code{data/nogain\_start.json}; per cell the median over trials of the first validation and
of the best, in decades): in the $18$ cells where scratch's drop is in the top quarter the fine-tunes start $1.2$ decades
below scratch and improve $2.2$ decades within the run against scratch's $2.5$; in the other $54$, $0.9$ below, $2.6$
against $2.9$. The shortfall in improvement is the same $0.3$ decades in both. $ee\to Z\gamma$ at $D=10^2$ is the
exception: the fine-tunes start $0.9$ decades lower and improve $1.9$ decades, scratch $3.8$.

\paragraph{What the start already holds on $ee\to Z\gamma$ (2026-10-25).} A ridge read-out of every rung's frozen
pretraining (\code{tools/linear\_readout.py} as above; rung $0$ is the $ee\to u\bar u$ one; run FA-ccin2p3-b0302fe9,
\code{nogain\_readout\_za.py}, \code{data/nogain\_readout\_za.json}, Fig.~\ref{fig:nogain_readout_za}). At $D=10^{1.5}$ scratch reaches
$1.0\times10^{-2}$: the frozen features of rungs $7$ and $8$ read out below it ($8.1\times10^{-3}$, $9.0\times10^{-3}$)
and those of rungs $5$, $3$, $2$ and $6$ within $1.12$ to $1.55\times$ of it, while the fine-tunes of rungs $0$ to $6$
and $9$ sit $2.7$ to $5.6\times$ above scratch. The two rungs whose start beats scratch keep it: their fine-tunes end
lower still, $1.15$ and $1.28\times$ below the read-out. In the six other rungs
with a fine-tune the read-out beats it, by $1.81$ to $3.89\times$, by more than $2\times$ in $4$. There the start sits
near what scratch reaches and the fine-tune moves away from it. At $D=10^2$ scratch reaches
$5.0\times10^{-5}$ and no start does: the best read-out is rung $8$'s at $8.6\times10^{-5}$ ($1.70\times$), then rung $6$'s
($4.42\times$), rung $9$'s ($6.33\times$) and the finale's ($8.23\times$), up to $132\times$ for rung $0$; the fine-tunes sit
$4.6$ to $35\times$ above, and in rungs $5$, $6$ and $8$ the frozen read-out still beats the fine-tune, by $2.20$ to
$6.69\times$. So $ee\to Z\gamma$'s dip has two parts: at $D=10^{1.5}$ it is the fine-tune moving off a start within
$1.55\times$ of scratch, and at $D=10^2$ it is mostly that no pretrained representation carries the structure scratch finds
with $100$ events (at least $1.70\times$ short of scratch with a linear read-out), with the same failure on top in three
rungs. The finale's fine-tune cells are not in the collected grid, so its read-out has no fine-tune to compare.
(These read-outs were first reported multiplied by the target's variance factor a second time, which at
$D=10^{1.5}$ put rung $5$'s below scratch and made rung $7$'s tie its fine-tune.)

\begin{figure}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics{../analysis/transfer/figs/nogain.pdf}
\caption{Where fine-tuning does not gain. (a) The median gain over the ten pretrainings, $\log_{10}$ of scratch's
best over the fine-tune's, against scratch's drop into the cell from the half-decade of $D$ before, over the $72$
probe and $D$ cells of the $8$k grid (\code{analysis/transfer/nogain.py}). (b) $ee\to Z\gamma$: the learning rate
of the best trial per $D$, scratch's and the median over the ten rungs' fine-tunes, with the range every fine-tune
trial searched (\code{nogain\_fig.py}).}
\label{fig:nogain}
\end{figure}
The electroweak-Sudakov arm on the electroweak one-loop $ee\to d\bar d$ (\code{data/nogain\_arms.json}): scratch,
rung $1$ and the arm stay within $1.5\times$ of each other at every $D$ but one ($10^3$, the arm $2\times$ below), and all
three flatten between $D=10^2$ and $10^{2.5}$ ($7.6$ to $8.5\times10^{-3}$ for scratch). The arm's first validation on the
probe is $0.1$--$0.5$ at every $D$, so its pretraining on the electroweak $ee\to\mu\mu$ and $u\bar u$ virtuals carries
nothing of this target over, although the three share the Sudakov factor ($-3.7\times10^{-3}L^2$ on the Born). The
pools change sign ($65$--$92\%$ of the events negative), and a signed target's error sits at its zero crossings, which
differ between the processes. Per event at $D=10^4$ (\code{sudakov\_events.py}, \code{data/nogain\_sudakov\_events.json};
the test predictions written for the $\Delta$ plots): the $12\%$ of the events within a factor $10$ of the crossing
($|V|$ below a tenth of the pool's median) hold $96\%$ of scratch's squared error, $97\%$ of rung $1$'s (read at its chosen point, as in the $\Delta$ plots) and $95\%$ of the
arm's, and no arm gets a sign wrong. The arm loses where the other two do not, a factor $30$ to $10$ from the crossing
($8.7\%$ of the events): its error there is $1.9\times10^{-4}$ against $2.4$ and $3.2\times10^{-5}$, $37\%$ of its total.
So the probe is a zero-crossing problem for every arm, and the Sudakov pretraining makes the approach to the crossing
worse, not the far region its shared $L^2$ term describes.

\paragraph{Fine-tuning at small $D$, first round (2026-10-25).}\label{par:lowd_ft} Whether a gentler fine-tune
does better where the default one loses (par.~\ref{par:nogain}): every method at each cell's best fine-tune trial of the
grid (its HPs, steps and seed; CLAUDE.md's comparison protocol, step 3), on $60$ cells: rungs $1$, $7$, $9$ at
$D=10$, $10^{1.5}$, $10^2$, $10^{2.5}$ ($176$ to $4576$ steps) on $ee\to Z\gamma$, $ee\to d\bar d$ and $ud\to ud$, and
the two probes picked for this round by the largest mean gain at these $D$ besides them ($ee\to b\bar b$ at one loop, $+0.66$
decades; $u\bar u\to gg$, $+0.57$; \code{data/nogain.json}). Methods: the default (full retraining, the cell's layer
decay), rerun on one machine with the rest; linear probe then fine-tune (the head written as the frozen trunk's
ridge read-out before training, \code{fine\_tune.linear\_probe}); the fine-tune pulled toward the start, L2-SP
($\tfrac\lambda2|\theta-\theta^*|^2$ on every weight, \code{fine\_tune.ewc.uniform}); the lower half of the trunk frozen;
the frozen read-out itself; and per cell the better of read-out and default on validation (chosen on the split it is
scored on, as the grid's best-of-five is). The L2-SP arm ran at one strength, $\lambda=10^{-2}$, and the frozen arm at
one depth, blocks $0$ to $3$ of $8$, both values picked for this round and not tuned: a loss in those two arms is about one untuned
value, not about the method. $240$ runs and $60$ read-outs, $8.9$ GPU-h, none failed, each scored at its best validation checkpoint as the grid reports a cell (\code{tools/lowd\_ft.py},
\code{scripts/job\_lowd\_ft.sh}, \code{analysis/transfer/lowd\_ft.py}, \code{data/lowd\_ft.json};
Fig.~\ref{fig:lowd_ft}).

A single run is noisy at these $D$: the default rerun against the grid's record of the same trial is within $1.2\times$ in
$36$ of $60$ cells, the median $|\log_{10}|$ ratio $0.035$ but its $90\%$ quantile $0.41$ decades (up to $11.7\times$
on $u\bar u\to gg$, rung $1$, $D=10^2$); the medians over the $15$ cells per $D$ move little ($+0.25$ to $+0.78$ decades
over scratch rerun against $+0.39$ to $+0.78$ recorded). ``Over scratch'' sets a single rerun against the grid's scratch,
the best of eight trials selected on validation, so the selection favours scratch, which is why the rerun's gain is
the smaller at $D=10^2$ ($+0.25$ against $+0.39$ recorded). Over all five probes no method beats the default: the median gain
over it per $D$ is $-0.19$ to $+0.08$ decades for linear probe then fine-tune (it wins $5$ to $8$ of $15$ cells per
$D$), $0.00$ to $-0.87$ for L2-SP, $-0.06$ to $-0.19$ for the frozen half, and $-0.26$ to $-1.09$ for the read-out, which
falls further behind as $D$ grows; the per-cell choice takes the read-out in $3$, $5$, $2$ and $1$ of the $15$ cells
per $D$ (its median gain over scratch $+0.77$ decades against the default's $+0.57$ at $D=10^{1.5}$, equal at the others). On $ee\to Z\gamma$ alone linear probe then fine-tune beats the
default at every $D$, by $+0.11$, $+0.21$, $+0.21$ and $+0.38$ decades (medians over the three rungs), and the read-out by
$+0.16$ and $+0.38$ at $D=10$ and $10^{1.5}$. The first of those gains is a lower bound: on rung $1$ at $D=10$ linear
probe then fine-tune's reported value, $0.130$, is its validation after $2$ updates, where the weight average still
held $0.045$ of the random initialisation (par.~\ref{par:ema_start}), so it is biased upward, likely to near its
read-out ($0.116$); a lower value there can only raise that cell's gain and the median with it, and even at its read-out
the cell stays above scratch ($0.0619$). Yet the best of all methods stays below scratch there ($-0.07$, $-0.25$,
$-0.71$, $-0.01$ decades). So on the probe that does not gain the head-first start recovers part of the loss, never all of
it, and the same start costs a little on the probes that gain. The head-first start can also be lost: on $ee\to Z\gamma$, rung
$7$, $D=10^{1.5}$, the run starts from the read-out's head (the frozen read-out scores $8.1\times10^{-3}$) and its best
validation checkpoint is $2.4\times10^{-2}$, $2.9\times$ above that start and $3.4\times$ above the default
($7.0\times10^{-3}$), so at the default's HPs that fine-tune undoes the head it was given.

\paragraph{Fine-tuning at small $D$, each method with its own search (2026-10-26).}\label{par:lowd_dyhpo} The
first round ran every method at the default's HPs (par.~\ref{par:lowd_ft}). Here the three methods it did not settle,
linear probe then fine-tune, L2-SP and the frozen lower blocks, each get their own single-fidelity DyHPO search of five
trials per cell, on the same $60$ cells and steps: the grid's fine-tune space, plus for L2-SP its strength $\lambda$
(log-uniform from $10^{-8}$ to $1$) and for the frozen arm the number of frozen blocks ($2$, $4$ or $6$ of $8$)
(\code{sweep/gen\_lowd\_dyhpo.py}). Each method's best is set against the grid's own five-trial search of the default
fine-tune in the same cell, best against best (CLAUDE.md's comparison protocol, step $4$), on
\code{val\_loss\_no\_reg}, the MSE of $\log|\mathcal M|^2$ at each best trial's best validation checkpoint; every gain
below is a median over cells. All $900$ trials in the $180$ sweeps on CC have a
result. Seven linear-probe trials on H100 had failed the probe's head-match assertion from TF32 rounding of its forward
passes, not from a wrong head; the probe now runs those passes with TF32 off and the seven were rerun at their points.
Two results a full disk left as empty files were rerun at the same candidates and the empty files are not read. On the
frozen arm, rung $7$, $ee\to b\bar b$ at one loop, $D=10$, a double requeue ran one candidate twice, so that sweep has
$6$ results and its best is over its five distinct candidates (\code{analysis/transfer/lowd\_dyhpo.py},
\code{data/lowd\_dyhpo.json}; Fig.~\ref{fig:lowd_dyhpo}).

No method separates from the default. The median over the $15$ cells per $D$ of the gain over the default
($\log_{10}$ of the default's MSE over the method's), at $D=10$, $10^{1.5}$, $10^2$ and $10^{2.5}$, is $-0.04$,
$+0.07$, $+0.06$, $-0.04$ decades for linear probe then fine-tune (it wins $7$, $9$, $8$, $6$ of $15$ cells), $+0.03$,
$-0.03$, $-0.03$, $-0.02$ for L2-SP (wins $8$, $7$, $4$, $7$) and $-0.03$, $-0.08$, $-0.07$, $+0.04$ for the frozen
blocks (wins $6$, $6$, $6$, $9$). Over all $60$ cells the three win $30$, $26$ and $27$, with medians $-0.01$, $-0.02$
and $-0.03$ decades. These medians are at the scale of the noise of the comparison itself: a single run's value moves
by up to $0.41$ decades at the $90\%$ quantile (the first round's rerun of the default against the grid's record of the
same trial), and the median of that move over a $D$'s cells reaches $0.06$ decades. Over scratch the three track the
default ($+0.41$ to $+0.78$ decades for the default), except L2-SP at $D=10^2$, whose median falls to $0.00$ where it
wins only $4$ of the $15$ cells against the default. On $ee\to Z\gamma$, the probe where linear probe then fine-tune
beat the default at the default's HPs, its own search keeps a gain at three $D$, $+0.11$, $+0.13$ and $+0.26$ decades
(medians over the three rungs), and loses it at $D=10^{2.5}$ ($-0.04$). So searching L2-SP's strength and the frozen
depth removes the first round's loss from their untuned values but adds no gain, and at $D\le 10^{2.5}$ none of the
three gentler fine-tunes is worth its extra search over the default full retraining with layer decay.

\paragraph{The fine-tunes' weight average started on the random initialisation (2026-10-26).}\label{par:ema_start}
Until commit \code{ddfc0314} a fine-tune with EMA on built its weight average on the network's fresh random
initialisation and loaded the parent's weights only afterwards, and the average was never reset, so it kept a share of
the random weights. With the average's warm-up decay $\min(\beta,(1+n)/(10+n))$ at update $n$, that share after $N$
updates is $W(N)=10!\,(N+1)!/(N+10)!$, the same for every $\beta$ the fine-tunes used: $0.18$ after one update,
$0.045$ after two, $0.014$ after three. Every validation of such a run scores the average, so its checkpoint choice and
its reported value carry the share. It touched every fine-tune with EMA on run before the fix: the transfer study's,
both small-$D$ rounds' and the Muon test's (par.~\ref{par:v3_muon}); scratch runs and runs with EMA off are not
affected. A fine-tune validates every $1\%$ of its updates and at least every update, so only the $D=10$ fine-tunes
($176$ updates, a validation after each) can report a validation within the first few updates; from $D=10^{1.5}$ on
the first validation comes at update $7$ or later, where the share is negligible. In the transfer study none of the
$327$ fine-tune searches at $D=10$ reports its value at updates $1$ to $3$, so none of its conclusions changes. It does
reach linear probe then fine-tune's first validation in par.~\ref{par:ft_harm}, and one cell of the first small-$D$
round (par.~\ref{par:lowd_ft}), both corrected there. $D=10$ fine-tunes run before and after the fix are not
comparable without this correction (\code{analysis/transfer/ema\_start\_audit.py},
\code{data/ema\_start\_audit.json}).

% claim: W3=0.014
% claim: transfer_d10.searches=327
% allow: 10 1 because they are the constants of the average's warm-up decay and the design's smallest D, settings
% allow: 10^{1.5} 176 7 because they are a design D, the D=10 cell's update count and the first validation update at D=10^{1.5}, settings
% allow: 3 because it bounds the updates read, a setting

\paragraph{Why a fine-tune loses to scratch at small $D$ (2026-10-26).}\label{par:ft_harm} Whether the fine-tunes that
end above scratch, in the grid (par.~\ref{par:nogain}) and in the two rounds above, start from a pretrained network that
already holds less than scratch reaches, or start better and lose it. A reported value is its curve's minimum, so a
losing curve never dips below scratch at any validation; what the start holds is read off the frozen trunk instead, as
the ridge read-out of par.~\ref{par:lowd_ft}, which is exactly the step-$0$ validation of linear probe then fine-tune.
(The other arms start from the pretraining's own head, far worse than the read-out on these targets; the read-out is
what the trunk holds.) With $S$ scratch's best, $R$ the read-out and $F$ the fine-tune's best, a losing cell is (i) a
bad start, $R\ge S$ and $F\le R$: fine-tuning improves on the start, not enough; (ii) a good start undone, $R<S<F$; or
(iii) other: $R\ge S$ and $F>R$, the fine-tune ends worse than a linear head on its own frozen trunk, or the cell has
no read-out. The sources are the grid's fine-tunes at $D=10$ to $10^{2.5}$ (each cell's best trial), the first round's
$240$ runs (their validation curves copied home from CC, \code{data/lowd\_ft\_curves.json}) and each method's best
trial of the DyHPO round; a cell counts once per source and method, $224$ losing rows over $30$ of the $60$ cells of
the two rounds. They split $100$ (i), $4$ (ii), $45$ (iii, worse than the read-out) and $75$ (iii, grid cells on rungs
and probes no read-out was run for). Over the $149$ rows with a read-out, the read-out sits a median $+1.09$ decades
above scratch and the fine-tune $-0.36$ decades below the read-out: the trunk holds a fit about ten times worse than
scratch reaches, and fine-tuning closes about a third of that gap. The probes differ. On $ud\to ud$ ($41$ losing rows,
$38$ of them (i)) and $u\bar u\to gg$ ($16$, $14$ (i)) the start is far worse, $+1.77$ and $+1.31$ decades over scratch,
and fine-tuning gains much on it, $-1.00$ and $-0.60$ decades below the read-out, without getting there. On $ee\to
Z\gamma$ ($92$ losing rows: $48$ (i), $2$ (ii), $42$ (iii)) the start is nearer, $+0.80$ decades, but fine-tuning gains
nothing on it: the median fine-tune ends at the read-out ($-0.03$ decades), and in about half of its losing rows above
it. What the first steps do to a fitted head shows in linear probe then fine-tune, which starts at the read-out: its
first validation is above it in $78$ of the $102$ runs the random initialisation's share does not reach (median
$1.09\times$). The other $18$, its $D=10$ runs with EMA on, are contaminated by that share at their first validation
(par.~\ref{par:ema_start}) and are not counted: all $18$ sit above the read-out there, by a median $4.9\times$, and
Fig.~\ref{fig:ft_harm}b still shows them. Its best is below the read-out in all but $10$ of the $120$.
The losing curves reach their minimum later in the run than the winning ones (median at $0.55$ of the run against
$0.33$), a fine-tune still improving slowly, not one overfitting after an early best. So fine-tuning seldom destroys
a start that beats scratch ($4$ rows); the losses are a start the pretraining did not prepare for these probes at these
$D$, and on $ee\to Z\gamma$ a fine-tune that does not move off that start (Fig.~\ref{fig:ft_harm};
\code{analysis/transfer/ft\_harm.py}, \code{data/ft\_harm.json}).
% par:ft_harm, the read-out's limit
This read-out has a limit that bounds what it says. The starts are the transfer study's pretrainings, trained on
catalogs much smaller and less diverse than the current one and before the later work on the target and its
preprocessing, and a linear head on their frozen trunk measures little more than how well each of them zero-shots the
probe. That a start is bad here says how far those old pretrainings were from these probes, not how far a current
pretraining would be, so the test that blends the read-out into the fine-tune's start is not run. Whether fine-tuning a
current pretraining beats scratch at small $D$ moved to the Muon test's fine-tunes, with sixteen scratch runs added
on the same four probes at the same four $D$ so each fine-tune has its scratch, and is answered there
(par.~\ref{par:v3_muon}); catalog\_v4's pretraining, once it exists, repeats it.
% par:ft_harm: class counts redone after the ee->Z gamma read-outs lost their doubled variance factor (code review)
% src: python3 analysis/transfer/ft_harm.py @3ca721f3
% claim: curves_first_round=240
% claim: losing=224
% claim: losing_cells=30
% claim: cls.i=100
% claim: cls.ii=4
% claim: cls.iii-worse=45
% claim: cls.iii-noreadout=75
% claim: with_readout=149
% claim: readout_over_scratch=1.09
% claim: ft_over_readout=-0.36
% claim: probe.ud_ud.losing=41
% claim: probe.ud_ud.i=38
% claim: probe.uubar_gg.losing=16

\begin{figure}[t]

\centering
\widerow{\includegraphics{../analysis/transfer/figs/ft_harm.pdf}}
\caption{Why a fine-tune loses to scratch at small $D$. (a) Per row (grid, first round, each method's own search; cells

start is worse than scratch, above the dashed diagonal it ends worse than its own read-out. (b) Linear probe then

(\code{analysis/transfer/ft\_harm.py}).}

\paragraph{Distance from the pretraining.} How far a probe is from what a pretraining already knows, measured before
any fine-tune step: the parent's output $h$ on the probe's validation split, against the probe's own standardized target
$z=(\log|\mathcal M|^2-\mu_q)/\sigma_q$ (\code{tools/zero\_shot\_ft.py}). The parent outputs in its own standardized units,
so $h$ is compared with $z$ up to an affine map,
\begin{equation}
  L_0^*=\min_{a,b}\,\big\langle(a+b\,h-z)^2\big\rangle=1-\rho(h,z)^2 ,
\end{equation}
with $\rho$ the correlation over the validation events: $0$ when the frozen network predicts the probe's
$\log|\mathcal M|^2$ exactly up to its offset and scale, $1$ when its output carries no linear information about it. The
plain mean-squared error of $h$ against $z$ is not used: a parent with the right shape but an offset $\alpha$ and scale
$\beta$ of its own scores $((\mu_q-\alpha)/\beta)^2+(\sigma_q/\beta-1)^2$, not $0$. No probe, nor any process in its
equality class, is in any of these pretrainings (the recipes' headers, \code{recipes/transfer\_ladder\_r<n>.yaml}), so
these are zero-shot predictions. Fig.~\ref{fig:zero_shot_gain} plots $L_0^*$ for the twelve probes and ten pretrainings
against the fine-tune's gain of Fig.~\ref{fig:gain_heatmap} (\code{zero\_shot\_gain.py}, \code{zero\_shot.json}, which also
holds the six star-arm probes of rung $1$ and of $ee\to u\bar u$). Over all $120$ points the two are anti-correlated
(Spearman $-0.37$, $p=4\times10^{-5}$): a pretraining whose frozen output already tracks the probe's amplitude gains
more from fine-tuning. Within a probe, across the ten pretrainings, the rank correlation is negative for ten of the
twelve, strongest on $u\bar u\to gg$ ($-0.85$), $ud\to ud$ and the one-loop $ee\to b\bar b$ ($-0.73$ each). The two
exceptions are $ee\to\nu_e\bar\nu_e$ ($+0.14$), where every pretraining sits at $L_0^*\geq0.43$, and $ee\to t\bar t$
($+0.52$), where $L_0^*$ is already $0.027$ from $ee\to u\bar u$ alone and the gain ($-0.1$ to $0.6$ decades) does
not follow it. $L_0^*$ ranks pretrainings for one probe; across probes it does not set the gain's scale, since scratch's own
difficulty differs from probe to probe.
\begin{figure}[H]
\centering
\includegraphics{../analysis/transfer/figs/zero_shot_gain_legend.pdf}\\
\includegraphics{../analysis/transfer/figs/zero_shot_gain.pdf}
\caption{Each pretraining's zero-shot distance to each probe up to an affine map, $L_0^*=1-\rho(h,z)^2$ ($h$ the frozen
parent's output, $z$ the probe's standardized $\log|\mathcal M|^2$), against the fine-tune's gain over scratch of
Fig.~\ref{fig:gain_heatmap}; one point per pretraining and probe, $8$k grid.}
\label{fig:zero_shot_gain}
\end{figure}

\paragraph{A $32$k horizon at $D=10^3$: no clear gain from one run.} One run per probe at its cell's best $8$k HPs, $32$k
steps (\code{job\_transfer\_calib.sh}, Jean Zay, 2026-10-04): against the $8$k cell, $ee\to t\bar t$ gains $4.0\times$,
$ud\to ud$ $1.5\times$, $ee\to Z\gamma$ $1.2\times$; $ee\to d\bar d$, the one-loop probes, $u\bar u\to Zgg$, $Zggg$ and
$ee\to\nu_e\bar\nu_e$ move by $\le1.2\times$ or rise ($0.3$--$1.0\times$); several best checkpoints sit early
($4800$--$12480$ of $32$k steps: over-training at $D=10^3$), and $u\bar u\to gg$ diverged at step $28224$ (its best before
that not read). One run against a best-of-eight search is biased toward the $8$k value, so the $8$k cells stand at
$D=10^3$.

\paragraph{The $32$k chosen points, and what the held items gave before the hold.} Every $32$k fine-tune cell runs two
fixed points (\code{horizon32k\_chosen.json}; \code{sweep/pick\_points.py} takes the candidate of the sweep's own pool
nearest each, through \code{generate\_sweep --extend --fixed-hp}): lr $1.2$ and $1.8\times10^{-3}$ with
$\lambda\approx3\times10^{-10}$, where the $32$k fine-tunes already done had their bests; scratch lr $0.7$ and
$1.4\times10^{-3}$ with $\lambda\approx2\times10^{-7}$, the $8$k scratch optimum moved to $32$k by $t^{-0.55}$. Rung $1$
and the redesigned arms run lr $1.3$ and $2.2\times10^{-3}$ on $u\bar d\to e^+\nu_e$, $ee\to d\bar d$ and its EW one loop.
A cell is scored on its two points only (\code{cells.best\_chosen}): the first $32$k round's sweeps also hold one or two
random start-up trials, which would favour its pretrainings. The points were chosen from the data they are compared
with, so the $32$k comparisons are preliminary. Scratch on $ee\to\nu_e\bar\nu_e$ was worse at $32$k at both points
($9\times10^{-4}$ against $7\times10^{-5}$ at $8$k, $D=10^4$): it keeps $8$k at $10^{3.5}$ and takes its whole $32$k search
at $10^4$ (\code{cells.HOLD\_8K}, \code{ALL\_TRIALS32}); $ud\to ud$ at $10^{2.5}$, $10^3$ is at $32$k for every pretraining.
Before the horizon item was held (2026-10-06), the first round gave a median $32$k$/8$k ratio of $0.27$ over $269$ cells,
$49$ of them worse at $32$k; how to score those is open with the held item. The $32$k HP landscape at $D=10^{3.5}$
(\code{tp3\_hpx32k\_*}, held with it) had one divergence: rung $0$ on the one-loop $ee\to d\bar d$, hp$180$ (lr
$1.5\times10^{-3}$, $\lambda=2\times10^{-6}$, EMA), non-finite at step $22\,491$ of $32$k; it is shown separately when the
landscape is written up. The equal-total-data rungs (held 2026-10-07; \code{analysis/transfer/eqd\_vs\_ladder.py}, the
$147$ cells of rungs $2$, $7$, $8$ that finished): the searches' best lr does not move (median
$\log_{10}(\mathrm{lr}_{eq}/\mathrm{lr}_{lad})=+0.02$ on rungs $2$, $7$), the landscape is flat across the window (second-best
trial $1.16\times$ the best, median), and the equal-data trial nearest the ladder's best point is $1.22\times$ the
equal-data best, so one run at the ladder's point would have done. Rung $7$'s equal-data cells are $1.31\times$ worse at
the median (Wilcoxon $p=0.008$, $83$ cells); over all $147$, $1.11\times$, $p=0.11$; no dependence on $D$
(Kruskal--Wallis $p=0.08$ on rung $7$, $0.14$ on all); single cells are noise-dominated (ratio $0.28$ to $5.7$, 5 to
95\%). The equal-data parents are hp73 alone, the ladder's the best of each rung's search (hp47 on rungs $1$, $5$, $8$,
hp185 on $3$, hp15 on $4$). One seed.

\paragraph{The star arms' four new probes, and the arms.} The new probes (Fig.~\ref{fig:rung_grid_new}) fine-tuned from rung
$1$ and from the $64$k $ee\to u\bar u$: on $ee\to d\bar d$ at one loop above $500$\,GeV both are $30$--$250\times$ below scratch at
$D\le10^{2.5}$ and $1.5$--$4\times$ above (the QCD-virtual target at $\mu=\sqrt s$ is close to the Born, which both pretrained
on); on $ee\to t\bar t$ at one loop near threshold both are $2.5$--$20\times$ \emph{worse} than scratch at $D\le10^{2.5}$, and rung $1$
is $1.6$--$3.6\times$ better from $10^3$ up. Each arm against rung $1$ on its own probe (Fig.~\ref{fig:star_arms},
\code{star\_arms.py}; which arms test their structure: the design paragraph below): soft and collinear emission ($ee\to d\bar dg$) $3$--$8\times$
lower at every $D$; initial-state collinear ($u\bar u\to Zg$) $2$--$7\times$; the $t\bar t$ threshold $2$--$4\times$ from $10^3$ up,
still above scratch at small $D$; the dead cone ($ee\to t\bar tg$) level, $2\times$ worse at $10^4$; the resonance and Sudakov
controls level, the Sudakov one up to $20\times$ worse at $D=10$. On $ee\to d\bar d$ every arm stays with rung $1$, within
$4\times$ at $10^4$: added structure costs the near probe little.
\begin{figure}[H]
\centering

\caption{The star arms' four probes, from scratch and fine-tuned from rung $1$ and the $64$k $ee\to u\bar u$.}
\label{fig:rung_grid_new}
\end{figure}

\widerow{\pnl{../analysis/transfer/figs/star_arms_a.pdf}\hfill\pnl{../analysis/transfer/figs/star_arms_b.pdf}} \\[1ex]
\widerow{\pnl{../analysis/transfer/figs/star_arms_c.pdf}\hfill\pnl{../analysis/transfer/figs/star_arms_d.pdf}} \\[1ex]

\pnl{../analysis/transfer/figs/star_arms_g.pdf}
\caption{The star arms as finally designed (the first resonance and Sudakov arms, which tested neither structure, are
not drawn): (a)--(f) each arm (rung $1$ plus one structure) on its own probe, against rung $1$ and scratch: soft and
collinear, initial-state collinear, dead cone, $t\bar t$ threshold, $W$ pole, electroweak Sudakov; in (e), (f) the arm
and rung $1$ at the same two chosen points per cell. (g) $ee\to d\bar d$ fine-tuned from every arm; rung $1$ solid for its
search, dashed at the two chosen points that the $W$-pole and electroweak-Sudakov arms ran.}
\label{fig:star_arms}
\end{figure}

\paragraph{The star arms' design, and the two that tested nothing.} Six arms (the user approved them, 2026-10-02), each rung $1$ plus one
structure, not cumulative (\code{recipes/transfer\_star\_<arm>.yaml}), pretrained at $64$k steps on hp47 and hp73, the
pretraining protocol of 2026-10-02 to 10-04 (hp73 alone since, Fig.~\ref{fig:pretrain_hp}); each arm also holds the
start-up trials that had run before the arms were cut to it (hp15, hp30, hp95) and a few leftover guided ones
(\code{tp3\_star\_<arm>}, $6$--$7.7$\,h a trial on lxplus's V100), then
fine-tuned with the grid's protocol on the arm's probe and on $ee\to d\bar d$ (\code{tp3\_s<arm>fte}; all cells in,
\code{analysis/transfer/star\_arms.py}). Four arms test the structure they name: soft and collinear emission
($+ee\to u\bar ug$, $u\bar ugg$; probe $ee\to d\bar dg$, the same topology with $d$ for $u$); initial-state collinear
($+u\bar d\to Wg$; probe $u\bar u\to Zg$); the $t\bar t$ threshold ($+u\bar u\to t\bar t$ tree and one loop at
$\sqrt s=363$--$450$; probe $ee\to t\bar t$ at one loop there: the Coulomb $1/\beta$ is a QCD one-loop term, so the
QCD-virtual target carries it); the massive quasi-collinear limit ($+ee\to b\bar bg$, $b\bar bgg$; probe $ee\to t\bar tg$,
$c$ being massless in the generator), weakly: $m_b/\sqrt s\le0.2$ against a strongly massive top. Two do not.
\emph{Narrow resonances} ($+ee\to ZZ$, $ZH$; probe $ee\to\nu_e\bar\nu_e$): the probe's resonance, the $s$-channel $Z$
pole, is already in rung $1$ ($ee\to\nu_\mu\bar\nu_\mu$, only the generation feature tells the two neutrinos apart), what
the probe adds is the $t$-channel $W$, and $ZZ$, $ZH$ (both also in rung $5$) put on-shell bosons in the final state,
which the probe has none of. \emph{Sudakov logarithms} ($+u\bar u\to d\bar d$, $ZZ$ at one loop, $\sqrt s\ge500$, both
rung-$9$ processes; probe $ee\to d\bar d$ at one loop there): every target is QCD-virtual at $\mu=\sqrt s$, so none
has the electroweak $\log^2(s/m_W^2)$; the probe is the QCD vertex correction to $\gamma^*/Z\to d\bar d$, which for
massless quarks at $\mu=\sqrt s$ is the Born times a constant (MadLoop, \code{tools/ew\_sudakov\_check.py}: finite over
Born $=0.0468$ at every point from $100$\,GeV to $3$\,TeV and three angles, constant to $10^{-16}$; so is the ladder's
one-loop probe $ee\to d\bar d$, the same target over $25$--$1000$\,GeV: its pool is the tree pool times a constant),
and the arm's processes carry boxes and initial-state vertices it lacks. Both arms' results stand as controls (unrelated
structure added to rung $1$), not as tests of their names. 

\paragraph{The redesign: the $W$ pole, and electroweak one-loop targets that carry the Sudakov logarithm.} The user's
go-ahead, 2026-10-04: a resonance arm on the $W$ pole, which no
rung before $6$ has (\code{transfer\_star\_wpole.yaml}: rung $1$ $+u\bar d\to e^+\nu_eg$, $+u\bar d\to\tau^+\nu_\tau$, a new
generator entry, $\tau$ massive; probe $u\bar d\to e^+\nu_e$, \code{udbar\_enu}, in no equality class), and a Sudakov arm
on electroweak one-loop targets (\code{transfer\_star\_sudakovew.yaml}: rung $1$ $+ee\to\mu\mu$, $u\bar u$ at one loop,
electroweak, $\sqrt s=500$--$1000$; probe $ee\to d\bar d$ there, \code{ee\_dd\_ew\_nlo}). The one-loop pipeline now
generates electroweak virtuals (\code{[virt=QED]}, \code{loop\_qcd\_qed\_sm}; the target is the finite part over
$\alpha/2\pi$ at $\mu^2=s$; the per-event guard and the certification check the universal QED double pole
$-\sum_iQ_i^2$ over the massless charged legs, met to $10^{-13}$ on all three). They carry the logarithm: for
$ee\to d\bar d$ at $90^\circ$ the finite part over the Born goes from $-6\%$ at $200$\,GeV to $-29\%$ at $3$\,TeV, a
fit in $L=\ln(s/m_W^2)$ giving $-3.7\times10^{-3}L^2$ ($-2.8$, $-4.7\times10^{-3}$ at $\cos\theta=\pm0.5$;
\code{ew\_sudakov\_check.json}); the pools change sign (finite part negative on $65$--$92\%$ of the events). Both arms
pretrained on hp73 ($64$k steps, lxplus) and fine-tuned on their probe and on $ee\to d\bar d$ at the two chosen points per
cell (Fig.~\ref{fig:star_arms} (e)--(g), against rung $1$ at the same points): the $W$-pole arm is $17\times$ below rung $1$ on
$u\bar d\to e^+\nu_e$, the electroweak Sudakov arm $1.1\times$ on the electroweak $ee\to d\bar d$, where rung $1$ is itself no
better than scratch at any $D$, as expected of a structure no pretraining had.

\paragraph{The finale and the synthetic pretraining on the $8$k grid.} Both fine-tuned on the twelve ladder probes,
the finale also on the six star-arm probes, $7$ values of $D$ each (\code{analysis/transfer/finale\_synth.py},
\code{finale\_synth\_scaling.py}; per cell MSE of $\log|\mathcal M|^2$ at the best checkpoint, the probe's
\code{val\_loss\_no\_reg} times its $\sigma_p^2$; Figs.~\ref{fig:finale_synth} and \ref{fig:finale_synth_scaling}).
A cell is the best of its $5$-trial search where that finished ($122$ cells), else one run at the reference cell's best
point ($88$ cells, rung $9$'s or the star arm's; the user's call, 2026-10-07), so the two kinds of cell are measured
differently; a search that ran twice counts its first $5$ trials. The tests are paired, cell against the reference cell
of the same probe and $D$; since the $7$ cells of a probe are not independent, the test that counts is the one over
probes (each probe's median difference, Wilcoxon), with the per-cell figure alongside as descriptive.
\emph{Finale against rung $9$:} no overall difference (median over probes $-0.01$\,dex, better on $6$ of $12$,
$p=0.91$; over cells $+0.02$, $p=0.80$), but by probe: better on all $7$ cells of $u\bar u\to Zgg$ and $u\bar u\to Zggg$
(median $+0.43$, $+0.56$\,dex) and $6/7$ of $u\bar u\to Zg$ and $ee\to Z\gamma$; worse on all $7$ of $ee\to d\bar d$
($-0.90$) and $u\bar u\to gg$ ($-0.32$); sign tests $p=0.016$ each, not significant once the twelve probes are
counted. A possible source of the $Z$+jets gain, untested: the finale adds $ee\to u\bar ug$, $u\bar ugg$, which by
crossing are $q\bar q\to\gamma^*/Z$ plus gluons, absent from the ladder. \emph{Finale against each star arm on its own
probe:} level (median over the $6$ probes $-0.03$\,dex, better on $2$, $p=0.84$; over $42$ cells $-0.02$, $p=0.55$).
\emph{Synthetic against rung $9$ and scratch:} the synthetic pretraining was set up as a cheap substitute for real
data (no matrix-element code, no compile; Sec.~\ref{sec:synthetic}), not as an improvement, so the question is how much
of rung $9$'s transfer it keeps. It beats scratch in $66$ of $84$ cells and stays within a factor $1.7$ of rung $9$ at
the median (median over probes $-0.23$\,dex, better than rung $9$ on $3$ of $12$, $p=0.034$; over $84$ cells $-0.24$,
better in $33\%$). The gap is largest on $ee\to d\bar d$ and the one-loop $ee\to d\bar d$ ($0/7$ cells each), the probes
closest to rung $9$'s own processes; it is level on $ee\to Z\gamma$, $ee\to W^+W^-$ and $u\bar u\to Zgg$.

\emph{Synthetic at $32$k (complete, 2026-10-11 03:30; \code{synth32k} $96/96$, collected from CC and lxplus;
\code{analysis/transfer/synth32k.py}, Fig.~\ref{fig:synth32k}).} All $48$ cells: the twelve probes at $D=10^{3.5}$ to
$10^5$, $u d\to u d$ also at $10^{2.5}$, $10^3$; each the best of its two chosen points (MSE of $\log|\mathcal M|^2$ at the
best checkpoint), as is every reference below. These are fixed points, no search, so the DyHPO fix (sec:lrlaw) does not
touch them. \emph{Against scratch at $32$k} ($46$ cells): median $+0.26$\,dex, better in $30$ (Wilcoxon $p=0.007$); over
probes median $+0.29$, better on $8$ of $12$ ($p=0.034$), from $-0.16$ ($ee\to d\bar d$) to $+0.92$ ($ud\to ud$). By $D$:
$+0.22$ at $10^{3.5}$ (better in $10/12$), $+0.06$ at $10^4$ ($6/12$), $-0.13$ at $10^{4.5}$ ($5/12$), $+0.39$ at $10^5$
($9/10$). The gain does not grow steadily with $D$ as the partial count suggested ($+1.09$ at $10^5$ on two cells); it
dips at $10^{4.5}$ and recovers at $10^5$. The extremes are $-0.85$ ($ee\to\nu_e\bar\nu_e$, $10^4$, where both chosen
points spiked, below) and $+1.44$ ($ud\to ud$, $10^5$). Scratch at $ud\to ud$, $10^{2.5}$ and $10^3$ ran two other points
than its chosen ones and is left out of the tests; against the best of those the synthetic is $+0.32$ and $+0.88$\,dex.
\emph{Against rung $9$ at $32$k} ($29$ cells: all twelve probes at $10^{3.5}$, $10^4$; $ud\to ud$ at $10^{2.5}$, $10^3$;
$ee\to d\bar d$ at $10^{4.5}$, $10^5$ and the one-loop $d\bar d$ at $10^{4.5}$, the cells of the held horizon item that ran):
median $-0.63$\,dex, better in $5$ ($p=1.4\times10^{-5}$); over probes $-0.55$, better on $2$ of $12$ (the one-loop $b\bar b$,
$+0.21$; $ee\to W^+W^-$, $+0.08$; $p=0.005$); worst $u\bar u\to gg$ and $ee\to d\bar d$ ($-1.16$, $-1.14$). By $D$: $-0.41$
at $10^{3.5}$ ($1/12$), $-0.72$ at $10^4$ ($3/12$). So at $32$k the synthetic pretraining keeps less of rung $9$'s
transfer than at $8$k ($-0.24$ over probes, $p=0.034$): a factor $\sim\!4$ at the median, against $1.7$.
\emph{Unstable trials}, each still entering with its best checkpoint: \code{tp3\_synfte32k\_ee\_WW\_d9} hp$44$ blew up at
step $25\,855$ (best $1.3\times10^{-5}$, the cell's value); $13$ more of the $96$ trials have their validation loss jump
more than $100\times$ above their best after it and recover only partly (on the one-loop $b\bar b$ at $10^4$, $10^{4.5}$,
the one-loop $d\bar d$ at $10^{3.5}$, $10^4$, $ee\to\nu_e\bar\nu_e$ at $10^4$, $ee\to t\bar t$ at $10^{3.5}$, $ud\to ud$ at
$10^{2.5}$, $u\bar u\to Zgg$ at $10^{3.5}$, $10^4$), with the jumps between steps $1\,920$ and $11\,840$; four cells take
their value from such a trial ($ee\to\nu_e\bar\nu_e$ $10^4$, one-loop $d\bar d$ $10^{3.5}$, $ud\to ud$ $10^{2.5}$,
$u\bar u\to Zgg$ $10^{3.5}$). One seed.

\begin{figure}[H]
\centering
\widerow{\pnl{../analysis/transfer/figs/synth32k_a.pdf}\hfill\pnl{../analysis/transfer/figs/synth32k_b.pdf}}
\caption{The synthetic pretraining fine-tuned at $32$k, per cell $\log_{10}(L_\text{reference}/L_\text{synthetic})$
against scratch at $32$k (a) and rung $9$ at $32$k where it ran (b), every cell the best of its two chosen points; the
median is drawn where a value of $D$ has at least three cells. Above zero the synthetic pretraining is better.}
\label{fig:synth32k}
\end{figure}

\emph{High multiplicity, first cells (2026-10-09 12:40; D20 to D24).} At $D=10^4$, $32$k: the finale
fine-tuned on $u\bar u\to Z+5g$ ($5$ of $5$ trials) reaches $2.7\times10^{-3}$ (MSE of $\log|\mathcal M|^2$ at the best
checkpoint), on $Z+4g$ $4.4\times10^{-3}$ ($3$ of $5$); scratch so far $4.9\times10^{-3}$ on $Z+5g$ ($2$ of $8$) and
$7.0\times10^{-3}$ on $Z+4g$ ($1$ of $8$), no comparison before both searches are in. For scale, the same cell on $Z+3g$:
finale $1.6\times10^{-3}$, scratch $2.9\times10^{-3}$; on $Z+2g$ $9.4\times10^{-5}$. So the losses do not grow from $2\to4$
to $2\to6$ the way they did from $2\to3$ to $2\to4$. Four of the eight fine-tune trials have their best checkpoint before
step $3\,000$, all at $\mathrm{lr}\ge4\times10^{-3}$: their validation loss jumps by one to three orders after it (one $Z+5g$
trial to $18$ near step $9\,600$) and recovers only partly; the trials at $\mathrm{lr}\le4\times10^{-3}$ train smoothly
to the end. The $1$--$10\times10^{-3}$ fine-tune window is too high at the top for these probes. The add-$W$ steps
($16$k; validation \code{val\_loss\_no\_reg} on $W+4g$, $W+5g$, preprocessed units, 13:30): EWC complete, best
$4.85\times10^{-5}$ of $5$ (the two trials at $\mathrm{lr}\ge7.9\times10^{-3}$ at $2.7$ and $4.3\times10^{-4}$), naive
$4.5\times10^{-5}$ ($2$ of $5$); replay ($2$ of $5$) is scored on all $30$ processes, not comparable here.

\emph{High multiplicity, the full grid (complete, \code{highmult} $254/254$, collected from CC and lxplus 2026-10-11 09:40; \code{analysis/transfer/highmult.py}).} Each cell: scratch the best of its $8$-trial search (the first $8$ by observation order: the $D=10^5$ searches ran $15$ on $Z+4g$ and $13$ on $Z+5g$, refilled for trials that had finished on the other site by \code{sweep/refill.py}, which counted one site's results, fixed 2026-10-11, and the split searches at $10^{3.5}$ and $10^{4.5}$ ran $9$; only $Z+5g$ at $10^5$ changes, $5.95\times10^{-4}$ against $5.54\times10^{-4}$ over all $13$), each pretraining the best of its two fixed points (D25); MSE of $\log|\mathcal M|^2$ at the best checkpoint; $8$k steps up to $D=10^3$, $32$k from $10^{3.5}$ (Fig.~\ref{fig:highmult}). Gains over scratch, in dex:

$1.5$ & $+0.80$ & $+1.11$ & $+1.20$ & $+1.29$ & $+1.56$ & $+1.22$ \\

Every pretraining gains about one decade over scratch at $D\le10^{1.5}$ on both probes, and the gain falls with $D$ to
nothing at $D\approx10^4$; past it no pretraining is clearly better than scratch ($-0.39$ to $+0.19$\,dex, within the
spread of one seed). Scratch at $D=10^1$ sits at $2.3$ and $2.9$ (no fit at all). Adding $W+4g$, $W+5g$ to the
pretraining is the best arm at every $D\le10^{3.5}$ but $Z+4g$ at $10^{1.5}$ (where the synthetic arm is $0.09$\,dex
higher): $0.18$--$0.54$\,dex over the finale on both probes ($Z+4g$ at $10^{2.5}$: $6.8\times10^{-3}$ against the finale's $2.3\times10^{-2}$), so processes of the probe's own
multiplicity in a neighbouring family help; at $D\ge10^{4.5}$ it is the worst. The synthetic $2\to5$, $2\to6$ processes
are close to the finale at low $D$ ($-0.07$ to $+0.21$\,dex over it at $D\le10^{3.5}$, $+0.40$ on $Z+4g$ at $10^{1.5}$) and the best arm at
$D=10^5$ on both probes, by $0.1$--$0.2$\,dex. Neither the finale nor the (version-$1$) synthetic arm saw a $2\to5$ or $2\to6$
process of the probes' kind, and both still give a decade where data is scarce. The $D=10^4$ row is the children
table's (below). $Z+5g$ scratch trials hp 30 and 15 of $D=10^{2.5}$ trained on lxplus's regenerated train pool (2026-10-10
17:09--18:00: a trial that started before the pools were copied from CC rebuilt it, same \code{recipe\_id},
different bytes, the generator not being byte-reproducible across sites; CC's copy restored at 18:00, md5
\code{dc03352472ea} on both, the $Z+4g$ and val/test pools matching); no other lxplus $Z+5g$ trial started in that window, and neither is the cell's value (hp 196,
$9.18\times10^{-2}$, started 20:34). Scratch trial hp 18 of $Z+4g$ at $D=10^4$
(\code{tp3\_hmscr32k\_uubar\_Zgggg\_d8}) diverged; like every trial it enters its search with its best checkpoint, and one
trial was added to keep the search at $8$. The
fixed-point cells use no search; at $D\le10^{3}$ two points of the paper's window against an $8$-trial scratch search.

\emph{Why the gain shrinks with $D$: the horizon test at $D=10^3$ (D38, \code{trendtests}; 2026-10-12 19:10, $9$ of the
item's $12$ runs: scratch $7$ of $8$, the finale $2$ of $2$; the $128$k pair at $D=10^{4.5}$ complete 2026-10-13).} The grid
trains $8$k steps up to $D=10^3$ and $32$k from $10^{3.5}$, and the gain falls fastest across that boundary
($Z+4g$: $+0.48$ to $+0.21$\,dex). The $Z+4g$ cell at $D=10^3$ rerun at $32$k: scratch $3.73\times10^{-2}$ (best of
$7$; $3.94\times10^{-2}$ at $8$k), the finale $1.32\times10^{-2}$ ($1.32\times10^{-2}$ at $8$k), a gain of $+0.45$\,dex
against $+0.48$. The horizon change does not explain the fall: at $D=10^3$ neither arm uses the extra steps (the
finale's best checkpoints at steps $1\,920$ and $2\,560$, scratch's at $3\,520$--$8\,640$, all far before $32$k).
The second test, $Z+4g$ at $D=10^{4.5}$ at $128$k steps instead of $32$k, each arm at its $32$k cell's best point
(scratch hp15, the finale hp162; complete 2026-10-13 11:48, CC): scratch $8.70\times10^{-4}$ ($8.31\times10^{-4}$ at
$32$k, best step $51\,200$), the finale $1.23\times10^{-3}$ ($1.35\times10^{-3}$, best step $34\,560$), a gain of
$-0.15$\,dex against $-0.21$. Four times the steps moves neither arm by more than $10\%$, and both stop improving
near $35$--$50$k steps, so at $10^{4.5}$ events the cell is limited by its data, not its horizon. Neither test finds
a horizon effect; what moved this cell was the finale's fixed points (next paragraph: a search reaches
$9.32\times10^{-4}$). One seed each.

\emph{The fixed points against a search on one cell (D35; complete, $8/8$, CC and lxplus, 2026-10-13 05:30).} The finale
on $Z+4g$ at $D=10^{4.5}$, $32$k, an $8$-trial search over scratch's own space (the search ran split between CC and
lxplus after a rebalance, so the two halves suggested from separate DyHPO states). Best $9.32\times10^{-4}$ (lr
$3.8\times10^{-3}$, $\lambda=7.6\times10^{-7}$, EMA on, still improving at step $32$k), then $1.28$, $1.54$, $1.86\times10^{-3}$;
the cell's two fixed points gave $1.35\times10^{-3}$, scratch's search $8.31\times10^{-4}$. The search recovers
$0.16$ of the $0.21$\,dex the finale trailed scratch by: the finale is $-0.05$\,dex from scratch, inside one seed. So at
this cell the negative gain was mostly the fixed points: the best fine-tune sits at a regularisation three decades
above them ($\lambda\approx3\times10^{-10}$) and a higher lr, outside what the paper's landscape (fit on lower
multiplicities and smaller $D$) chose. The fall of the gain to zero with $D$ stands; that it goes below zero at
$D\ge10^{4.5}$ does not, at least on this cell. One seed.

\emph{Forgetting in the add-$W$ step (complete, \code{contadd} $23/23$, collected from CC and lxplus 2026-10-11 03:40).}
The finale ($28$ processes, hp73) trained on $u\bar d\to W+4g$, $W+5g$ for $16$k steps, three ways, each a $5$-trial
search: alone (naive), with the finale's processes replayed (replay), and with EWC on the finale's own Fisher. Each
trial's best checkpoint scored on the finale's $28$ processes (their validation MSE, preprocessed, on the finale's pools
and normalization; \code{tools/run\_data\_tools.py eval}, runs \code{FA-ccin2p3-4e4e6153} and \code{FA-ccin2p3-3ab918ca}),
against the finale's own ($4.9\times10^{-6}$, geometric mean over the $28$); the $W$ column is each trial's own
validation on $W+4g$, $W+5g$ at its best checkpoint (geometric mean of the two). The evaluation's own $W$ numbers are not
used: a trial trained on $W$ alone fed its $W$ events the off-shellness input as zero (no $Z$, $t$ or $H$ propagator, so
no column varies and \code{fine\_tune.offshell\_stats=parent} fits them there), while the evaluation run applies the
finale's scale, which shifts that input; it reproduces the trials' own $W$ loss only for replay (same scale) and naive
trial 145, and is up to $10^3\times$ off for the others. Three replay trials blew up and are listed below the table, not
in it; naive trial 130 and replay trial 159 ran twice (a requeue), and the first run is the one counted.

\emph{Blown up} (best checkpoint at step $640$ or earlier, validation then up by $10^4$--$10^5\times$, ending $9$--$700\times$ above it), each at its best
checkpoint: replay 21 (step $160$, $W$ $2.0\times10^{-3}$; old$/$finale $32$ in the first evaluation), replay 116
(step $160$, $W$ $2.2\times10^{-3}$), replay 198 (step $640$, $W$ $8.0\times10^{-4}$), all at $\mathrm{lr}\ge2.9\times10^{-3}$;
EWC 2 (in the table) peaked at step $5\,440$, rose $800\times$ and came back to $1.5\times$ its best by the end.
Training on $W$ alone wipes out the finale's processes: naive loses $4$--$5$ orders on the geometric mean, every one of the
$28$ worse by at least $64\times$. EWC with the finale's own Fisher holds back only at a large penalty with a moderate lr
($\lambda=1.4\times10^{5}$, trial 13: $1.8\times10^{3}\times$ worse; $\lambda=339$, trial 108, is as bad as naive) and pays
little on $W$ ($6.7$ against $4.8\times10^{-5}$); the two at $\lambda\ge5\times10^{5}$ ran at $\mathrm{lr}\ge7.9\times10^{-3}$
and are worse than trials 13 and 31 on both counts. Replay keeps the old processes (trial 44: $1.06\times$, $14$ of $28$
worse by more than $10\%$, the worst $u\bar u\to t\bar t$ at NLO, $2.7\times$) at $3\times$ the naive loss on $W$; its four
finished trials sit within $1.5\times$ of each other on $W$. The searches' first three trials (random start-up) and EWC's
five ran before the DyHPO fix (sec:lrlaw); naive's guided trials 162, 57 and replay's 116, 187, 19, 198 after it.
\emph{Parents} (D23): EWC's best on $W$ is trial 108, best on all $30$ trial 13 (unchanged); naive's best on $W$ is now
trial 57 ($4.34\times10^{-5}$, $4\%$ below trial 102, the partial write-up's), its best on the mix is trial 130 ($0.11$ on all $30$, level with 102); replay's best on $W$ and on all $30$ is trial 44
($6.5\times10^{-6}$, then 159 and 19 within $1.7\times$). The second evaluation scored the finale again: $0.97\times$ the first on
the geometric mean, single processes $0.5$--$1.0\times$, the run-to-run spread of the evaluation itself.
Only EWC's two parents had children at first; naive's and replay's were never launched, a gap in D23's scope found at
this write-up. They run since 2026-10-11 04:10 on CC (\code{tp3\_wadd\_naivewfte32k\_*} from naive 57,
\code{tp3\_wadd\_replaywfte32k\_*} from replay 44, which is also replay's best on all $30$; \code{tp3\_wadd\_naivemixfte32k\_*}
from naive 130), same probes, $D$ and fixed points.

Which parent gives the best child is not decided by how much it forgot. The two EWC parents first suggested so
(EWC 108, $1.5\times10^{5}\times$ worse on the old processes, $3.9\times$ and $2.5\times$ below the finale's own cell; EWC 13,
$1.8\times10^{3}\times$, $1.8\times$ and $1.3\times$), but naive's two parents, which forgot about equally ($8.9\times10^{4}$
and $4.1\times10^{4}\times$) and fit $W$ equally ($4.3$, $4.6\times10^{-5}$), give children $2.2\times$ and $1.7\times$
apart (57 level with the finale, 130 $2.6\times$ and $1.7\times$ below it). Replay's parent, which kept the old processes
($1.06\times$), gives a child level with the finale ($1.26\times$, $0.97\times$). So among parents of equal $W$ loss and
equal forgetting the children spread by $\sim\!2$--$3\times$ on one seed; the best two (EWC 108, naive 130) are the only
arms at least $0.3$\,dex above scratch at this $D$, and continued training on $W$ beats adding $W+4g$, $W+5g$ to the
pretraining from the start (finale$+W$) only for some parents ($2.3\times$ and $2.9\times$ for EWC 108, level for naive 57
and replay 44). Keeping the old processes buys nothing for the $Z+ng$ child; which of the forgetting parents does best
looks like parent-to-parent variance, which needs seeds of the parents, not more methods, to settle. The finale's cell is now its two fixed points
(hp 97, 37 on $Z+4g$; 31, 107 on $Z+5g$); the $2.72\times10^{-3}$ quoted before was the best of a leftover search, outside
the protocol. Every fixed-point fine-tune from EWC's parents and from finale$+W$ has its best
checkpoint at step $1\,280$--$2\,880$ of $32\,000$ (naive 130's at $1\,920$--$3\,520$; naive 57's and replay 44's at $1\,920$--$13\,440$, half of them past
$8\,000$); after it the validation rises $4$--$230\times$ (EWC 108's $Z+4g$
children $140$ and $230\times$, the only ones past $100\times$) and ends $1$--$5\times$ above its best: these cells are the
first $3$k steps, and a lower lr could do better. The finale's and the synthetic cells' best points mostly peak later (step $3\,840$--$17\,600$; the finale's hp 37 on
$Z+4g$ at $2\,880$). One seed, two fixed points per cell, one value of $D$.

\emph{Forgetting along the add-$W$ run (complete, 2026-10-11 04:30, \code{addhoriz} $105/105$; D30, D31; collected from CC and
lxplus).} The finale fine-tuned on $W+4g$, $W+5g$ for $1$k, $2$k and $4$k steps by seven methods, each a $5$-trial search
(\code{tp3\_waddm\_*}; \code{analysis/transfer/waddm\_tradeoff.py}, Fig.~\ref{fig:waddm_tradeoff}): naive, EWC with the
finale's Fisher ($\lambda\in[10^2,10^7]$), LoRA, frozen lower blocks ($2$, $4$ or $6$ of $8$), layer-wise lr decay
($[0.75,1]$), and replay buffers of $1\%$ and $10\%$ of each old process's train pool. The finale's $28$ processes are
monitors: scored at every validation ($100$ per run) on the finale's pools, never trained on (except through a buffer),
outside the aggregate that selects the checkpoint. $W$ is the run's \code{val\_loss\_no\_reg} (geometric mean over
$W+4g$, $W+5g$, preprocessed); forgetting is the monitors' geometric-mean validation MSE over the finale's own on the same
pools ($4.90\times10^{-6}$, \code{FA-ccin2p3-4e4e6153}). Three searches ran more than $5$ trials (naive $1$k and $4$k, LoRA $1$k: $7$ distinct
points each; four pending trials re-ran under the same HPs after a move between sites and gave the same curves, counted
once); all are used. All trials but the first five of naive $1$k and LoRA $1$k ran after the DyHPO fix (4606315).

The old processes go at once: at the first validation ($10$, $20$ or $40$ steps, inside the warm-up) every run is $50$ to
$2\,200\times$ worse on them, the buffers included (they recover later), except LoRA at its lowest lr ($5$--$7\times$). Without a buffer, no point
of any curve keeps the forgetting under $100\times$ once $W$ is below $10^{-2}$; the front of naive, EWC, frozen blocks
and lr decay sits at $200$--$300\times$ over $W$ from $10^{-2}$ to $10^{-3}$ and climbs to $10^{3}$--$4\times10^{4}$ as
$W$ reaches $1$--$3\times10^{-4}$, so their best-on-all-$30$ checkpoint is the first or second validation, a model barely
trained on $W$. A shorter run does not buy retention: it only stops earlier on the same front, at a higher $W$ ($1$k
$\approx2.9\times10^{-4}$, $4$k $\approx1.2\times10^{-4}$; the $16$k naive step reached $4.5\times10^{-5}$). Near the best
$W$ the methods differ by about one order: lr decay at $4$k ($1.5\times10^{-4}$, $1.5\times10^{3}\times$), LoRA and EWC at
$1$k ($\approx10^{3}\times$) against naive's $2$--$4\times10^{4}\times$. Only replay changes the picture: $1\%$ of the old
pools holds the forgetting at $10$--$30\times$ at $W=3$--$5\times10^{-4}$, $10\%$ at $1.8$--$5\times$ with $W=2.9$--$5\times10^{-4}$ (best on both at $2$k: $1.8\times$, $W=4.2\times10^{-4}$),
against the full replay's $1.06\times$ at $1.5\times10^{-4}$ in the $16$k step above (whose old-$28$ numbers come from a
separate evaluation, not the in-run monitors). One seed per point.

\emph{Children of the replay parents (complete, \code{replaykids} $24/24$, collected from CC 2026-10-12 19:40; D34, D37).}
Only the two buffer methods' parents, each its best checkpoint on $W$ and its best on all $30$ (replay $1\%$: $4$k trial
$17$ at steps $4\,000$ and $200$; replay $10\%$: $4$k trial $46$, and $2$k trial $68$ at step $2\,000$), fine-tuned at
$D=10^4$, $32$k, on $Z+4g$, $Z+5g$ and, as a probe outside both the $Z+ng$ and the $W+ng$ families, $e^+e^-\to t\bar t$;
each child at the finale cell's own two points (the same candidates, so only the parent differs), the best of the two.
Gain over the finale's child of the same cell, in dex:
\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{lrrr}
parent & $Z+4g$ & $Z+5g$ & $e^+e^-\to t\bar t$ \\ \hline
replay $1\%$, best on $W$ & $+0.09$ & $-0.04$ & $+0.06$ \\
replay $1\%$, best on all $30$ (step $200$) & $+0.04$ & $-0.09$ & $+0.15$ \\
replay $10\%$, best on $W$ & $+0.06$ & $-0.02$ & $-0.12$ \\
replay $10\%$, best on all $30$ & $+0.07$ & $-0.07$ & $-0.04$ \\ \hline
the finale (reference) & $3.68\times10^{-3}$ & $3.13\times10^{-3}$ & $2.40\times10^{-9}$ \\
scratch & $2.84\times10^{-3}$ & $4.75\times10^{-3}$ & $2.75\times10^{-8}$ \\
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
All four children sit within $0.15$\,dex of the finale's on every probe, inside the spread of one seed (two equally
forgetting naive parents gave children $2\times$ apart, above). Adding $W+4g$, $W+5g$ with a replay buffer therefore
neither helps $Z+4g$, $Z+5g$ at $D=10^4$ nor costs anything on $t\bar t$: the buffer keeps the old transfer, and the new
processes bring nothing measurable here. Pretraining on $W+4g$, $W+5g$ from the start (finale$+W$) gave $+0.11$ and
$+0.12$\,dex over scratch on the same two cells (the high-multiplicity grid), against $-0.07$ to $-0.02$ and $+0.09$ to
$+0.17$ for these children; at $D=10^4$ both are within the one-seed spread, so this cell cannot separate adding $W$ late
from having it from the start; the high-multiplicity grid's low-$D$ cells, where finale$+W$ gained $2$--$3\times$ over
the finale, can. One seed.

\emph{The same children at low $D$ (complete, \code{replaylowd} $130/130$, collected from CC and lxplus 2026-10-13
00:50; D39; \code{analysis/transfer/replay\_children.py}, Fig.~\ref{fig:replay_children}).} $D=10^1$--$10^3$ on the $8$k
grid, the same four parents and probes; on $e^+e^-\to t\bar t$ the finale was rerun at the children's two points (its $8$k
cells were searches). Gain over the finale's child, in dex ($D=10^4$ from above):
\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{l rrrrrr rrrrrr}
 & \multicolumn{6}{c}{$Z+4g$, $\log_{10}D=$} & \multicolumn{6}{c}{$Z+5g$, $\log_{10}D=$} \\
 & $1$ & $1.5$ & $2$ & $2.5$ & $3$ & $4$ & $1$ & $1.5$ & $2$ & $2.5$ & $3$ & $4$ \\ \hline
replay $1\%$, best on $W$     & $+0.05$ & $+0.21$ & $+0.27$ & $+0.27$ & $+0.20$ & $+0.09$ & $+0.33$ & $+0.39$ & $+0.31$ & $+0.23$ & $+0.20$ & $-0.04$ \\
replay $1\%$, best on all $30$ & $-0.01$ & $+0.06$ & $+0.01$ & $+0.03$ & $+0.02$ & $+0.04$ & $+0.05$ & $+0.05$ & $+0.07$ & $-0.05$ & $-0.02$ & $-0.09$ \\
replay $10\%$, best on $W$    & $+0.02$ & $+0.19$ & $+0.21$ & $+0.26$ & $+0.20$ & $+0.06$ & $+0.25$ & $+0.34$ & $+0.32$ & $+0.25$ & $+0.22$ & $-0.02$ \\
replay $10\%$, best on all $30$ & $-0.02$ & $+0.05$ & $+0.17$ & $+0.14$ & $+0.11$ & $+0.07$ & $+0.01$ & $+0.28$ & $+0.20$ & $+0.09$ & $+0.11$ & $-0.07$ \\ \hline
finale$+W$, pretrained        & $+0.18$ & $+0.31$ & $+0.48$ & $+0.52$ & $+0.49$ & $+0.22$ & $+0.54$ & $+0.27$ & $+0.30$ & $+0.42$ & $+0.33$ & $-0.06$ \\
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Adding $W+4g$, $W+5g$ late, with a replay buffer, keeps a large part of what having them in the pretraining gives: the
two best-on-$W$ parents gain $0.2$--$0.4$\,dex ($1.6$--$2.5\times$) over the finale at every $D$ from $10^{1.5}$ to
$10^3$ on both probes, against $0.3$--$0.5$\,dex for finale$+W$ on $Z+4g$ and about the same as finale$+W$ on $Z+5g$; the
gain fades to zero at $10^4$ (finale$+W$'s to $+0.22$ on $Z+4g$ and $-0.06$ on $Z+5g$). The buffer size barely matters for
the best-on-$W$ parents ($1\%$ and $10\%$ within $0.08$\,dex of each other). The best-on-all-$30$ parents gain little or nothing: replay $1\%$'s is the step-$200$
checkpoint, the finale with $200$ steps of $W$ ($\le0.07$\,dex), replay $10\%$'s ($2$k, step $2\,000$) half of the
best-on-$W$ gain. So the more a parent has learned $W$, the more its child gains on $Z+ng$. On $e^+e^-\to t\bar t$, outside
both families, the children move both ways without a pattern: from $-0.54$ to $+0.60$\,dex over the cells
($D=10^2$: $-0.16$ to $-0.45$; $10^3$: $+0.14$ to $+0.60$), the four parents' medians over the six values of $D$ are $-0.01$, $+0.08$,
$+0.10$ and $-0.22$ (replay $10\%$, best on $W$, below the finale at four of six $D$). At a one-seed spread of about
$\pm0.5$\,dex on these cells, at most a small cost on the unrelated probe, for the parent that learned $W$ longest
with the larger buffer; seeds would settle it. One seed per point.

\emph{Reading.} Adding processes late is not as good as having them in the pretraining from the start: on $Z+4g$ the
best late parent trails finale$+W$ by $0.1$--$0.3$\,dex at every $D$, on $Z+5g$ it trails by $0.1$--$0.2$\,dex at $D=10^1$, $10^{2.5}$ and
$10^3$, is level at $10^2$ and $10^4$ and above at $10^{1.5}$ ($+0.12$), and a forgetting of $3$--$30\times$ on the old processes remains (above). It still keeps
most of the gain at a small fraction of the cost: the add-$W$ step is $4$k steps against the $64$k of a pretraining. The
balance depends on the model's size. For a pretraining as cheap as ours, pretraining again with the new processes is
affordable and better; for a very large pretrained model, retraining it from scratch only to add a few processes may
cost far more than the extra gain is worth on the probes that would profit, and continued training with a small
replay buffer is the better trade.

\paragraph{Weight interpolation between the finale and the add-$W$ run (2026-10-22, D31 item 1).} The best checkpoint on
$W$ of the naive add-$W$ run at $1$k, $2$k and $4$k steps, mixed with the finale as $\theta(\alpha)=(1-\alpha)\,
\theta_{\rm finale}+\alpha\,\theta_W$ (the parameters only), $\alpha=0,0.1,\ldots,1$, scored on the add-$W$ run's own
data as the table above (\code{tools/run\_data\_tools.py interp}, \code{analysis/transfer/interp\_tradeoff.py},
Fig.~\ref{fig:interp_tradeoff}). The ends reproduce: $\alpha=1$ gives each run's recorded $W$ ($2.94$, $1.83$,
$1.20\times10^{-4}$) and forgetting ($2.3$, $3.0$, $4.4\times10^4$), $\alpha=0$ the finale ($0.97$). In between there is
no good point: at $\alpha=0.1$, $W$ is $0.17$ ($1.7\times$ below the finale's $0.28$) while the forgetting is already
$268$ to $563$; it rises steadily to the end. Every interpolated point is worse on both axes than the naive run's own
early validations (which reach $W=0.06$ at a forgetting of $226$), and far from the $10\%$ replay buffer ($W=3$ to
$5\times10^{-4}$ at a forgetting of $3.5$ to $5.2$). So the add-$W$ solution is not linearly connected to the finale at low
loss on both; of the methods tried, replay is the only one that keeps the old processes.

\begin{figure}[H]

$0$, bottom right, to $1$, top left, in steps of $0.1$): $W$ against the forgetting of the finale's $28$ processes,
with the Pareto fronts of the naive run's and the $10\%$ replay buffer's own validation points.}
\label{fig:interp_tradeoff}
\end{figure}

\paragraph{The conditionings and per-process accumulation, judged on fine-tuning, at $D=10^2$ and $10^3$.} The design
as approved (2026-10-06) was the twelve probes at $D=10^2$, $10^3$ ($8$k, $5$-trial search) and $10^4$ ($32$k, two chosen
points); the $10^4$ cells come in the next paragraph, so what follows is $2$ of the grid's $7$ values of $D$, and the
other four ($10$, $10^{1.5}$, $10^{2.5}$, $10^{3.5}$) were never part of the arms' design. Each arm is rung $9$'s
pretraining with one change, at hp73 and $64$k steps (the user's calls (B) and (C)), and each search shares its DyHPO
seed, hence its candidate pool and start-up points, with rung $9$'s on the same cell
(\code{analysis/transfer/levers\_accum.py}, Fig.~\ref{fig:levers_accum}; per cell $\log_{10}(L_\text{rung 9}/L_\text{arm})$,
MSE of $\log|\mathcal M|^2$ at the best checkpoint). Tests over the twelve probes (each probe's mean over its two $D$),
cells in brackets. Pretraining \code{val\_loss} against rung $9$'s $1.33\times10^{-6}$, then the fine-tune: Feynman
diagrams on, $2.48\times10^{-6}$, level (median $-0.09$\,dex, better on $5$ probes, $p=0.27$; cells $-0.08$); coupling
scalars off, $1.93\times10^{-6}$, level ($-0.09$, $4$ probes, $p=0.34$); off-shellness off, $1.68\times10^{-6}$, worse
($-0.29$, $3$ probes, $p=0.016$; cells $-0.23$, $4$ of $24$); generation feature off, $5.04\times10^{-6}$, the worst
pretraining, yet \emph{better} ($+0.21$, $10$ probes, $p=0.009$; cells $+0.18$, $17$ of $24$); per-process accumulation,
$9.20\times10^{-7}$, the best pretraining, yet worse ($-0.16$, $1$ probe, $p=0.001$; cells $-0.19$, $6$ of $24$). Five
arms tested: with a Bonferroni factor $5$, accumulation and the generation feature stay below $0.05$, off-shellness does
not. In these two arms the pretraining ranking is reversed on fine-tuning. One seed per arm (rung $5$ twice at hp73
differed by $8\%$ in pretraining loss). The accumulation pretraining took $14.6$\,h of wall time against $\sim6.7$\,h for
rung $9$ ($19$ micro-batches of $862$ events per update). Taken per $D$ (Wilcoxon over the twelve probes, Fig.~\ref{fig:levers_accum} now with the $D=10^4$ cells of the
next paragraph), the generation feature's advantage is one value of $D$, not ``small $D$'': $+0.09$\,dex at $10^2$ ($7$ of
$12$, $p=0.23$), $+0.26$ at $10^3$ ($10$ of $12$, $p=0.02$), $+0.04$ at $10^4$ ($7$ of $12$, $p=0.73$); the pooled
$+0.21$ above (better on $10$ of $12$ probes) pools $10^2$ and $10^3$ per probe before the median over probes, so it is
neither of the per-$D$ medians. Off-shellness off is worse at each $D$ ($-0.24$, $-0.23$, $-0.34$; nominal $p=0.03$, $0.02$, $0.02$,
none below $0.05$ after the factor $5$ for the arms, before any factor for the three $D$); Feynman diagrams on ($-0.07$,
$-0.10$, $-0.24$) and coupling scalars off ($-0.11$, $+0.06$, $-0.14$) reach no nominal $p<0.05$ at any $D$. The
$10^2$, $10^3$ cells are $8$k fine-tunes and the $10^4$ cells $32$k.

\paragraph{The conditionings at $D=10^4$, and the finale at $32$k (2026-10-08 19:00).} A $32$k cell is the best
of rung $9$'s two chosen points (\code{horizon32k\_chosen.json}), the arm run at the same two, so it is measured at
different compute from the $8$k cells above, and the two are not pooled. Per probe $\log_{10}(L_\text{rung 9}/L_\text{arm})$,
all twelve probes in for the four lever arms (Wilcoxon over probes, Bonferroni $\times5$ as above): off-shellness off,
median $-0.34$\,dex, better on $1$ of $12$, $p=0.021$ ($\times5$: $0.11$), the same sign as at $10^2$, $10^3$ ($-0.29$);
Feynman diagrams on (the old encoder), $-0.24$, $3$ of $12$, $p=0.077$ (worst one-loop $ee\to b\bar b$ $-1.04$);
coupling scalars off, $-0.14$, $3$ of $12$, $p=0.47$ ($ee\to W^+W^-$ alone $+2.29$); generation feature off, $+0.04$,
$7$ of $12$, $p=0.73$: its advantage at $10^2$, $10^3$ ($+0.21$, $10$ of $12$) is gone at $10^4$. One seed per arm.
The accumulation arm redone as in arXiv:2606.23791 (\code{tp3\_accrr\_r9}, pretraining \code{val\_loss}
$5.58\times10^{-6}$, $4.2\times$ rung $9$'s and $6\times$ the first accumulation arm's $9.2\times10^{-7}$) is complete
(collected 2026-10-10 11:00; Fig.~\ref{fig:accrr}, \code{analysis/transfer/levdiagl.py accum}). Against rung $9$, median over
probes and Wilcoxon over probes: at $D=10^2$ ($8$k) $-0.23$\,dex, better on $5$ of $12$, $p=0.11$, from $u\bar u\to gg$ $-1.61$
and $ud\to ud$ $-1.21$ to $ee\to W^+W^-$ $+0.31$; at $D=10^3$ $-0.18$, $4$ of $12$, $p=0.18$; at $D=10^4$ ($32$k) $-0.14$,
$5$ of $12$, $p=0.20$, from one-loop $ee\to b\bar b$ $+0.43$ to $ee\to t\bar t$ $-1.11$. Pooled over $10^2$ and $10^3$ per probe as
in Fig.~\ref{fig:levers_accum}: $-0.21$, better on $4$ of $12$, $p=0.052$ ($\times5$: $0.26$). The first accumulation arm on
the same cells: $-0.17$ ($2$ of $12$, $p=0.034$) at $10^2$, $-0.22$ ($4$ of $12$, $p=0.034$) at $10^3$; head to head the redone arm is
$-0.19$\,dex behind it at $10^2$ ($6$ of $12$, $p=0.30$) and level at $10^3$ ($+0.02$, $6$ of $12$, $p=0.62$). Accumulation as
arXiv:2606.23791 does it fine-tunes no better than rung $9$ at any $D$, and no better than our first version; its pretraining
ends $4\times$ higher. One seed per arm. The diagram arm with their encoder (\code{tp3\_levdiagl}, width $64$) finished its $64$k-step pretraining at
\code{val\_loss} $2.04\times10^{-6}$ (best step $63\,360$), $1.5\times$ rung $9$'s; its $36$ fine-tune sweeps are paired with rung $9$'s
cells as the accumulation arm's are (Fig.~\ref{fig:levdiagl}, \code{analysis/transfer/levdiagl.py}; complete, collected
2026-10-10 06:30). All $12$ cells at $D=10^4$ ($32$k) in: median over probes $-0.10$\,dex against rung $9$, better on $4$ of $12$,
$p=0.15$, from $ee\to b\bar b$ one-loop $-0.51$ and $u\bar u\to Zgg$ $-0.53$ to $+0.18$ (one-loop $ee\to d\bar d$,
$u\bar u\to Zg$); our encoder's arm there: $-0.24$, better on $3$, $p=0.08$. At $D=10^3$ ($8$k): median $+0.07$,
better on $7$ of $12$, $p=0.52$ (our encoder $-0.10$, $4$ of $12$, $p=0.34$); at $D=10^2$: $+0.06$, better on $6$ of $12$,
$p=0.52$ (ours $-0.07$, $5$ of $12$, $p=0.38$); Wilcoxon over probes throughout. The encoder of arXiv:2606.23791 sits
$0.1$--$0.15$\,dex above ours at every $D$ in the medians against rung $9$ (head to head per probe: better than ours on $8$,
$9$, $8$ of $12$, median $+0.34$, $+0.21$, $+0.10$\,dex at $D=10^2$, $10^3$, $10^4$) and level with rung $9$ at $D\le10^3$, below it at $10^4$; neither diagram arm
improves on rung $9$ at any $D$.

\begin{figure}[H]
\centering
\includegraphics{../analysis/transfer/figs/levers_accum_legend.pdf}\\
\widerow{\pnl{../analysis/transfer/figs/levers_accum_a.pdf}\hfill\pnl{../analysis/transfer/figs/levers_accum_b.pdf}} \\[1ex]
