\paragraph{Real zero-shot.} Every other run standardizes $y=\log|\mathcal M|^2$ per dataset with $(\mu_p,\sigma_p)$
fitted on $p$'s own data, so it is never trained to predict a process's normalization and its prediction on a process
without data is defined only up to an affine map (\code{val\_loss\_affine}). \code{tp3\_gstd\_r9} is rung $9$ at hp73 and $64$k steps with one shared standardization
(\code{data.shared\_standardization}: $(\mu,\sigma)=(-2.89,4.40)$ pooled over the $19$ processes, each keeping its own
log or signed-log), so its output is a prediction of $\log|\mathcal M|^2$ itself, $\mu+\sigma h$, for a process it has
no data of (\code{tools/zero\_shot\_ft.py --parent}, \code{analysis/transfer/zero\_shot\_real.py},
\code{zero\_shot\_gstd.json}; a ladder fine-tune's config per probe supplies the probe's validation split, rung $9$'s
where its run dir is on lxplus, rung $4$'s otherwise; the off-shellness inputs on the scored pretraining's own scale, merged as a fine-tune merges
them: a first scoring pinned the borrowed cell's scale, which moved $ee\to\nu_e\bar\nu_e$ and $ud\to ud$ only). In units of the probe's variance (MSE of $\log|\mathcal M|^2$ over
$\sigma_p^2$; $1$ is as good as the probe's exact mean, which the model is not given): $ee\to d\bar d$ $0.06$,
$ee\to t\bar t$ $0.14$, the one-loop $ee\to d\bar d$ $0.46$ (the first and third better than scratch on $10$ events);
$ee\to Z\gamma$, $u\bar u\to Zggg$, $ee\to\nu_e\bar\nu_e$, $ud\to ud$ $1.2$--$1.5$, $ee\to W^+W^-$ $1.8$;
far off on the one-loop $ee\to b\bar b$ ($16$), $u\bar u\to Zgg$ ($13$), $u\bar u\to Zg$ ($50$) and $u\bar u\to gg$
($406$). Event by event (Fig.~\ref{fig:zero_shot_2d}, \code{zero\_shot\_dist.py}) the failures are of three kinds:
an offset with the right dependence on the kinematics ($u\bar u\to Zgg$, $\sim9$ units low, parallel to the diagonal;
$u\bar u\to Zggg$ and the one-loop $ee\to b\bar b$, $2$--$4$ low); a prediction nearly constant over the event
($ee\to W^+W^-$ at $\sim-4.5$ while the truth spans $-7$ to $5$, most of $ee\to\nu_e\bar\nu_e$); and a wrong dependence
($ud\to ud$, $ee\to Z\gamma$ capped at $\sim-1.5$, $u\bar u\to gg$ spread over $-21$ to $-4$ against a true range of
$0$--$3$, $u\bar u\to Zg$). Scratch on $10^2$ events beats the zero-shot prediction on every probe. No affine map is
applied here: it would remove exactly the scale and offset this parent is trained to predict (the affine score is for
the per-process standardized parents, Fig.~\ref{fig:zero_shot_gain}). In distribution, the shared standardization costs
accuracy: MSE of $\log|\mathcal M|^2$ at the best checkpoint, geometric mean over the $19$ processes, $9.4\times10^{-5}$
against rung $9$'s $7.6\times10^{-6}$ ($12\times$; $2.3$--$420\times$ by process, worst on $u\bar u\to t\bar t$), the
shared run sitting near $5\times10^{-5}$ on almost every process (one seed; hp73 was tuned for per-process units).

\paragraph{Jonas's causal mask on our rung 9 (2026-10-15, D55).}\label{sec:jcaus} Two pretrainings at rung 9's point
(hp73, $64$k steps, CC V100, $6.5$\,h each): \code{tp3\_jcaus\_r9}, the draft's causal construction
(\code{model.net.causal\_mask}: a causal attention mask over particles ordered by descending $p\cdot P$ within identical
outgoing particles, the output the mean over particles of $\log A_i(p_1..p_i)$, the target standardized with a mean
affine in the multiplicity fitted over the $19$ processes, coupling powers dropped from the inputs), and
\code{tp3\_jcausbnn\_r9}, the same with the draft's Bayesian last layer (Heidelberg's \code{VBLinear} as a $\mu$P readout,
evaluated at its posterior-mean weights); against \code{tp3\_gstd\_r9} (one shared standardization, no mask). Real
zero-shot, no data of the probe (\code{tools/zero\_shot\_parent.py}, \code{analysis/transfer/zero\_shot\_jcaus.py},
Fig.~\ref{fig:zero_shot_jcaus}): on $u\bar d\to W^+4g$, one gluon past rung 9, the validation MSE of $\log|\mathcal M|^2$
over the probe's variance is $0.084$ (causal), $0.10$ (with BNN) and $1.95$ (shared std.); on $u\bar u\to Z+ng$, a family
rung 9 never saw, $0.18$ to $0.86$ (causal), $0.31$ to $0.47$ (BNN), $0.52$ to $50$ (shared std.). Most of the gain is the
placement of the scale: after the best affine map fitted on the probe the three are close ($W{+}4g$: $0.0062$, $0.0070$,
$0.013$; $Z{+}ng$ for $n\ge3$ the shared-standardization parent is slightly better, $0.067$ to $0.095$ against $0.080$ to
$0.089$). The BNN layer helps only at $Z{+}g$ ($0.36$ against $0.86$) and is worse at every larger $n$, unlike the draft,
where it helps the extrapolation. Four things change at once between the causal and shared-standardization parents (the
mask, the ordering, the affine-in-$N$ statistics, the dropped coupling powers). The control \code{tp3\_jaff\_r9} (D65:
the causal parent's config without the three \code{causal\_*} keys, so no mask and no prefix frames, the rest identical,
same HPs and seed) separates the mask: its raw zero-shot is $0.165$ on $W{+}4g$ and $1.02$, $0.36$, $0.19$, $0.43$, $0.63$
on $Z{+}ng$, $n=1..5$, against the causal $0.084$ and $0.86$, $0.25$, $0.18$, $0.18$, $0.21$. Most of the gain over the
shared standardization is therefore the other changes (the affine-in-$N$ statistics above all); in one seed per arm the mask adds a factor
$\approx2$ on $W{+}4g$ and $2$ to $3$ at $n=4,5$, the multiplicities furthest past training, and little at $n\le3$. After
the affine map the control has the best shape on $Z{+}ng$ ($0.040$ to $0.10$, against $0.080$ to $0.41$ causal) and is
between the two on $W{+}4g$ ($0.011$): the mask helps place the level, not the shape. One seed per arm. Not Jonas's implementation (his code is not public, the
draft is the spec): the local frames are built from the prefix with the first $4$ particles seeing each other, our extra
scalar inputs stay without the coupling powers, and the training set is rung 9 ($10^5$ events per process), not $Z+ng$ with $10^7$ per $n$. One
seed per arm; the hp73 trial has EMA off.

\begin{figure}[H]
\centering
\includegraphics{../analysis/transfer/figs/zero_shot_jcaus.pdf}
\caption{Real zero-shot of four rung-9 pretrainings at the same HPs: the causal mask, the causal mask with the Bayesian
last layer, the causal parent without the mask (control), and the shared standardization without mask. Circles: $u\bar u\to Z+ng$ (no $Z+ng$ in training); open
diamonds: $u\bar d\to W^+4g$ (training stops at $W^+3g$). Left: validation MSE of $\log|\mathcal M|^2$ over the probe's
variance. Right: the same after the best affine map fitted on the probe.}
\label{fig:zero_shot_jcaus}
\end{figure}

\paragraph{Level and spread of the zero-shot prediction (2026-10-16).} The raw zero-shot MSE splits into the squared mean
offset and the rest, $\mathrm{MSE}=\mathrm{bias}^2+\mathrm{MSE}_{\rm after\ offset}$, and the rest is mostly the spread:
the prediction's standard deviation over the truth's (Fig.~\ref{fig:zero_shot_parts}, \code{zero\_shot\_parts.py}, the
same \code{ZERO\_SHOT} rows as Fig.~\ref{fig:zero_shot_jcaus}). For the three parents with the affine-in-$N$ statistics
the level is placed well past $n=2$: $\mathrm{bias}^2/\mathrm{Var}\le0.10$ on $Z{+}ng$, $n\ge3$, and $\le0.05$ on
$W{+}4g$; the exception is $Z{+}g$, at $0.45$ (causal) and $0.73$ (control). What remains is a compressed
prediction: its spread is $0.26$ to $0.66$ of the truth's at $n\ge3$ and $0.61$ to $0.80$ on $W{+}4g$, falling with $n$
(control $0.60$, $0.38$, $0.26$ at $n=3,4,5$), while the correlation stays $0.92$ to $0.997$. So past $n=2$ the affine map
mostly restores the dynamic range, the growth of $\log|\mathcal M|^2$'s spread with each extra gluon ($\mathrm{Var}=1.5$,
$6.8$, $11.6$, $16.3$, $19.5$ for $n=1..5$), not the overall level. The shared-standardization parent misses the level
by $\mathrm{bias}^2/\mathrm{Var}=0.27$ to $47$. The comparisons are raw: the draft's extrapolation is scored on the
standardized $\log|\mathcal M|^2$ with the mean linear in $n$ fitted on the same family, and its $R^2$ with no map
(an offset alone makes it negative), so the left panel of Fig.~\ref{fig:zero_shot_jcaus}, not the affine one, is what
compares with them.

\begin{figure}[H]
\centering
\includegraphics{../analysis/transfer/figs/zero_shot_parts.pdf}
\caption{The real zero-shot error of Fig.~\ref{fig:zero_shot_jcaus}'s four parents split into level and spread. Left: the
squared mean offset over the probe's variance. Right: the standard deviation of the prediction over that of the truth
(dashed: equal). Circles: $u\bar u\to Z+ng$; open diamonds: $u\bar d\to W^+4g$.}
\label{fig:zero_shot_parts}
\end{figure}

\paragraph{Against the zero-shot results of the collaboration draft (2026-10-15).} Two sections of the draft
(\code{\textasciitilde/drafts/fm\_amplitudes/paper.tex}) report zero-shot numbers, each on a different scope from ours.
``Extrapolating to more legs'' (Jonas) trains on $q\bar q\to Z+ng$, $n\le4$ ($10$M events per $n$ from a published
dataset) and predicts $n=5$ at an MSE of $\sim7\times10^{-4}$ on the standardized $\log|\mathcal M|^2$: one family,
one more gluon, a standardization mean fitted linear in $n$, and an autoregressive (causal) attention mask over the
particles in virtuality order. The draft has no run without the mask, so whether the mask carries the extrapolation is
not shown. ``Conditioning on Feynman diagrams'' (Suprio, the draft's sec:cond) keeps the setup and the held-out split
of arXiv:2606.23791: on its held-out $gg\to u\bar dW^-$ the encodings reach $R^2=0.35$ to $0.91$ ($0.80$ to $0.91$ with
diagram information, in-out $0.35$), on $gg\to t\bar tH$ at most $0.53$. Held out there means absent from that
split's training processes, of which $96$ of the $149$ held-out ones are exact relabels (Sec.~\ref{sec:catalog}).
Ours is never in training, with the probe's whole relabelling class held out; $1-R^2$ is about the variance-normalized
MSE above, so $R^2\approx0.94$ on $ee\to d\bar d$, $0.86$ on $ee\to t\bar t$, $0.54$ on the one-loop $ee\to d\bar d$,
and negative on the other nine probes. On our good probes our zero-shot is at the top of Suprio's held-out range; the
others have no counterpart there, and Jonas's extrapolation is one more gluon within a family seen at every lower $n$. No two
of the three share a test set.

\subsection{Hand-off: open items}\label{sec:ladder-open}
\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{The new catalog, catalog\_v3 (D98; the user, 2026-10-23: ``design a new similarly sized catalog with true
    diversity, no repetitions or relabelings, being very intentional on what is left out as heldout'').} Design as approved:
    the unit is an equality class (par.\ catalog\_v3, the census: $1103$ SM tree classes plus $12$ BSM); one sampler (the
    mixture), one cut set, $\alpha_s$ stored per event at generation, every pool the same size; layers: every tree class,
    one-loop QCD virtuals, the certified loop-induced, $\alpha_s$ far points, a heavy-quark layer with the top's quantum
    numbers at $m_Q\in\{50,300,600\}$\,GeV, and the $Z'$ and 2HDM processes. Held-out axes in the approved order, each
    probe with its whole class: H1 new combinations, H2 multiplicity ($2\to4$ of families with no $2\to4$ in training,
    and $2\to5$), H3 particle content (the physical top, bracketed by the heavy-quark layer; one particle never seen, in a
    removal twin), H4 a vertex class (the quartic gauge family, in the same twin), H5 order, H6 parameter interpolation, H7
    $100$\,TeV and $p_T\geq30$\,GeV. The regimes are D93's (H2, H7, H3, H5); H1, H4 and H6 are proposed on top.
    \emph{Storage precision} (D101, \code{analysis/catalog\_v3/pool\_precision.py}, run FA-ccin2p3-cb09b906): over
    the $2667$ existing val pools ($2.6\times10^7$ events) no $|\mathcal M|^2$ lies outside float32's normal range
    ($\log_{10}|\mathcal M|^2\in[-25.7, 9.6]$, twelve decades above the floor), $53$ pools hold negative values, every PDG id
    survives the cast, and casting $|\mathcal M|^2$ moves $\log|\mathcal M|^2$ by at most $6\times10^{-8}$. Casting the
    momenta (200 pools at every multiplicity) moves the load-time factors of the target: $\log\hat s$ by at most
    $1.2\times10^{-3}$ ($99.9\%$ quantile $3\times10^{-4}$), the emission scales by at most $0.022$ (quantile
    $3\times10^{-3}$), the smallest $\log s_{ij}$ by at most $0.011$, and a massless leg's $m^2$ by up to $81$\,GeV$^2$, the
    worst all at $13$ and $100$\,TeV. Below the catalog losses ($\gtrsim10^{-2}$) but not below single-process ones
    ($10^{-11}$ at best); the network's float32 cache already rounds its inputs the same way. Pools stay float64 ($52$\,GB).
    \emph{The selection} (\code{recipes/select\_catalog\_v3.py}, seed $0$; \code{catalog\_v3\_roles.json}) on the extended
    census (D100: $+Z$, $+H$ on every $2\to3$ class, quark and lepton pairs on every $2\to2$): $1586$ distinct tree classes
    ($65$, $321$, $1200$ at $2\to2$, $2\to3$, $2\to4$). $1127$ train; H3 $237$ (every class with an external top); H2
    $168$ ($54$ of $361$ families, a class held out when any of its members' parents is); H1 $54$ (no H2 parent among
    them). One-loop layer $150$: every coloured $2\to2$ class and $2\to3$ ones drawn per beam (a $2\to3$ MadLoop pool
    costs $100$--$200$ LO units); $121$ train, $29$ held out (H5). Heavy-quark layer $105$ pools: every $2\to2$ top
    class, $5$ at $2\to3$ and $23$ at $2\to4$, each at $50$, $300$, $600$ GeV. The removal twin drops the $\tau$, $\nu_\tau$
    and the quartic gauge family, in every layer: $151$ training pools ($136$ with a $\tau$ or $\nu_\tau$, H3p; $15$ on a quartic-gauge class, H4), $1246$ left (\code{recipes/make\_catalog\_v3\_recipes.py}). The held-out loop-induced class, drawn with the selection's seed among the $17$: $gg\to ZH$. Generation estimate from catalog\_v2's $6$ CPU-h for $478$
    entries: $100$ to $150$ core-hours, $3$ to $4$ h of wall on one $48$-core node, no GPU; approved (D102) and generated.
  \item \textbf{catalog\_v4: the new coverage (open).} The user's design decision of 2026-10-25 (``Okay, yes to 1 to 3,
    % allow: 1 3 because they are in the user's words, no results
    % allow: 10 5 0.4 0.99 6 because they are the approved design's cuts and window top, no results
    yes to electroweak production and the smaller fixes, no to the decayed final states'') on the coverage check (par.\
    The catalog's coverage, a check): hadronic classes at $p_T>10$\,GeV, $|\eta|<5$, $\Delta R>0.4$, no pair-mass cut,
    $e^+e^-$ at $|\cos\theta|<0.99$; $b$ and $\tau$ cut like jets and leptons; $\sqrt{\hat s}$ log uniform from

    forward component's two shares are still the check's choices.

    factors, spinor-helicity numerators with selection rules. Not built: initial states beyond the catalog's; quartic
    vertices; soft emission as an eikonal factor times a lower-multiplicity amplitude; one-loop amplitudes from scalar
    integrals (Sec.~\ref{sec:syn-loops}, plan item (5)); multiplicities well past $2\to4$, where MadGraph struggles and the

\section{Non-SM Lagrangians as training data}\label{sec:nonsm}
The user's call of 2026-10-14 (D49, ``Use non SM lagrangians (less important)''): amplitudes from Lagrangians other than
the SM's, as data. This section records the design, the pools and a first transfer test.

\paragraph{Models and parameter points.} Three UFO models from MadGraph's model database (\code{import model} downloads
them; MG5 3.7.0 on CC-IN2P3), each at one fixed point (\code{mg5\_pipeline\_final.NONSM}), each bringing a structure
no SM pretraining has:
\begin{itemize}
  \item \code{DMsimp\_s\_spin1}: a vector mediator $Y_1$ (PDG $5000001$, a $Z'$), $M=500$\,GeV, vector and axial couplings to every lepton
    ($g_V^\ell=0.3$, $g_A^\ell=0.1$), quarks at the model default $g_V^q=0.25$, the dark matter switched off ($g_V^{X_d}=0$);
    its width $18.64$\,GeV is MG5's \code{compute\_widths} at this point. A new heavy resonance inside the probes' $\sqrt s$
    window, interfering with $\gamma$ and $Z$.
  \item \code{2HDM} (general, Higgs basis): $H^\pm$ $200$\,GeV, $h_2$ $250$, $h_3$ $300$, $h_1$ the SM-like $125$\,GeV;
    the second doublet's lepton Yukawas set to zero, so $e^+e^-\to$ scalars is gauge exchange only. Spin $0$ with charge
    is a combination no SM particle has, so it tests the particle encoding on a new point.
  \item \code{SMEFTatNLO} (restriction \code{LO}): dimension $6$ at $\Lambda=1$\,TeV, every Wilson coefficient zero except
    the process's own: the four-lepton contact $c_{ll}^{1122}=0.5$ in $ee\to\mu\mu$, the top's $Z$ dipole $c_{tZ}=0.5$
    and the $(ee)(tt)$ contact $c_{te}^{1}=0.5$ in $ee\to t\bar t$; generated with \code{NP<=1}, so the target holds
    the interference and the squared dimension-6 term. New vertex structures (a four-fermion contact, a
    $\sigma^{\mu\nu}q_\nu$ vertex) whose effect grows with $s$.
\end{itemize}
Every model's SM inputs are set to the \code{sm} card's ($\alpha^{-1}=132.507$, $G_F$, $M_Z=91.188$, $M_H=125$, the
widths) and the locked $m_t=172.5$; the SMEFT model is an $\{M_W, M_Z, G_F\}$ scheme, run at the \code{sm} card's derived
$M_W=80.419$. So each model's SM part is the SM pools' amplitude, which \code{tools/nonsm\_validate.py} checks with the
non-SM couplings at zero. The values are illustrative, chosen so the new structure is visible inside $\sqrt s\le1$\,TeV
(the $Z'$, $H^\pm$ pair and $h_2h_3$ thresholds inside it, the contact terms of order the SM amplitude at $1$\,TeV), not
fits to data.

\paragraph{Encoding.} The particle table gains five rows ($Y_1$; $H^+$, $H^-$ with $T_3=\pm\tfrac12$; $h_2$, $h_3$),
with the generator's masses. They are appended after the SM rows (\code{particle\_ids.BSM\_PARTICLE\_PROPERTIES}), so
every SM particle keeps its index and its encoded row bit for bit (checked in all $32$ combinations of the encoding
flags), the massless rows' neutral mass is frozen at its SM value, and a pretraining's checkpoint loads with the longer
table (its property buffer is a prefix of the new one; \code{wrappers.py}). The $Y_1$ sits at a standardized
$\log_{10}m$ of $2.8$, above the top's $1.8$: an extrapolation in the mass input. Unchanged: the coupling-order vector
(the new couplings are not QED or QCD powers, so $ee\to\mu\mu$ through the $Z'$ carries SM $ee\to\mu\mu$'s $[0,0,0,2]$),
the coupling scalars (no \code{physics} block) and the off-shellness columns ($Z$, $t$, $H$; the $Y_1$ propagator has
none, so the model has to find the new pole from the momenta).

\paragraph{Pools.} Seven $2\to2$ processes (\code{recipes/nonsm\_all.yaml}, the ladder's conventions: $10^5$ train,
$10^4$ val and test events, mixture sampling, $\sqrt s$ from $5\%$ above threshold, else $25$\,GeV, to $1$\,TeV):
$ee\to\mu\mu$ and $ee\to u\bar u$ through the $Z'$, $ee\to Y_1\gamma$; $ee\to H^+H^-$, $ee\to h_2h_3$; $ee\to\mu\mu$ and
$ee\to t\bar t$ in the SMEFT. Built by \code{scripts/job\_nonsm\_gen.sh} (CPU, CC-IN2P3), which also writes their
diagram sidecars and runs the validation.

\paragraph{First test (design).} The rung-$9$ pretraining (\code{tp3\_ladder\_r9}, hp73) scored zero-shot
(Sec.~\ref{sec:ladder}'s $L_0^\ast$) on all seven, and fine-tuned at the ladder's two fixed points against scratch's
$8$-trial search on two probes, $ee\to\mu\mu$ through the $Z'$ (a known process, every rung pretrains on SM $ee\to\mu\mu$,
deformed by a new pole) and $ee\to H^+H^-$ (new external particles), at $D=10^3$ and $10^4$, $8$k steps
Proposed, not run: this cell set (two of seven probes, two $D$, one parent, one seed) is a subset of the ladder's grid
and waits for the user's choice of cells (CLAUDE.md rule 0b).

\section{The synthetic-amplitude prior}\label{sec:synthetic}
The transfer study's step $6$ pretrains on amplitudes that no process produced (\code{tools/synthetic\_amplitudes.py},
\code{SYN\_VERSION}$=1$; \code{recipes/transfer\_synthetic.yaml}), the prior-fitted-network idea of the hand-off
(Sec.~\ref{sec:prior-open}, item $6$) carried to matrix elements. The question it answers: is what transfers from the
ladder physics, or only ``a smooth Lorentz-invariant function of the invariants with poles at the right places''? A
second motive is cost: a prior that needs no MadGraph has no per-process generation, compile or certification step, and
can supply as many distinct processes as one asks for, where the real catalog stops at the processes MadGraph was run on.
This section states the generator exactly, the reason behind each choice (and, where no reason was ever written down,
says so), how its output differs from real amplitudes, and what it costs to generate.

\subsection{Definition}
This is \code{SYN\_VERSION}$=1$, the generator behind every synthetic run reported here (the pretraining and the
\code{tp3\_synfte} fine-tunes). Each paragraph states the rule as run, what it imitates in a real tree-level
calculation, and why it was chosen (or that no reason was recorded). Alternatives raised since (2026-10-08 and 09) are
marked \emph{Proposed, not run}; none is implemented. How close to real amplitudes the prior should be is a convention
still to settle, so they are options to try side by side, not corrections of version $1$.

\paragraph{Names and seeds.} A synthetic process is a name, \code{syn\_<k>} (or \code{syn<n>\_<k>} with $n$ final
particles forced). Everything below is drawn once from a generator seeded by
$(\text{salt},\,\code{SYN\_VERSION},\,n_\text{forced},\,k)$, so the name alone regenerates the process, and a change of
the generator bumps the version and with it every pool's recipe id.

\paragraph{Notation: momenta, invariants, channels.} The $p$ are the physical momenta: $p_a,p_b$ of the beams, $p_f$ of
the final particles, $p_a+p_b=\sum_f p_f$. As in the amplitude literature every leg is treated as outgoing: an incoming
particle of momentum $p$ is an outgoing one of momentum $q=-p$ (crossing), so $q_{a,b}=-p_{a,b}$, $q_f=p_f$ and
$\sum_{i=1}^N q_i=0$ over the $N=n+2$ legs. The pair invariants are $s_{ij}=(q_i+q_j)^2$, $N(N-1)/2$ of them
($s_{12},s_{13},s_{14},s_{23},s_{24},s_{34}$ at $N=4$; $s=s_{12}$, and for $2\to2$ the Mandelstam $t=s_{13}$, $u=s_{14}$).
An internal line of a tree diagram splits the legs into a set $S$ and the rest; its momentum is $\sum_{i\in S}q_i$ and its
virtuality
\begin{equation}
  s_S=\Big(\sum_{i\in S}q_i\Big)^2 .
  \label{eq:syn-sS}
\end{equation}
The line is \emph{s-type} when $S$ holds only final particles: $S=\{\mu^+,\mu^-\}$ in $e^+e^-\to Z\to\mu^+\mu^-$, where
$s_S=(p_{\mu^+}+p_{\mu^-})^2>0$ is the pair's invariant mass squared; only such a line can sit on a resonance. It is
\emph{t-type} when $S$ holds one beam and some finals: the $t$-channel photon of Bhabha scattering,
$S=\{e^-_\text{in},e^-_\text{out}\}$, $s_S=(p_\text{out}-p_\text{in})^2=t\le0$, spacelike, never at a pole.

\paragraph{Legs.} An initial pair and $n$ final particles,
\begin{equation}
  (a,b)\sim\{e^-e^+\!:\,0.35,\ u\bar u\!:\,0.30,\ u\bar d\!:\,0.15,\ d\bar d\!:\,0.12,\ uu\!:\,0.08\},\qquad
  n\sim\{2\!:\,0.30,\ 3\!:\,0.35,\ 4\!:\,0.35\},
\end{equation}
each final particle drawn independently from the SM list with weights $w_g=2.5$, $w_\gamma=1.2$, $w_Z=0.7$,
$w_{W^\pm}=0.6$, $w_H=0.4$, $w_{\nu_e,\nu_\mu,\bar\nu}=0.4$, $w_{e^\pm,\mu^\pm,u,d,\bar u,\bar d}=1$,
$w_{s,c,\bar s,\bar c}=0.6$, $w_{b,\bar b}=0.7$, $w_{t,\bar t}=0.5$, and the draw repeated until electric charge,
baryon number and lepton number are conserved and $\sum_{f}m_f\le600$\,GeV. The beams are massless along $z$; every mass
is the generator's (the property table \code{GLOBAL\_PROPERTY\_MATRIX}).
\par\smallskip\noindent \emph{Why.} Real SM particles at their real masses make the model's particle-property inputs the ones it sees on real
data: only the function $|\mathcal M|^2$ is fake. The initial pairs and $n\le4$ were copied from the real catalog, and the
mass cap keeps the $\sqrt s$ window $[\max(25,1.05\sum m_f),\,1000]$\,GeV (the catalog's rule) at least $370$\,GeV wide.
The weights were set by hand to favour gluons and photons and keep heavy finals rarer, never tuned. Flavour is not
conserved: $uu\to c\,t\,g$ is a legal draw. In the SM at tree level with a diagonal CKM matrix the quark generation is
conserved (no flavour-changing neutral currents; a $W$ turns $u$ into $d$, not into $s$), so such a particle list has no
real counterpart; no reason for leaving the rule out was recorded.
\par\smallskip\noindent \emph{Proposed, not run.} Quark-generation conservation in the draw. Initial states not copied from the catalog: any
pair, gluon-initiated ($gg$, $qg$) included. Multiplicities well beyond $2\to4$ (Sec.~\ref{sec:syn-cost}): the point of
the prior is that it can make what MadGraph cannot, not that it matches the existing catalog.

\paragraph{Diagrams.} $K\sim\mathcal U\{1,\dots,K_\text{max}(N)\}$ diagrams, $K_\text{max}=3,5,6$ for $N=4,5,6$ legs.
Each is a random \emph{cubic tree}: a tree-level diagram with only three-line vertices, as the gauge theories'
$f\bar fV$ and $VVV$ vertices are. Every leg starts as its own cluster; two clusters chosen at random are joined at a
new vertex, and the line leaving it is a propagator carrying the sum of their momenta; this repeats until three
clusters remain, which meet at the last vertex. For legs $1$--$5$:
\begin{center}
\begin{tikzpicture}[x=1cm,y=1cm,thick]
  \coordinate (A) at (0,0); \coordinate (B) at (2,0); \coordinate (C) at (4,0);
  \draw (-1,0.7) node[left]{$1$} -- (A); \draw (-1,-0.7) node[left]{$2$} -- (A);
  \draw (A) -- node[above]{$q_3{+}q_4{+}q_5$} (B); \draw (B) -- (2,-1) node[below]{$5$};
  \draw (B) -- node[above]{$q_3{+}q_4$} (C); \draw (C) -- (5,0.7) node[right]{$3$}; \draw (C) -- (5,-0.7) node[right]{$4$};
  \foreach \p in {A,B,C} \fill (\p) circle (2pt);
\end{tikzpicture}
\end{center}
merges $\{3,4\}$, then $\{5,\{3,4\}\}$; $\{1\}$, $\{2\}$ and $\{3,4,5\}$ meet at the left vertex. A cubic tree on $N$ legs
has $N-3$ internal lines. The particle on a line is drawn from those with the channel's charge $Q_S=\sum_{i\in S}Q_i$:
neutral $\{\gamma\!:0.3,\,g\!:0.3,\,Z\!:0.2,\,H\!:0.2\}$, $\pm1$ $\{W\!:0.5,\,e\!:0.25,\,\mu\!:0.25\}$,
$\pm\tfrac23$ $\{u\!:0.6,\,t\!:0.4\}$, $\pm\tfrac13$ $\{d\!:0.6,\,b\!:0.4\}$; a channel of any other charge rejects the tree.
\par\smallskip\noindent \emph{Why.} A real propagator always carries its channel's quantum numbers; matching the charge is the cheapest rule that
keeps that, and SM particles put the poles at real masses. The vertices are not SM vertices: nothing checks that the two
lines meeting a propagator can couple to it (syn\_00008 is $e^+e^-\to gg$ through a $t$-channel $W$; syn\_00010 exchanges
a gluon between leptons). $K_\text{max}$ is hand-set; $7$ and $8$ for $N=7$, $8$ were added on 2026-10-09 for the high-multiplicity test (names \code{syn5\_<k>}, \code{syn6\_<k>}), which leaves every earlier name's draw unchanged. Real diagram counts grow much faster: $e^+e^-\to\mu^+\mu^-$ has $2$
($\gamma$, $Z$), $e^+e^-\to u\bar ug$ $4$, $e^+e^-\to u\bar ugg$ tens, $gg\to n\,g$ $4$, $25$, $220$, $2485$ for $n=2$ to $5$.
\par\smallskip\noindent \emph{Proposed, not run.}
(a) \emph{$K$ growing with multiplicity:} the number of distinct cubic trees on $N$ labelled legs is $(2N-5)!!$
($3$, $15$, $105$, $945$ for $N=4$--$7$); $K$ could be drawn up to a fixed fraction of it, or log-uniform up to it. The
labelling cost is linear in $K$; version $1$'s \code{\_KMAX} now reaches $N=8$ ($2\to6$), with its fixed $K$ per $N$,
so a $K$ growing with $N$ would be a new version.
(b) \emph{Vertex legality:} a line's particle drawn only among those the SM couples to the two lines it joins
($f\bar fV$ with the right charges and flavours, $VVV$, $VVH$, $f\bar fH$), the tree rejected otherwise; the numerators
stay random. This keeps ``no Feynman rules'' for the function while making every diagram one that could exist.
(c) \emph{Higher-point vertices:} quartic $VVVV$, $VVHH$, $HHHH$ and effective $ggH$, $\gamma\gamma H$ vertices, built by
joining three clusters at once (one internal line fewer per quartic vertex). The dimension count below fixes the energy
power whatever the vertices are, and a quartic vertex brings $g^2$ to the coupling. A small change to the tree builder.

\paragraph{Propagators.} Each line contributes
\begin{equation}
  D_S^{\,e}\quad\text{with}\quad D_S=s_S-M_S^2+i\,M_S\Gamma_S,\qquad
  \Gamma_S=\begin{cases} r_X M_S & \text{s-type, } M_S>0,\\ 0 & \text{otherwise,}\end{cases}\qquad
  \log r_X\sim\mathcal U[\log10^{-3},\log10^{-1}]
  \label{eq:syn-prop}
\end{equation}
($r_X$ one per particle species $X$ in the process), with power $e=1$ for $M_S>10$\,GeV and $e\sim\mathcal U\{\tfrac12,1\}$
for a massless or light ($b$, $4.7$\,GeV) line; a massless line with $e=\tfrac12$ enters as $|s_S|^{1/2}$. A massless or
light line is allowed only where $s_S$ stays away from zero: an s-type channel needs two cut final legs (photons, gluons,
charged leptons and quarks under the fiducial cuts) or a total leg mass above $10$\,GeV; a t-type channel needs a cut or
massive leg on both sides; otherwise the line is redrawn as $Z/H$, $W$ or $t$ (a charge-$\tfrac13$ channel has no heavy
option and rejects the tree). The scalar (PDG $25$) gets $\log m_H\sim\mathcal U[\log20,\log700]$\,GeV in half of the
processes, $125$\,GeV otherwise (always $125$ with an external $H$).
\par\smallskip\noindent \emph{Real counterpart.} An unstable particle propagates as $i/(p^2-M^2+iM\Gamma)$, times a spin numerator for fermions
and vectors, so one s-channel resonance gives $|\mathcal M|^2$ the Breit--Wigner factor $1/[(s-M^2)^2+M^2\Gamma^2]$, the
peak of $e^+e^-\to\mu^+\mu^-$ at $\sqrt s=M_Z$. In nature $\Gamma$ is fixed by the couplings ($\Gamma_Z/M_Z=0.027$,
$\Gamma_W/M_W=0.026$, $\Gamma_t/m_t=0.008$, $\Gamma_H/m_H=3\times10^{-5}$); version $1$ draws $\Gamma/M$ in
$[10^{-3},0.1]$, spanning all but the Higgs, independently of $M$, so the model cannot memorise a width per mass. A
t-type line never reaches $M^2$, so its width changes nothing, in nature as here. The power $e$ stands in for spin. A
massless line has propagator $1/s_{ij}$, but in a gauge theory the numerator vanishes like $\sqrt{s_{ij}}$ when $i$ and
$j$ become collinear (helicity is conserved along the line), so the amplitude goes as $1/\sqrt{s_{ij}}$ and
$|\mathcal M|^2$ as $1/s_{ij}$, the collinear factorisation
\begin{equation*}
  |\mathcal M_{n+1}|^2\;\xrightarrow{\;i\parallel j\;}\;\frac{2g^2}{s_{ij}}\,P(z)\,|\mathcal M_n|^2 ,
\end{equation*}
with $P(z)$ the splitting function of the momentum fraction $z$. The random numerators do not vanish there, so power
$\tfrac12$ reproduces the $1/s_{ij}$ in $|\mathcal M|^2$; power $1$ gives the stronger $1/s_{ij}^2$, and the even mix
of the two was set by hand. A massive line keeps $e=1$: its pole is at $M^2\ne0$ and has no collinear limit. The
protection rule exists because an unprotected massless pole makes the target infinite on a set of events, which is also
why real event generation needs cuts. The random scalar mass puts resonances at masses the SM does not have, so pole
positions are learned from the propagator inputs (the off-shellness channel) rather than memorised.
\par\smallskip\noindent \emph{Proposed, not run: the collinear limit by construction.} Every massless line joining two legs $i,j$ keeps $e=1$,
and each numerator term multiplying it carries a spinor bracket $\langle ij\rangle$ or $[ij]$, with
$|\langle ij\rangle|=|[ij]|=|s_{ij}|^{1/2}$. The amplitude then goes as $1/\sqrt{s_{ij}}$ because the numerator vanishes,
as in a gauge theory, instead of through a fractional propagator power, and the bracket's phase carries the azimuthal
correlation of a real collinear splitting, which $|s_S|^{1/2}$ cannot. It needs the spinor numerators of the next
paragraph; a channel of three or more legs has a multi-particle limit, for which version $1$'s rule would stay.

\paragraph{Helicity amplitudes and numerators.} $H\sim\mathcal U\{2,3,4\}$ ``helicity'' amplitudes. For each helicity
$h$ and diagram $d$: a complex coefficient $c_{hd}=\alpha+i\beta$, $\alpha,\beta\sim\mathcal N(0,1)$; a degree
$\delta_{hd}\sim\mathcal U\{0,1,2\}$; and a polynomial of $1$ to $3$ monomials in the pair invariants,
\begin{equation}
  P_{hd}=\sum_{m=1}^{M_{hd}}a_m\prod_{j=1}^{\delta_{hd}}s_{\pi_{mj}},\qquad a_m\sim\mathcal N(0,1),\quad
  M_{hd}\sim\mathcal U\{1,2,3\},
\end{equation}
where $\pi_{mj}$ is the pair of legs drawn (uniformly, with repetition) for the $j$-th factor of monomial $m$; at $N=4$ a
degree-$2$ example is $P=a_1\,s_{13}s_{24}+a_2\,s_{12}^2$. All monomials of one numerator share the degree, which fixes
its mass dimension.
\par\smallskip\noindent \emph{Real counterpart.} Real particles carry spin states, helicity $\pm\tfrac12$ for fermions and $\pm1$ for photons
and gluons. The amplitude is computed per helicity configuration $\lambda$ of all legs; diagrams of one configuration add
as complex numbers and interfere, configurations add as squares, and an unpolarised measurement averages the beams'
spins,
\begin{equation*}
  |\mathcal M|^2_\text{unpol}=\frac14\sum_\lambda\Big|\sum_d\mathcal M_d(\lambda)\Big|^2 .
\end{equation*}
In massless $e^+e^-\to\mu^+\mu^-$ four configurations are non-zero, two giving $(1+\cos\theta)^2$ and two
$(1-\cos\theta)^2$, which sum to $1+\cos^2\theta$; after the spin sums the numerators are polynomials in the same
invariants, $|\mathcal M|^2=2e^4(t^2+u^2)/s^2$ for $e^+e^-\to\gamma^*\to\mu^+\mu^-$ and $-2e^4(u/s+s/u)$ for Compton
scattering. Version $1$ copies the \emph{shape} of the helicity sum: $H$ coherent sums over the $K$ diagrams, each with its
own random coefficients, squared and added (Eq.~\eqref{eq:syn}). The incoherent sum over $H\ge2$ avoids exact zeros of a
single $|A|^2$ (a real spin-summed $|\mathcal M|^2$ rarely vanishes) and Gaussian coefficients give interference with
random relative phases. These ``helicities'' are not tied to spins: no selection rule makes one vanish, and no angular
shape like $1+\cos^2\theta$ is built in. The degree range, monomial count and $H\le4$ are hand-set.
\par\smallskip\noindent \emph{Proposed, not run.}
(a) \emph{Spinor-helicity numerators} (built as version $6$, Sec.~\ref{sec:syn-versions}): give each leg a helicity $h_i$ and build each numerator from random monomials in
the brackets $\langle ij\rangle$, $[ij]$ of massless legs such that leg $i$ appears with little-group weight $-2h_i$
(the number of its angle brackets minus its square brackets). Each amplitude then changes only by a phase under the little-group
rescaling of the spinors, $|A|^2$ stays invariant, and real angular shapes follow: $e^+e^-\to\mu^+\mu^-$ through a photon is
$A(1^-,2^+,3^-,4^+)\propto\langle13\rangle[24]/s_{12}$, whose square is $s_{13}^2/s^2=t^2/s^2$, one of the $(1\pm\cos\theta)^2$ terms. Massive
legs need massive spinors, a larger step; they could keep invariants while massless legs get brackets.
(b) \emph{Helicity selection rules,} on top of (a) (also version $6$): only the configurations allowed in a gauge theory are summed. A
massless fermion line keeps its helicity through a gauge vertex (chirality), and tree amplitudes of gluons with all
helicities equal, or all but one, vanish. Both are cheap once (a) exists.

\paragraph{The amplitude.} With $E_d=\sum_{S\in d}e_S$ the summed propagator powers of diagram $d$,
\begin{equation}
  |\mathcal M|^2=g(\sqrt s)\;\frac1{|\Pi|}\sum_{\sigma\in\Pi}\ \sum_{h=1}^{H}\,
  \Big|\sum_{d=1}^{K}c_{hd}\,\frac{P_{hd}(s_{ij}^\sigma)\;s^{-k_{hd}/2}}{\prod_{S\in d}\big(D_S^\sigma\big)^{e_S}}\Big|^2,
  \qquad k_{hd}=2\delta_{hd}-2E_d-4+N,
  \label{eq:syn}
\end{equation}
where $\Pi$ is the set of permutations of identical legs (initial and final legs kept apart; $uu$ beams are exchanged too) and $\sigma$ relabels the momenta.
\par\smallskip\noindent \emph{Real counterpart.} A real tree-level result has the same skeleton,
$\tfrac14\sum_\lambda|\sum_d(\text{couplings})\times N_d(\lambda)/\prod D|^2$, with Feynman rules fixing the numerators
$N_d$, the propagator powers and the couplings. Only invariants enter Eq.~\eqref{eq:syn}, so it is Lorentz invariant by
construction. The permutation average does what Bose and Fermi symmetry do for a real amplitude: the target is a function
of the particle set, not of the leg order. The factor $s^{-k/2}$ has no real counterpart: Feynman rules produce
numerators of exactly the degree the mass dimension needs, while version $1$ draws the degree, and $s^{-k/2}$ restores
the dimension. The count: in natural units an amplitude with $N$ legs has mass dimension $4-N$ (it is what makes a
cross-section an area after the phase-space integral; a $2\to2$ amplitude such as $e^2\,t/s$ is a pure number), so
$|\mathcal M|^2$ has dimension $8-2N$. One term has $+2\delta$ from the numerator, $-k$ from $s^{-k/2}$ and $-2E_d$ from
the propagators; $2\delta-k-2E_d=4-N$ gives $k_{hd}$ above. Check: $N=4$, $\delta=1$, one propagator with $e=1$ gives
$k=0$, and $c\,s_{13}/D$ is dimensionless. So the overall energy scaling is that of a real process.
\par\smallskip\noindent \emph{Proposed, not run.} At high multiplicity the permutation average costs $|\Pi|$ evaluations per event ($n!$ for
$n$ identical gluons, $40320$ at $8$). Two cheaper symmetrisations: order identical legs by the invariant
$q_i\!\cdot\!(p_a+p_b)$ (their energies in the centre-of-mass frame) before one evaluation, which is exact and invariant
but leaves kinks where two energies cross; or draw the diagram set closed under the permutations, which keeps the
function smooth and costs the same as the average only when $K$ is already large.

\paragraph{Couplings.}
\begin{equation}
  g(\sqrt s)=(4\pi)^n\,\alpha^{\,n-k_s}\,\alpha_s(\sqrt s)^{k_s},\qquad
  \alpha_s(\mu)=\frac{\alpha_s(M_Z)}{1+\alpha_s(M_Z)\,b_0\ln(\mu^2/M_Z^2)},\quad b_0=\frac{33-2\cdot5}{12\pi},
  \label{eq:syn-coup}
\end{equation}
$\alpha_s(M_Z)=0.118$, $\alpha=1/132.507$. $k_s$ is the number of QCD vertices: $0$ with no coloured leg; otherwise one
per external gluon ($n_g$), plus, for each of the other $n-n_g$ final particles, one more with probability $0.3$ (that sum
of coin flips is the binomial $\mathrm{Bin}(n-n_g,0.3)$), capped at $n$: $k_s=\min\!\big(n,\;n_g+\mathrm{Bin}(n-n_g,0.3)\big)$.
\par\smallskip\noindent \emph{Real counterpart.} A $2\to n$ tree with three-line vertices has $n$ vertices, each bringing a coupling $g$ ($e$ or
$g_s$), so $|\mathcal M|^2\propto\prod_\text{vertices}4\pi\alpha_\text{v}$: $e^+e^-\to u\bar ug$ goes as
$(4\pi\alpha)^2(4\pi\alpha_s)$. The real order is fixed by the process ($e^+e^-\to u\bar ug$ $1$, $u\bar u\to gg$ $2$,
$u\bar u\to Zg$ $1$ QCD vertices); version $1$ draws it, with the $0.3$ set by hand. $\alpha_s$ runs per event at
$\mu=\sqrt s$ exactly as in the real pools, so the coupling inputs (\code{amp\_orders}) mean the same on both.
\par\smallskip\noindent \emph{Proposed, not run.} With vertex legality, $k_s$ would be counted from the diagrams' vertices instead of drawn.

\paragraph{Events.} The pools go through the real pools' own path: \code{mg5\_pipeline\_final} registers a \code{syn\_*}
name as a process with \code{kind: synthetic}, samples events with the same mixture policy, fiducial cuts and $\sqrt s$
window rule, and calls the synthetic labeller where MadGraph's matrix element would be. Each process also writes its
diagram sidecar, so \code{offshell\_per\_event} sees the true propagators. The pretraining used $19$ processes
(\code{syn\_00000}--\code{syn\_00018}: rung $9$'s process count) at $10^5$ training, $10^4$ validation and $10^4$ test
events each: $6$ are $2\to2$, $7$ are $2\to3$, $6$ are $2\to4$; $K$ from $1$ to $6$; $8$ carry a non-SM scalar mass
($21$--$381$\,GeV).

\paragraph{Checks.} \code{tests/test\_synthetic\_amplitudes.py} (CPU): $|\mathcal M|^2$ unchanged under random Lorentz
transformations (worst relative change $5\times10^{-12}$, asserted below $10^{-9}$);
$|\mathcal M|^2(\lambda P;\lambda M,\lambda\Gamma)=\lambda^{8-2N}|\mathcal M|^2(P)$ (below $10^{-10}$); the same name
gives the same structure and the same pool on every interpreter, and no two names draw the same structure; the pools
conserve momentum, keep legs on shell and $\sqrt s$ in its window, and are finite and positive; every sidecar propagator
is found among the events' invariant masses. The permutation average is not tested separately.

\subsection{How it differs from real amplitudes}
\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{No Feynman rules.} Vertices only conserve charge; nothing ties a propagator's particle to the lines it
    joins, the couplings to the vertices, or the widths to the couplings, and flavour is not conserved. This is
    deliberate at the level of the function: with Feynman rules the output would be real amplitudes, and the prior
    could no longer ask whether transfer needs physics or only ``a smooth Lorentz-invariant function with poles at the
    right places''. Vertex legality (proposed above) closes the gap for the diagrams without giving that up.
  \item \textbf{No spinor or gauge structure.} Real numerators come from traces of $\gamma$ matrices and polarisation
    sums: helicity selection rules, angular shapes such as $1+\cos^2\theta$, and gauge cancellations between diagrams
    that tame high-energy growth. Random polynomials have none of these. Spinor numerators and selection rules are
    proposed above; gauge invariance (the Ward identities) has no proposal yet and is the hardest of the three.
  \item \textbf{Soft limits.} When a real gluon or photon of momentum $k$ becomes soft (its energy $\to0$), the amplitude
    factorises into the amplitude without it times a universal \emph{eikonal} factor that depends only on the
    directions of the particles that emit it,
    \begin{equation*}
      |\mathcal M_{n+1}|^2\;\xrightarrow{\;k\to0\;}\;-g^2\sum_{i,j}\mathbf T_i\!\cdot\!\mathbf T_j\,
      \frac{p_i\!\cdot\!p_j}{(p_i\!\cdot\!k)(p_j\!\cdot\!k)}\,|\mathcal M_n|^2
    \end{equation*}
    ($\mathbf T_i$ the colour charge of leg $i$, the electric charge for a photon), divergent as $1/E_k^2$. Version $1$
    has no such factor: nothing in it knows that a final gluon was emitted from another leg. The cuts keep the real
    pools away from the strict limit, but the approach to it is in the data.
    \emph{Proposed, not run: emission.} A synthetic $2\to n{+}1$ process with an external $g$ or $\gamma$ built as
    $|\mathcal M_{n+1}|^2=S(k)\,|\mathcal M_n|^2(\tilde p)+R$: the eikonal factor $S$ (random colour-like charges
    $\mathbf T_i$, conserved in sum) times a synthetic $2\to n$ amplitude evaluated at momenta $\tilde p$ mapped to $n$
    legs by a recoil map (as subtraction schemes do), plus a regular synthetic remainder $R$ without the $1/E_k^2$.
    It is exact in the soft limit and costs one lower-multiplicity evaluation.
  \item \textbf{Tree-like and positive only.} No loop-level features: no logarithms of $s$ (the Sudakov arms'
    structure), no threshold behaviour, no imaginary parts, no sign changes (rung $9$ has a signed one-loop pool; all
    $19$ synthetic pools are positive and resolve to \code{log}). A proposal follows in Sec.~\ref{sec:syn-loops}.
  \item \textbf{Wider within a process.} The per-process standard deviation of $\log|\mathcal M|^2$ (the
    standardisation of \code{data\_stats.json}) has median $2.75$ (range $0.99$--$4.24$) over the $19$ synthetic pools,
    against $1.32$ ($0.21$--$3.38$) over rung $9$'s $19$; the per-process means span $-24.2$ to $5.0$ against $-14.4$ to
    $2.7$. The random numerators and the narrow drawn widths make the synthetic functions vary roughly twice as much in
    $\log|\mathcal M|^2$ over the same kind of phase space.
\end{itemize}
What it keeps: real particles and masses, real kinematics and cuts, Lorentz invariance, the mass dimension, poles at
propagator masses with Breit--Wigner shapes, collinear-like $1/s_{ij}$ singularities, running $\alpha_s$, Bose and Fermi
relabelling symmetry, and diagram sidecars that describe the function exactly.
Measured so far (Sec.~\ref{sec:ladder}, \code{analysis/transfer/finale\_synth*.py}, $8$k grid): fine-tuned from
it, the model beats scratch in $66$ of $84$ cells and stays within a factor $1.7$ of rung $9$ at the median (median over
probes $-0.23$\,dex, better than rung $9$ on $3$ of $12$, $p=0.034$).

\subsection{Versions 2 to 6 (2026-10-12)}\label{sec:syn-versions}
Built for the user's call of 2026-10-12 (\code{site} decisions D36, D38; version $6$ D40): add the proposals above one at a time, each
version keeping the previous one's changes, and track the generation rate, so that the prior gets closer to real
high-multiplicity amplitudes without becoming as slow as MadGraph. Each version is pretrained once at the finale's
hyperparameters (no search) and fine-tuned at the two fixed points. Version $1$ is unchanged byte for byte (every name a
recipe uses keeps its structure hash); the new versions are named \code{syn[<n>]v<V>\_<k>}, $V=2$--$5$, seeded by
$(\text{salt},V,n_\text{forced},k)$, and evaluated by a vectorised labeller that reproduces version $1$ to $10^{-13}$.
\emph{Versions $2$--$5$ as first defined (revision $1$, the next six paragraphs) drew far fewer diagrams than intended
and are superseded by revision $2$ and version $6$ at the end of this subsection; they stay reproducible under their
names.}
\paragraph{Version 2: diagram count growing with multiplicity} (proposal (a)). $K$ is drawn log-uniform on
$1,\dots,K_\text{cap}$, $K_\text{cap}=\max\big(K_\text{max}^{(1)}(N),\lfloor(2N-5)!!/(4|\Pi|)\rfloor\big)$, with $|\Pi|$ the
permutations of identical legs; the diagrams are distinct up to those permutations. The symmetrisation turns each drawn
diagram into $|\Pi|$ labelled ones, so the cap bounds the labelled trees per event by $(2N-5)!!/4$, and the cost is
linear in that number. Why a quarter: the SM cubic-tree count of $q\bar q\to V+n\,g$ is $384$ at $2\to5$ ($0.41$ of
$(2N-5)!!$) and $3840$ at $2\to6$ ($0.37$); a quarter stays below the real count and bounds the work at about $2600$
terms per helicity at $2\to6$.
\paragraph{Version 3: SM vertices} (proposal (b)), and quark generation and lepton family conserved in the leg draw.
Trees are built bottom-up so that every vertex is one of $f\bar f\gamma$, $f\bar fZ$, $q\bar qg$, $f\bar fH$ ($b$, $t$),
$W$ within a doublet, $WW\gamma$, $WWZ$, $ggg$, $WWH$, $ZZH$. Each diagram carries its own coupling,
$(4\pi\alpha_s)^{n_\text{QCD}/2}(4\pi\alpha)^{n_\text{QED}/2}$ in the amplitude with $\alpha_s$ running per event, so
diagrams of different order interfere; the order vector holds the maxima over diagrams (the pipeline's convention).
\paragraph{Version 4: collinear and soft limits by construction.} A massless line joining two massless final legs
$i,k$ keeps power $1$, $s_{ik}$ is left out of that diagram's monomials, and each numerator term is multiplied by $B_{ik}$
or its conjugate (drawn per term),
\begin{equation*}
  B_{ik}=|s_{ik}|^{1/2}\,W_{ik}\,\frac{\mathrm{tr}(ikba)}{|\mathrm{tr}(ikba)|},\qquad
  \mathrm{tr}(ikba)=\langle ik\rangle[kb]\langle ba\rangle[ai],\qquad
  W_{ik}=\prod_{x\in\{i,k\}\cap\{g,\gamma\}}\Big(\frac{(q_i+q_k)\cdot P}{q_x\cdot P}\Big)^{1/2},
\end{equation*}
$P$ the total incoming momentum. The phase is Lorentz invariant ($|\mathrm{tr}|^2=s_{ik}s_{kb}s_{ba}s_{ai}$ to $10^{-12}$).
The result: $|\mathcal M|^2\propto1/s_{ik}$ in the collinear limit and $\propto1/E_k^2$ for a soft gluon or photon, the
eikonal \emph{power} only: the coefficient is the random hard function, not the eikonal factor times a lower-point
amplitude. The factor $W$ is what makes the soft power come out (the collinear construction alone gives $1/s_{ik}$ but
not $1/E^2$). Initial-state lines and massless lines joining three or more legs keep version $1$'s rule. $|\mathcal M|^2$
is averaged with its parity image, since the phase of $B$ is parity-odd and real unpolarised amplitudes are
parity-even.
\paragraph{Version 5: the target's shape} (D36's ``target the generation''). $q\bar q'\to V+4g$ (\code{syn5v5\_*}) and
$V+5g$ (\code{syn6v5\_*}), version $4$'s rules otherwise; $V=\gamma,Z,W^+,H$ by $k\bmod4$ (so any four consecutive names
hold all four bosons), beams $u\bar u$ or $d\bar d$ at $\tfrac12$ each ($u\bar d$ for the $W$). The Higgs couples to
gluons only through an effective $ggH$ vertex (coupling $\alpha_s e/(3\pi)$, the top-loop's order), allowed on the
external $H$ only. \code{syn5v5\_00001}, \code{syn6v5\_00001} ($Z+4g$, $Z+5g$) and \code{syn5v5\_00002},
\code{syn6v5\_00002} ($W^++4g$, $W^++5g$) have exactly the probes' (and the $W$ arm's) particle content.
\paragraph{Validation} (\code{tests/test\_synthetic\_versions.py}; $20$ processes and $6000$ events per version).
$|\mathcal M|^2$ finite and positive on every event. Largest relative change under a Lorentz transformation
$2.5\times10^{-11}$, $3.2\times10^{-12}$, $9.1\times10^{-12}$, $2.9\times10^{-12}$ (v2--v5); mass dimension to
$2\times10^{-12}$; identical-leg swaps to $7\times10^{-14}$. Every vertex of $649$ diagrams ($3645$ vertices) is an SM vertex.
Limits as slopes along momentum-conserving sequences: collinear $-1.000$ to $-0.997$ (v4, $16$ pairs) and $-1.000$
(v5, $57$), soft $-2.000$ (v4, $11$ legs; v5, $114$); versions $1$--$3$ give $1/s_{ik}^2$ on most such pairs ($8$ of $22$,
$39$ of $43$, $21$ of $24$). The soft power sets in below about $10^{-4}$ of the gluon's energy when a resonant massive
line is adjacent.
\paragraph{Rates} (stored events/s, one laptop core, $2000$ events per process, \code{tools/bench\_synthetic.py --hm}):
\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{lccccc}
 & v1 & v2 & v3 & v4 & v5 \\ \hline
$2\to5$, median & $30.8$k & $10.1$k & $24.7$k & $27.2$k & $10.7$k \\
$2\to6$, median & $14.9$k & $6.0$k & $9.8$k & $19.1$k & $2.1$k \\
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The slowest process is $W^++5g$ (\code{syn6v5\_00002}, $2040$ labelled terms) at $963$ events/s, the labeller $94\%$ of
the time. Against MadGraph on the same core (one lxplus CPU job, 2026-10-12, \code{FA-lxplus-86c9f2c4},
\code{tools/bench\_synthetic.py --real ... --hm}, $2000$ stored events per process; medians, synthetic over four processes):
\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{lcccccc}
 & MadGraph & v1 & v2 & v3 & v4 & v5 \\ \hline
$2\to5$ & $88$ ($u\bar u\to Z+4g$, $u\bar d\to W+4g$: $64$--$112$) & $16.0$k & $4.9$k & $15.1$k & $14.2$k & $5.0$k \\
$2\to6$ & $4$ ($Z+5g$, $W+5g$: $3$--$5$) & $6.7$k & $3.2$k & $5.1$k & $10.8$k & $1.1$k \\
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The slowest synthetic version is still $\sim\!50\times$ faster than MadGraph at $2\to5$ and $\sim\!250\times$ at $2\to6$
(v5's slowest process, $\gamma+5g$, $616$ events/s, against $3$--$5$), before MadGraph's compile. Recipes: \code{recipes/transfer\_finale\_synhm\_v<V>.yaml},
the finale's $28$ processes plus each version's eight.

\paragraph{Revision 2: every legal diagram} (the user's correction of 2026-10-12: ``if in the end we end up with only
$\sim200$ legal diagrams, we aren't doing what we said''; and on a proposed cap of $4000$ labelled diagrams, 2026-10-13:
``I would first try to optimize as much as we can what we are doing, before deciding to compromise on other things''). Revision $1$ above drew $K$ log-uniform \emph{up to} the cap,
so a typical process had a handful of diagrams (\code{syn5v2\_00001}: $5$ of a possible $236$; \code{syn6v5\_00001},
$Z+5g$: $1$ orbit of the $3840$ legal diagrams). Revision $2$ replaces it under new names, \code{syn[<n>]v<V>r2\_<k>},
seeded by $(\text{salt},V,n_\text{forced},k,2,\text{target-shaped})$, so no revised process can meet a pool cached
under a revision-$1$ name; revision $1$ stays reproducible (its structure hashes are frozen in the test) and is
superseded, and its pretrainings were cancelled (2026-10-12, with the scope change). Version $2$ (no legality) now takes
$K=K_\text{cap}$. Versions $3$--$5$ use \emph{every} SM-legal tree of the process, listed by the subset recursion of
\code{legal\_diagram\_count} with version $3$'s vertices and light-line protection: $384$ for $q\bar q\to V+4g$ ($V=\gamma,
Z,W$), $561$ for $H+4g$, $3840$ for $V+5g$. That set is closed under the permutations of identical legs, so the
numerators are drawn once per permutation orbit and carried to the orbit's images, and $|\mathcal M|^2$ is one
coherent sum over every labelled diagram (each orbit's $|\Pi|$ images weighted by $1/|\text{stabiliser}|$) instead of
version $1$'s incoherent average over $|\Pi|$; lines a stabiliser maps onto each other share their drawn power. There
is no cap on the count: the largest recipe process, \code{syn6v3r2\_00001} ($uu\to uu\,4g$), keeps all $14610$, and
$H+5g$ (the effective $ggH$ vertex adds diagrams) all $6555$. Only above $5\times10^4$ legal trees, which no recipe
name reaches, would the orbits come from uniformly sampled trees instead of a full listing.
Diagram counts of the recipes' processes (labelled diagrams, every legal one):
\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{lll}
 & $2\to5$ (\code{syn5...\_00000}--$03$) & $2\to6$ (\code{syn6...\_00000}--$03$) \\ \hline
v2r2 ($K$, charge-table trees) & $236$, $236$, $236$, $236$ & $1299$, $108$, $2598$, $1299$ \\
v3r2 & $93$, $130$, $588$, $40$ & $1276$, $14610$, $220$, $1984$ \\
v4r2 & $126$, $64$, $112$, $312$ & $441$, $1439$, $3438$, $5200$ \\
v5r2, v6t ($\gamma,Z,W^+,H$) & $384$, $384$, $384$, $561$ & $3840$, $3840$, $3840$, $6555$ \\
v6 & $317$, $252$, $126$, $315$ & $4512$, $7305$, $624$, $1008$ \\
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
(v2r2's $K$ counts drawn diagrams up to the permutations, each evaluated in all $|\Pi|$ images as before.)

\paragraph{Version 6: spinor brackets and helicity selection rules} (D40: ``a v6 with the terms generated by $\langle
ij\rangle$, $[ij]$ terms, and the helicity selection rules''; proposals (a) and (b) of the helicity paragraph). Built on
revision $2$ of version $5$ (every legal diagram, the $ggH$ vertex), in two forms: \code{syn[<n>]v6\_<k>} on version
$4$'s general legs and \code{syn[<n>]v6t\_<k>} on version $5$'s target shape. Every leg carries a helicity in the
all-outgoing convention: $\pm\tfrac12$ for fermions, $\pm1$ for $g,\gamma$, $-1,0,+1$ for $Z,W$, $0$ for $H$. Spinors
are those of the physical momenta, with $[ij]=\langle ij\rangle^*$ and $|\langle ij\rangle|^2=2p_i\!\cdot\!p_j$; a
massive leg enters through the light-cone component of its momentum along its direction in the centre-of-mass frame,
$p^\flat=\tfrac12(E+|\vec k|)(1,\hat k)$ there, i.e.\ $p=p^\flat+\frac{m^2}{2p^\flat\cdot q}\,q$ with $q$ null against it:
its helicity is the usual one, quantised along the momentum in the CM frame, $p^\flat\to p$ as $m\to0$, and the
choice is covariant, symmetric in the beams and the same in every term (a reference that changed between terms, a beam
chosen by its label, mixes helicity bases and broke Lorentz invariance at $\mathcal O(1)$ with identical beams; the
other light-cone component, against the momentum, lands exactly on the partner of a $2\to2$ process). Each diagram
(orbit) and configuration $\lambda$ has its own numerator $c\,a\,\Phi\,s^{-k/2}$, $c$ and $a$ Gaussian as before and
$\Phi$ one monomial in the brackets (the configuration sum already gives each diagram many independent random
structures, and one monomial halves the cost against version $1$'s one to three), built to satisfy:
\begin{itemize}
  \item \emph{little-group weight}: leg $i$ appears with (angle minus square) count $-2h_i$, so
    $A_\lambda\to t_i^{-2h_i}A_\lambda$ when $\lambda_i\to t_i\lambda_i$, $\tilde\lambda_i\to\tilde\lambda_i/t_i$;
  \item \emph{soft}: a gluon or photon has as many brackets in the denominator as in the numerator, so $\Phi$ stays
    finite as it goes soft; its denominators pair with a beam ($\langle ka\rangle$, never zero inside the fiducial
    cuts, as in the eikonal $\langle ai\rangle[ik]/\langle ak\rangle$);
  \item \emph{collinear}: a massless line on two massless final legs $i,k$ keeps power $1$ and $\Phi$ has exactly one
    $\langle ik\rangle$ or $[ik]$ (a gauge leg's helicity fixes which). With the soft rule this gives $1/s_{ik}$ and
    $1/E_k^2$ without version $4$'s factor $W$;
  \item \emph{dimension}: the remaining brackets pair legs at random (no $\langle ii\rangle$, no second bracket of a
    collinear pair), padded with weight-$0$ dipoles on non-gauge legs to $n_b+2\delta$ brackets, $\delta\sim\mathcal
    U\{0,1,2\}$ as version $1$'s degree; $s^{-k/2}$ with $k=n_b-2E_d+N-4$ restores the mass dimension.
\end{itemize}
The selection rules: helicity is conserved along every massless fermion line (its ends opposite in the all-outgoing
convention), left-handed along a line through a $W$ vertex or ending on a neutrino (the SM's charged current and
neutrinos are left-handed); with every external leg massless and no scalar in the process, configurations with fewer
than two negative or two positive helicities vanish (the all-plus and one-minus tree amplitudes). Massive fermions
($b$, $t$) and massive external vectors are exempt ($q\bar q\to Zg$ has every configuration). A diagram contributes
only to the configurations its fermion lines allow, and a configuration that no diagram allows is exactly zero. Then
\begin{equation*}
  |\mathcal M|^2=\frac14\sum_{\lambda}\tfrac12\big(|A_\lambda(p)|^2+|A_\lambda(\tilde p)|^2\big),
\end{equation*}
$\tilde p$ the spatially inverted momenta (the parity image swaps every $\langle ij\rangle$ with $[ij]$), $\tfrac14$
the beams' average. For a configuration whose parity partner is allowed term by term, the partner's numerators are the
conjugates, so the pair costs one evaluation; a chiral configuration is averaged with its own image, which keeps
$|\mathcal M|^2$ parity even, as an unpolarised tree is. Configurations per process: $60$ ($\gamma+4g$), $96$ ($Z+4g$),
$48$ ($W^++4g$, chiral), $32$ ($H+4g$); at $2\to6$ $124$, $192$, $96$, $64$. The evaluation multiplies brackets
gathered from a per-event table; the work is configurations (pairs counted once) times orbit representatives times
$|\Pi|$, $0.85$ million terms per event for $Z+5g$ and $W^++5g$ (stabilisers count each image once per element), with no cap.

\paragraph{Validation of revision 2 and version 6} \code{tests/test\_synthetic\_versions.py}, $20$ processes per revised version and per v6 form ($8$ for v6t), $6000$ events
each ($5040$ for v6 and $1440$ for v6t, whose $2\to6$ processes label slowly). Revision $1$'s structure hashes of the $44$ names
in use and drawn first are frozen, and every revision-$1$ label is bit-identical to the trunk's code. $|\mathcal M|^2$
finite and positive on every event; largest relative change under a Lorentz transformation $3.3\times10^{-11}$,
$1.1\times10^{-11}$, $4.0\times10^{-12}$, $1.0\times10^{-12}$ (v2r2--v5r2), $4.9\times10^{-11}$ (v6), $2.5\times10^{-12}$
(v6t); mass dimension to $3\times10^{-12}$; identical-leg swaps to $2\times10^{-13}$. Every vertex of $20577$ diagrams
($119887$ vertices, all versions) is an SM vertex; all $88$ revision-$2$ and v6 processes keep every legal diagram,
and the labelled diagrams equal the orbit sizes summed. Version $6$: under the little-group scaling with
complex $t_i$ each of $26140$ helicity amplitudes (times events) changes by $\prod_i t_i^{-2h_i}$ to $6\times10^{-13}$; the
surviving configurations ($1307$; $3033$ vanish) equal the set recomputed in the test from the trees and the rules,
each is non-zero, $|\mathcal M|^2$ equals the configuration sum of the equation above to $3\times10^{-15}$, so the
vanishing ones contribute nothing, and it is parity even exactly. Limits along momentum-conserving sequences (the
collinear one now down to $\theta=10^{-6.5}$: with thousands of regular diagrams some events reach the asymptote only
below $10^{-5.5}$): collinear slope $-1.003$ to $-0.997$ (v4r2, $28$ pairs), $-1.008$ to $-0.982$ (v5r2, $70$), $-1.006$
to $-0.993$ (v6, $33$), $-1.008$ to $-0.992$ (v6t, $28$); soft $-2.000$ ($20$, $140$, $30$, $56$ gauge legs). v6 gets both
from the brackets alone. v2r2 and v3r2 give $1/s_{ik}^2$ on $59$ of $62$ and $37$ of $37$ pairs, as their revision $1$.

No version improves on v1 consistently (v3r2 at $Z{+}4g$, $D=10^2$ and v5r2 at $Z{+}4g$, $D=10^4$ are ahead by $0.03$ to $0.07$ dex, inside one seed's scatter), and none comes near the finale$+W$ pretraining. Giving the random processes their real
diagram counts (v2r2) makes the child $2\times$ worse at $D=10^2$, $10^3$ on both probes; restricting to Standard Model
vertices (v3r2) recovers v1's level, and the collinear and soft structure (v4r2) changes nothing measurable on top. The
target-shaped version is \emph{worse} at $D=10^2$, by $4$--$5\times$ against the finale ($Z+5g$ even $0.08$\,dex below
scratch), and level from $10^3$ on. Its eight processes include $q\bar q\to Z+4g$ and $Z+5g$ with exactly the probes'
particles and order vector but a synthetic $|\mathcal M|^2$: the pretrained model has learned a wrong function for the
very input the probe presents, and with $10^2$ events and $2\,816$ steps the fine-tune cannot unlearn it. A synthetic
process that coincides with a real one in every input the model sees is a wrong label, not a prior. One seed.
